    c          : Vec<f64>,       // n_total — cost (minimise convention)
    n_struct   : usize,
    n_slack    : usize,
    n_artificial : usize,
    var_map    : Vec<OrigVar>,   // standard index -> original VarId + sense (slack/artificial tag)
    row_signs  : Vec<i8>,        // ±1: track which rows were negated for b >= 0
  }

Standard-form conversion rules (per constraint type):

Relation Action Added columns
Le (<=) add slack s >= 0 1 slack
Ge (>=) subtract surplus s >= 0, add artificial a >= 0 1 surplus + 1 artificial
Eq (=) add artificial a >= 0 1 artificial

For Sense::Maximize: negate every entry of c before solving; negate the reported objective at postsolve.

Free variable x (lb = -inf, ub = +inf): split into x = x+ - x-, both >= 0; var_map records the split.

Bounded variable with finite lb: substitute x' = x - lb, so x' >= 0 and ub' = ub - lb.

8.6 Basis Representation and LU Layout

  Basis {
    basic     : Vec<VarId>,         // basic[i] = variable occupying row i; len = m
    status    : Vec<VarStatus>,     // status[j] for every variable j in n_total
    factor    : LuFactorization,
    n_updates : usize,              // eta vectors accumulated since last refactorization
  }

  LuFactorization {
    l_eta     : Vec<EtaVector>,     // spike columns from pivots (Forrest-Tomlin)
    u         : SparseMatrix,       // upper triangular factor (row-sparse)
    l_dense   : Option<SparseMatrix>, // lower triangular factor (col-sparse), rebuilt on refact
    row_perm  : Vec<usize>,         // P: row permutation applied before factorization
    col_perm  : Vec<usize>,         // Q: column permutation
    n_updates : usize,
    condition_est : f64,            // cheap 1-norm condition estimate; triggers refact when large
  }

FTRAN (forward solve B x = r): apply L^-1 (eta columns left-to-right), then U^-1 (back-substitution). BTRAN (backward solve B^T y = c_B): apply U^-T (forward substitution), then L^-T (eta columns right-to-left).

Refactorization trigger: n_updates >= refact_interval (default 50) OR condition_est > cond_threshold (default 1e12).

8.7 Pivot Sequence — Worked Example (3-variable LP)

Problem:

  maximise   5x1 + 4x2 + 3x3
  subject to
    6x1 + 4x2 + 2x3 <= 240   [row 0]
    3x1 + 2x2 + 5x3 <= 270   [row 1]
    5x1 + 6x2 + 5x3 <= 420   [row 2]
    x1, x2, x3 >= 0

Converted (minimise -5x1 - 4x2 - 3x3; add slacks s4, s5, s6):

Initial tableau (rows = constraints; basis = {s4,s5,s6}; obj row last):

     x1   x2   x3  | s4  s5  s6 | RHS
  [  6    4    2   |  1   0   0 | 240 ]  row0
  [  3    2    5   |  0   1   0 | 270 ]  row1
  [  5    6    5   |  0   0   1 | 420 ]  row2
  [ -5   -4   -3   |  0   0   0 |   0 ]  obj

Iteration 1 — Dantzig entering: x1 (most negative rc = -5, col 0). Ratio test: 240/6=40, 270/3=90, 420/5=84 → min=40, row0 leaves (s4 leaves). Pivot on (row0, col0); x1 enters basis.

Post-pivot tableau (abbreviated):

     x1   x2    x3  | s4     s5  s6  | RHS
  [  1   2/3  1/3  | 1/6     0   0  |  40 ]  row0 (x1 basic)
  [  0   0    4   | -1/2    1   0  | 150 ]  row1
  [  0  10/3  10/3 | -5/6    0   1  | 220 ]  row2
  [  0  -2/3  -4/3 |  5/6    0   0  | 200 ]  obj

Iteration 2 — entering: x3 (rc = -4/3). Ratio test: 40/(1/3)=120, 150/4=37.5, 220/(10/3)=66 → row1 leaves (s5). Objective after pivot: 200 + (4/3)*(37.5) = 250.

Iteration 3 — entering: x2 (rc remains negative). After pivot → all reduced costs >= 0; optimal.

Final: x1=30, x2=0, x3=37.5, objective=300. KKT check: primal feasible + dual feasible + complementary slackness verified within eps_opt=1e-8.

8.8 Malformed / Failure-Path Example

Input: constraints specify A x >= b with b > 0, but after Phase I the sum-of-artificials optimum is 3.2e-5 > eps_feas (1e-8).

Outcome path:

  1. Phase I terminates with objective 3.2e-5.
  2. Solver checks: min-artificial-objective > eps_feas → primal infeasible.
  3. Extract dual ray d from Phase I dual values: d satisfies A^T d <= 0, b^T d > 0 (Farkas certificate).
  4. Status::Infeasible, Certificate::DualRay(d) emitted.
  5. Postsolve maps d back through presolve undo-stack (re-scaling, restored fixed vars).
  6. CLI prints: INFEASIBLE — dual ray [d0, d1, ...], verifiable by lp-cli check --certificate.

Edge case — Phase I optimum exactly zero but artificials still basic: occurs when degenerate rows keep an artificial at value 0. Resolution: pivot artificials out of the basis using a non-artificial column (pivot-on-zero procedure); if no such column exists the row is redundant and dropped.

8.9 Sensitivity Analysis Data Model

Post-optimal sensitivity requires the optimal basis B and dual prices y = c_B B^-1.

Range type Definition Formula
obj range for x_j basic interval [lb_j, ub_j] for c_j keeping B optimal delta s.t. all rc_k + delta*(B^-1 a_j)_k remain dual-feasible
obj range for x_j nonbasic keep rc_j >= 0 (or <= 0); simple scalar interval c_j - rc_j ± epsilon
RHS range for row i interval [lb_i, ub_i] for b_i keeping B primal-feasible theta s.t. x_B + theta * B^-1 e_i >= 0 per bound
Shadow price (row i) y_i = (c_B B^-1)_i available from BTRAN after solve
Reduced cost x_j nonbasic rc_j = c_j - y^T a_j available directly

Sensitivity struct stores all ranges and dual prices; computed in one pass using BTRAN results already available from Phase II.

8.10 Serialisation / Wire Formats

MPS format (fixed-field, 10-column):

  EBNF:
  mps-file   ::= NAME-section ROWS-section COLS-section RHS-section
                 [BOUNDS-section] [RANGES-section] ENDATA
  NAME-section ::= "NAME" ws name NL
  ROWS-section ::= "ROWS" NL (row-line)+
  row-line     ::= ws sense ws row-name NL
  sense        ::= "N" | "L" | "G" | "E"
  COLS-section ::= "COLUMNS" NL (col-entry)+
  col-entry    ::= ws col-name ws row-name ws value [ws row-name ws value] NL
  RHS-section  ::= "RHS" NL (rhs-entry)+
  BOUNDS-section ::= "BOUNDS" NL (bound-entry)+
  bound-entry  ::= ws btype ws bnd-name ws col-name [ws value] NL
  btype        ::= "UP"|"LO"|"FX"|"FR"|"MI"|"BV"|"LI"|"UI"|"PL"

LP format (free-form, CPLEX-style):

  lp-file ::= [obj-section] constraints-section [bounds-section] [generals-section] END
  obj-section ::= ("Minimize"|"Maximize") NL obj-name ":" linear-expr NL
  linear-expr ::= [sign] term (sign term)*
  term        ::= [coef] var-name
  sign        ::= "+" | "-"

Solution output (JSON, optional):

  {
    "status": "Optimal",
    "objective": 300.0,
    "variables": { "x1": 30.0, "x3": 37.5 },
    "duals": { "row0": 0.833, "row1": 0.667 },
    "certificate": { "type": "KktOptimal" }
  }

Basis warm-start format (binary, little-endian):

  [4B magic: 0x4C505342] [4B version] [4B m] [4B n_total]
  [m x 4B: basic[] VarId values]
  [n_total x 1B: VarStatus enum byte]

8.11 Tolerance Schema

All float comparisons use named constants; comparing to exact zero is forbidden (P6).

Constant Default Used in
EPS_FEAS 1e-8 primal feasibility check, Phase I termination
EPS_OPT 1e-8 optimality / dual feasibility (reduced cost sign)
EPS_PIVOT 1e-7 pivot element too small → skip column or refactorize
EPS_RATIO 1e-9 ratio test tie-breaking
COND_THRESHOLD 1e12 condition estimate → force refactorization
REFACT_INTERVAL 50 max eta updates before scheduled refactorization
ITER_LIMIT 1_000_000 hard iteration cap → Status::IterationLimit

8.12 Presolve Undo Stack

Each presolve transformation pushes a typed record:

Record Fields Postsolve action
RemoveEmptyRow(i) row index no-op (dual = 0)
FixVariable(j, val) VarId, fixed value set x[j] = val; compute dual contribution
SubstituteSingleton(j, i, coef) VarId, row, coefficient x[j] = (b[i] - sum_{k!=j} a_ik x_k) / coef
TightenBound(j, old_lb, old_ub) VarId, bounds restore bounds; verify optimum unchanged
RemoveRedundantRow(i, multiplier) row, Farkas multiplier compute dual[i] = multiplier * base_dual
Scale(row_scales, col_scales) Vec<f64>, Vec<f64> x[j] /= col_scales[j]; y[i] /= row_scales[i]

Postsolve processes the stack in reverse order (LIFO). Invariant I9 requires postsolve to produce a solution feasible to the original problem within EPS_FEAS.

8.13 Edge Cases

Case Detection Handling
All-zero row during presolve remove if b[i]==0; else infeasible
Duplicate rows hash comparison in presolve keep one; set dual of removed = 0
Unbounded variable (no finite bounds) var_map free-split split x+/x- in standard form
Artificial remains basic at 0 Phase I termination pivot out using degenerate pivot; if impossible, row is dependent
Basis becomes singular mid-solve pivot element < EPS_PIVOT refactorize; if still singular, mark Status::Numerical
Cycling detected (stall counter) same objective N consecutive iters switch pricing to Bland (P3 / I7)
Very dense column nnz > density_threshold deprioritize in Devex pricing to limit fill
b[i] < 0 after row selection detected in to_standard_form multiply row by -1; record in row_signs
Free variable with artificial Ge/Eq row for free var split var, add artificial as usual
Infeasible bounds (lb > ub) validation in builder return error before solving

9. Tech Stack ADR Table

ADR # Decision Locked Choice Rejected Alternatives Rationale When the Rejected Choice Wins
ADR-01 Implementation language Rust (stable toolchain) C++, Python, Julia Memory safety without GC; zero-cost abstractions; f64 layout guarantees; ecosystem for numerical crates C++ when integrating with existing solver codebases (CLP/SoPlex); Python/Julia for a research prototype prioritizing modeling expressiveness over runtime
ADR-02 Build system Cargo workspace (one workspace root, 13 member crates) Bazel, cmake, buck Native Rust; per-crate Cargo.toml; workspace-level [profile]; no extra toolchain Bazel/cmake when integrating into a polyglot or existing C++ numerical build
ADR-03 Dependency policy Minimal: rayon (optional parallelism), serde+serde_json (solution I/O), clap (CLI), thiserror (error types), criterion (bench) nalgebra, ndarray, faer Keep numerical core self-contained; nalgebra/ndarray add overhead without simplex-specific primitives nalgebra/ndarray/faer when you need general dense linear algebra (eigen/SVD) beyond simplex-specific sparse ops
ADR-04 Matrix storage format Column-major CSC (SparseMatrix in lp-core) Dense Vec<Vec<f64>>, row-major CSR, triplet COO Simplex accesses columns (entering variable); CSC gives O(nnz_col) per column operation Dense Vec<Vec<f64>> for tiny dense problems where simplicity wins; COO for one-time assembly before conversion
ADR-05 Dense tableau engine Plain Vec<f64> row-major 2D array in lp-tableau BLAS, ndarray Dense tableau is the reference/test oracle only; clarity and differential-testing matter more than performance here BLAS/ndarray when the dense path must actually be fast (not just a reference) for moderate dense problems
ADR-06 Revised simplex basis representation Eta-vector product form + sparse LU in lp-lu Full explicit B^-1, dense LU Eta vectors accumulate cheaply; LU gives stable FTRAN/BTRAN; refactorize periodically → O(nnz) memory Explicit B⁻¹ for very small bases where dense updates are cache-friendly; dense LU when the basis itself is dense
ADR-07 LU update algorithm Forrest-Tomlin (spike-based rank-1) Bartels-Golub (column replacement), MINOS PFI Forrest-Tomlin preserves sparsity better on rank-1 pivots; Bartels-Golub available as fallback Bartels-Golub when stability under arbitrary column replacement matters more than sparsity preservation; PFI for implementation simplicity
ADR-08 Pivot pricing (default) Dantzig (most-negative reduced cost) with fallback to Bland Steepest-edge only, random, largest-increase Dantzig standard reference; Bland mandatory for anti-cycling (P3/I7); Devex optional for performance Steepest-edge/Devex when pivot count (not per-pivot cost) dominates on large problems — fewer, better-chosen pivots win
ADR-09 Anti-cycling mechanism Bland's rule (index-based) as primary; lexicographic perturbation as secondary Perturbation only, no anti-cycling Bland guarantees termination in finite pivots; lexicographic is faster but more complex; both implemented Perturbation-only when degeneracy is mild and Bland’s per-pivot index scan would dominate runtime
ADR-10 Two-phase vs Big-M Two-phase (Phase I minimises sum-of-artificials; Phase II optimises original) Big-M penalty Big-M introduces numerical instability from large coefficients; two-phase is numerically clean Big-M when a single solve with a safe, problem-bounded M is simpler and coefficients stay well-scaled
ADR-11 Bounded-variable handling Native ratio-test extension in lp-bounds (bound flips, NonbasicUpper status) Add explicit bound constraints as rows Explicit rows grow m; native BV simplex keeps basis small (P9) Explicit bound rows when the solver lacks a bounded-variable ratio test and added rows are acceptable for small problems
ADR-12 Dual simplex Implemented in lp-dual for warm-start reoptimisation Re-solve from scratch Warm-start on bound/RHS changes avoids redundant Phase I; essential for branch-and-bound parent (P8) Re-solve from scratch when no warm-start basis is available (a brand-new unrelated problem)
ADR-13 Presolve scope Singleton rows/cols, empty rows, bound tightening, redundancy removal, fixed-variable substitution Full network-simplex presolve, interior-point-style presolve Covers >80% of practical reduction; more complex presolve rules deferred to stretch goals Full IPM-style/network presolve for problems with heavy structure (network flows) where aggressive reduction pays off
ADR-14 Scaling Geometric mean scaling (rows then columns); Curtis-Reid iterative as alternative Equilibration, no scaling Geometric mean is O(nnz) per pass, reduces condition without dense ops; Curtis-Reid available when condition stays high Equilibration when a single max-norm pass suffices; no scaling when the matrix is already well-conditioned (avoids a pass)
ADR-15 Serialisation format (solution) JSON via serde_json (default); plain-text summary for CLI CSV, binary protobuf, MessagePack JSON is human-readable for debugging; basis warm-start uses a compact custom binary (ADR-16) protobuf/MessagePack when solution volume is large and compact binary I/O matters; CSV for spreadsheet interop
ADR-16 Basis warm-start format Custom little-endian binary (magic + version + VarStatus bytes) JSON basis, CPLEX .bas format Minimal size; fast read/write; version field allows forward compatibility CPLEX .bas format when interoperating with CPLEX warm-starts; JSON basis when warm-start readability matters
ADR-17 MPS parser Hand-written fixed-field MPS reader in lp-parse nom/pest grammar, regex Fixed-field MPS is simple enough; hand-written avoids parser-combinator dependency; covers free-format MPS variant too A parser-combinator grammar when the input format is complex/evolving and a declarative grammar eases maintenance
ADR-18 LP-format parser Hand-written recursive-descent in lp-parse LALRPOP, pest LP format grammar is LL(2); recursive descent is sufficient and avoids code-gen in build A parser generator (LALRPOP/pest) when the LP grammar grows beyond LL(2) and ambiguity handling is needed
ADR-19 Error handling thiserror-derived error enums per crate; no unwrap in library code anyhow, Box<dyn Error> Typed errors allow match on Status and structured propagation; anyhow acceptable in lp-cli binary only anyhow in the CLI binary where error kinds are never matched
ADR-20 Concurrency model Single-threaded solver core; optional rayon for batch-solve parallelism at CLI level async/tokio, per-pivot threads Simplex inner loop is sequential by design; parallelism at problem level (multiple independent solves) Per-pivot/block parallelism when a single huge LP’s FTRAN/BTRAN can be parallelized (large sparse bases on many cores)
ADR-21 Logging tracing crate with structured spans (per-phase, per-pivot-batch) log crate, env_logger tracing supports span-based profiling; spans align to phases and LU refactorizations for perf diagnosis The log facade when span-based per-pivot profiling is not needed
ADR-22 Metrics / instrumentation Counter and histogram via tracing attributes; no external metrics server for library Prometheus client, statsd Library has no network dependency; metrics consumed by CLI or test harness via tracing subscriber Prometheus when the solver runs as a long-lived service exposing metrics over the network
ADR-23 Unit testing Cargo #[test] in each crate; property tests via proptest for standard-form roundtrip and ratio test QuickCheck, fuzz-only proptest shrinks counterexamples; unit tests cover tableau arithmetic and LU update correctness Fuzz-only when inputs are adversarial parser inputs rather than structured properties; QuickCheck if already standardized on it
ADR-24 Fuzzing cargo-fuzz (libFuzzer) targets on MPS parser and pivot input AFL, honggfuzz cargo-fuzz integrates with Cargo workspace; coverage-guided; targets parser and numerical edge inputs AFL/honggfuzz for whole-binary, coverage-guided fuzzing of the CLI
ADR-25 Conformance testing Netlib LP test set; compare objective to reference (GLPK/CLP) within 1e-6 relative tolerance MIPLIB (MIP problems), random LP generation only Netlib covers degenerate, near-infeasible, and large-scale cases; standard benchmark in LP literature MIPLIB once MIP is implemented (Deferred); random LP generation to stress numerical edge cases beyond Netlib coverage
ADR-26 Benchmarking criterion micro-benchmarks (pivot, FTRAN, LU refact); end-to-end solve time on Netlib subset bench attribute, hyperfine criterion provides statistical confidence intervals; Netlib end-to-end catches regression on realistic problems hyperfine for whole-binary end-to-end timing; the #[bench] attribute when pinned to nightly
ADR-27 CI/CD GitHub Actions: cargo test --workspace, cargo clippy -- -D warnings, cargo fmt --check, fuzz for 60s on PR Jenkins, GitLab CI GitHub Actions free for open-source; matrix over stable + beta Rust; fuzz gate catches parser panics early Self-hosted CI for private repos needing large-memory runners for big Netlib instances
ADR-28 Packaging Crates published to crates.io as a workspace; lp-cli as a standalone binary via cargo install Homebrew formula, Nix flake crates.io standard for Rust; downstream can depend on lp-core/lp-simplex as library crates Homebrew/Nix when distributing the CLI to users who manage tooling via those package managers
ADR-29 Reproducibility Deterministic pivot sequence given same problem + tolerances + PivotRule; no global mutable state; rand seeded only for perturbation, seed exposed in config Non-deterministic tie-breaking P10 reproducibility; required for differential testing against GLPK/CLP; seed in config enables debugging Non-deterministic (randomized) tie-breaking when it empirically reduces pivot count and reproducibility is not required
ADR-30 Licensing MIT OR Apache-2.0 (dual) GPL (would infect downstream), BSD-3 Standard Rust ecosystem dual license; compatible with GLPK test data reference (GPL data only for tests, not linked) GPL when copyleft reciprocity is desired; BSD-3 when a permissive license without Apache’s patent clause is preferred

11. Core Schema and Core Types

All types below are normative. Phase implementations must use these exact names and shapes. No type in this section may be renamed or structurally altered without a corresponding ADR update.

11.1 lp-core types

Primitive identities

Type Signature Ownership Mutation Concurrency Serialization Compat
VarId struct VarId(pub u32); Copy immutable after creation Send + Sync serde: transparent u32 stable across problem versions if var order preserved
ConstraintId struct ConstraintId(pub u32); Copy immutable after creation Send + Sync serde: transparent u32 same
  • Both newtypes derive Copy, Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord, Hash.
  • VarId(u32::MAX) is reserved as a sentinel; never allocate it.

Enumerations

enum Sense { Maximize, Minimize }
enum Relation { Le, Eq, Ge }       // constraint sense relative to RHS
enum Status {
    Optimal,
    Infeasible,
    Unbounded,
    IterationLimit,
    Numerical,
}
enum VarStatus {
    Basic,
    NonbasicLower,
    NonbasicUpper,
    Free,                           // nonbasic free variable at zero
}
  • All derive Copy, Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq.
  • Sense and Relation derive Hash for use in hash maps.
  • Status must always appear paired with a Certificate (P5; I8); a bare Status value returned from a public solve function is a compile error enforced by returning SolveResult.

Bound

struct Bound { pub lower: f64, pub upper: f64 }
  • lower = f64::NEG_INFINITY → no lower bound; upper = f64::INFINITY → no upper bound.
  • is_free(&self) -> bool — both infinite.
  • is_fixed(&self) -> bool — lower == upper && lower.is_finite().
  • contains(&self, v: f64) -> bool — within [lower - FEAS_TOL, upper + FEAS_TOL].
  • Owned value type; Copy.

Tolerances

// tolerances.rs — all named constants; never use magic floats
const FEAS_TOL:  f64 = 1e-7;    // primal feasibility; I2
const OPT_TOL:   f64 = 1e-8;    // reduced-cost optimality; I4
const PIVOT_TOL: f64 = 1e-7;    // minimum pivot element magnitude; I1
const REFACTOR_FILL_RATIO: f64 = 3.0;  // trigger refactorization when eta fill exceeds this × original nnz
const MAX_CONDITION_EST:   f64 = 1e12; // trigger refactorization when condition estimate exceeds
  • ADR 0005 records rationale; changing a value requires ADR update.
  • P6: no inline float literal comparisons to zero anywhere in the codebase.

SolverError

enum SolverError {
    DimensionMismatch { expected: usize, got: usize },
    InvalidBound { var: VarId, lower: f64, upper: f64 },
    SingularBasis,
    NumericalFailure { context: &'static str },
    ParseError { line: usize, msg: String },
    IterationLimitReached { limit: usize },
    InternalInvariantViolation(&'static str),
}
  • Implements std::error::Error.
  • InternalInvariantViolation carries the invariant label (e.g. "I1: basis singularity").

11.2 lp-model types

SparseMatrix

struct SparseMatrix {
    pub nrows: usize,
    pub ncols: usize,
    values:   Vec<f64>,   // nonzero values, column-major
    row_ind:  Vec<usize>, // row index per value
    col_ptr:  Vec<usize>, // col_ptr[j]..col_ptr[j+1] spans column j; len = ncols+1
}
  • Column-major CSC layout for efficient column access (FTRAN, pricing).
  • col(j) -> impl Iterator<Item=(usize, f64)> — row-index, value pairs.
  • Mutable only during problem construction; frozen before solve via SparseMatrix::freeze().
  • Not Clone by default (large allocation); explicit deep_clone() required.
  • Serialized as three flat arrays + dims (MessagePack or bincode for speed).

LpProblem

struct LpProblem {
    pub sense:      Sense,
    pub objective:  Vec<f64>,       // length = n_vars; c[j] for var j
    pub matrix:     SparseMatrix,   // A (m x n); m = #constraints, n = #vars
    pub rhs:        Vec<f64>,       // length = m
    pub relations:  Vec<Relation>,  // length = m
    pub bounds:     Vec<Bound>,     // length = n_vars; default Bound{0, +inf}
    pub var_names:  Vec<String>,    // optional; empty = unnamed
    pub con_names:  Vec<String>,    // optional; empty = unnamed
}
  • Constructed via ProblemBuilder; direct field mutation only during construction phase.
  • validate(&self) -> Result<(), SolverError> — dimension checks, finite-or-inf bounds, no NaN in data.
  • Owned; not Copy; Clone derived for test purposes but discouraged in hot paths.
  • Serializeable to/from MPS via lp-parse writer.

StandardForm

struct StandardForm {
    pub a_std:        SparseMatrix,  // [A | I_slack | I_art] in CSC, m x n_total
    pub b:            Vec<f64>,      // RHS, length m; all >= 0 after row sign flip
    pub c:            Vec<f64>,      // minimization objective, length n_total
    pub n_struct:     usize,         // structural variables (original + bound split)
    pub n_slack:      usize,         // slack/surplus variables added
    pub n_artificial: usize,         // artificial variables for Phase I
    pub var_map:      VarMap,        // back-mapping: standard index -> original VarId + type
}
  • Produced by to_standard_form(problem: &LpProblem) -> Result<StandardForm>.
  • Invariant I10: objective of StandardForm equals original LP objective at any feasible point.
  • Immutable after construction; passed by reference to simplex drivers.

VarMap

struct VarMap {
    entries: Vec<VarMapEntry>,  // index = standard-form column index
}
enum VarMapEntry {
    Structural { orig: VarId, split_neg: bool },
    Slack      { con: ConstraintId },
    Artificial { con: ConstraintId },
}
  • recover(std_index: usize, x_std: &[f64]) -> (VarId, f64) — maps solution back to original variable value (handles split free vars by subtracting positive/negative parts).
  • Postsolve uses VarMap after presolve's own Postsolve stack is unwound.

11.3 lp-tableau types

Tableau

struct Tableau {
    tab:   Vec<Vec<f64>>,  // tab[i][j]: row i = constraint i, row m = objective; col n = RHS
    basis: Vec<usize>,     // basis[i] = column index of basic var for row i; length m
    m:     usize,          // number of constraints
    n:     usize,          // total columns (struct + slack + artificial)
}
  • Dense O(m·n) layout; Phase 3 reference implementation only (P4).
  • pivot(row: usize, col: usize) -> Result<(), SolverError> — signature only; performs full tableau update.
  • rhs(&self) -> &[f64] — last column slice (basic variable values).
  • Mutable during solve; not shared across threads.

RatioTestResult

struct RatioTestResult {
    pub leaving:    Option<usize>,  // None = unbounded direction
    pub step:       f64,            // theta: step size along entering direction
    pub bound_flip: bool,           // entering variable hit its opposite bound (Phase 8)
}
  • bound_flip = true only when bounded-variable simplex is active (lp-bounds).
  • leaving = None with step = f64::INFINITY → unbounded; caller emits Certificate::PrimalRay.

11.4 lp-revised types

Basis

struct Basis {
    pub basic:   Vec<VarId>,        // length m; basic[i] = variable occupying row i of basis
    pub status:  Vec<VarStatus>,    // length n_total; status per variable
    pub factor:  LuFactorization,   // current factorization of B; see lp-lu
}
  • Central mutable state during revised simplex and dual simplex.
  • update(entering: VarId, leaving_row: usize, d: &[f64]) -> Result<(), SolverError> — perform one pivot: update basic, status, and call lp-lu rank-1 update.
  • save() -> SavedBasis / restore(s: SavedBasis) — enable warm-start (P8).
  • Not Sync; owned by a single solver thread.

SavedBasis

struct SavedBasis {
    pub basic:  Vec<VarId>,
    pub status: Vec<VarStatus>,
    // LuFactorization NOT saved; rebuilt on restore to avoid stale factors
}
  • Cheaply cloneable; caller stores for warm-start.
  • On restore, solver calls LuFactorization::factorize from the current basis columns.

11.5 lp-lu types

LuFactorization

struct LuFactorization {
    pub l_eta:     Vec<EtaVector>,    // product-form L update vectors (one per pivot after initial factor)
    pub u:         SparseMatrix,      // upper-triangular U factor (CSC)
    pub row_perm:  PermutationVec,    // row permutation P from partial pivoting
    pub col_perm:  PermutationVec,    // column permutation Q for sparsity
    pub n_updates: usize,             // count of rank-1 updates since last full factorization
}
  • factorize(b_cols: &[&[f64]], nrows: usize) -> Result<LuFactorization, SolverError> — full Markowitz/partial-pivot LU.
  • ftran_solve(&self, rhs: &mut Vec<f64>) -> Result<(), SolverError> — solves B x = rhs in place.
  • btran_solve(&self, rhs: &mut Vec<f64>) -> Result<(), SolverError> — solves B^T y = rhs in place.
  • forrest_tomlin_update(&mut self, entering_col: &[f64], leaving_row: usize) -> Result<(), SolverError>.
  • should_refactorize(&self) -> bool — true if n_updates > threshold or fill ratio exceeded (REFACTOR_FILL_RATIO) or condition estimate > MAX_CONDITION_EST.

EtaVector

struct EtaVector {
    pub pivot_row: usize,
    pub indices:   Vec<usize>,
    pub values:    Vec<f64>,
}
  • Sparse representation of one column of the product-form L factor update.
  • Applied left-to-right in FTRAN; right-to-left (transposed) in BTRAN.

PermutationVec

struct PermutationVec { forward: Vec<usize>, inverse: Vec<usize> }
  • apply_forward(&self, v: &mut [f64]) / apply_inverse(&self, v: &mut [f64]) — in-place permutation.
  • Maintained in sync: forward[i] = j iff inverse[j] = i.

11.6 lp-pricing types

Pricer trait

trait Pricer {
    fn select_entering(
        &self,
        reduced_costs: &[f64],
        status: &[VarStatus],
        sense: Sense,
    ) -> Option<usize>;
}
  • Dantzig, Bland, Devex all implement Pricer.
  • select_entering returns None when no improving direction exists (optimality).
  • Stateful types (Devex weights) hold &mut self versions for the weight update.

11.7 lp-sensitivity types

Sensitivity

struct Sensitivity {
    pub obj_ranges:    Vec<(f64, f64)>,  // per structural var: (c_j_lo, c_j_hi) keeping basis optimal
    pub rhs_ranges:    Vec<(f64, f64)>,  // per constraint: (b_i_lo, b_i_hi) keeping basis feasible
    pub shadow_prices: Vec<f64>,         // dual values y_i = c_B B^-1 e_i; length m
    pub reduced_costs: Vec<f64>,         // \bar{c}_j for all vars; length n_total
}
  • Computed from Basis and LpProblem after a confirmed Status::Optimal.
  • All ranges are half-open: a value of f64::NEG_INFINITY or f64::INFINITY means the basis remains optimal regardless in that direction.
  • Immutable value type; returned by value from compute_sensitivity.

11.8 lp-simplex output types

Solution

struct Solution {
    pub status:         Status,
    pub certificate:    Certificate,     // P5: always present
    pub objective:      f64,             // primal objective at optimum (or best infeasible bound)
    pub x:              Vec<f64>,        // primal variable values, length = n_struct (original vars)
    pub duals_y:        Vec<f64>,        // dual multipliers, length m
    pub reduced_costs:  Vec<f64>,        // \bar{c}_j, length = n_struct
    pub iterations:     usize,
    pub solve_time_ms:  f64,
}

Certificate

enum Certificate {
    KktOptimal,                   // KKT conditions verified within tolerances
    PrimalRay(Vec<f64>),          // direction d s.t. c^T d < 0 and A d <= 0; proves unbounded
    DualRay(Vec<f64>),            // y s.t. y^T b < 0, y^T A >= 0; proves infeasibility (Farkas)
}
  • I8: PrimalRay(d) must satisfy: for all i, (A d)[i] <= FEAS_TOL, and c^T d < -OPT_TOL.
  • I8: DualRay(y) must satisfy Farkas' lemma within FEAS_TOL.
  • KktOptimal implies primal feasibility (I2), dual feasibility (I4), and strong duality (I5) all checked.

11.9 Cross-cutting type semantics summary

Property Policy
Float comparisons Always use named tolerances (FEAS_TOL, OPT_TOL, PIVOT_TOL); never == 0.0
NaN propagation Any NaN in tableau/basis data triggers SolverError::NumericalFailure immediately
Thread safety Solver state (Basis, Tableau, LuFactorization) is single-threaded; VarId, Bound, Status, Solution are Send + Sync
Serialization Solution and Sensitivity implement serde::Serialize; problem types use lp-parse writer, not serde
Versioning VarId and ConstraintId ordinals are stable within a problem instance; no cross-problem ID reuse
Invariant enforcement Debug builds assert I1–I10 at each phase boundary; release builds assert I1 (basis singularity) always
Error propagation All fallible operations return Result<T, SolverError>; unwrap forbidden outside tests
Anti-cycling (I7) PivotRule::Bland activates after STALL_THRESHOLD = 50 consecutive degenerate (zero-step) pivots

Phase 1 — Core Types + LP Problem Model

1.1 Objectives

  • Define all shared identity and enumeration types in lp-core with zero dependencies.
  • Implement LpProblem and SparseMatrix in lp-model with a builder API and full validation.
  • Establish dimension-agreement and bound-validity invariants enforced at construction time.
  • No solver logic; this phase produces the data model only.

1.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Anchors: ADR-04 (CSC sparse storage decision for SparseMatrix); P10 (VarId assigned in stable problem-builder insertion order — no hash-order dependence).

lp-core crate (src/lib.rs, src/types.rs):

Type Definition
VarId(u32) Newtype; indexes into objective/bounds/solution vecs
ConstraintId(u32) Newtype; indexes into rhs/relation vecs
Sense enum { Maximize, Minimize }
Relation enum { Le, Eq, Ge } — row-wise relation to rhs
Bound struct { lower: f64, upper: f64 } where ±f64::INFINITY encodes unboundedness
VarStatus enum { Basic, NonbasicLower, NonbasicUpper, Free }
Status enum { Optimal, Infeasible, Unbounded, IterationLimit, Numerical }

Derive Copy, Clone, Debug, PartialEq for all enums. Bound::free() sets both to infinity; Bound::nonneg() sets lower=0, upper=+inf.

lp-model crate (src/sparse.rs, src/problem.rs, src/builder.rs): SparseMatrix — column-major CSC:

values:     Vec<f64>          // nonzeros in column order
row_ind:    Vec<usize>        // row index per nonzero
col_ptr:    Vec<usize>        // col_ptr[j]..col_ptr[j+1] = column j's range; len = ncols+1
nrows, ncols: usize

Methods: col_iter(j) yields (row, val); to_dense() -> Vec<Vec<f64>> for testing; nnz(). LpProblem:

sense:      Sense
objective:  Vec<f64>         // length = n_vars; cost coefficients c
matrix:     SparseMatrix     // A: m rows, n_vars cols
rhs:        Vec<f64>         // length = m
relations:  Vec<Relation>    // length = m
bounds:     Vec<Bound>       // length = n_vars

LpBuilder: add_var(name, bound) -> VarId appends objective (0.0 default) + bounds; set_objective(sense, coeffs); add_constraint(coeffs: &[(VarId, f64)], rel, rhs) inserts CSC column entries; build() -> Result<LpProblem, ModelError> calls validate().

validate() checks: objective.len() == matrix.ncols; rhs.len() == relations.len() == matrix.nrows; bounds.len() == matrix.ncols; each Bound: lower <= upper, neither NaN; ≥1 variable and ≥1 constraint. Returns ModelError::DimensionMismatch or ModelError::InvalidBound on failure. ModelError enum in lp-core: DimensionMismatch { expected, got }, InvalidBound { var, lower, upper }, EmptyProblem.

1.3 Deliverable

  • lp-core compiles, exports all primitive types, passes unit tests for each enum variant.
  • lp-model compiles; LpBuilder produces a valid LpProblem from hand-coded small problems.
  • validate() rejects malformed problems with descriptive errors.
  • SparseMatrix::col_iter and to_dense pass round-trip tests.
  • No solver code exists; cargo test -p lp-core -p lp-model passes cleanly.

1.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Pass condition
Dimension mismatch rejected validate() returns Err(DimensionMismatch) for mismatched vecs
Invalid bound rejected lower > upper → Err(InvalidBound)
NaN bound rejected lower = NaN → Err(InvalidBound)
Round-trip sparse to_dense(SparseMatrix::from_dense(M)) == M for all test matrices
Builder round-trip 3-var / 2-constraint LP builds without error; objective, bounds exact
Zero-dependency lp-core cargo tree -p lp-core shows no external dependencies

1.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected result
fixtures/phase1/valid_2x3.lp.json 2 constraints, 3 vars, all Le, nonneg bounds builds OK, dimensions match
fixtures/phase1/dim_mismatch.json objective length != ncols DimensionMismatch
fixtures/phase1/bad_bound.json lower=5, upper=1 InvalidBound
fixtures/phase1/nan_bound.json lower=NaN InvalidBound
fixtures/phase1/empty_problem.json 0 vars EmptyProblem
fixtures/phase1/free_var.json one free variable (both bounds inf) builds OK

1.6 Pitfalls

  • NaN propagation in bounds: lower <= upper is false when either is NaN; add explicit NaN checks before comparing.
  • CSC col_ptr off-by-one: length must be ncols + 1; col_ptr[ncols] == nnz. Missing sentinel → OOB panics in column iteration.
  • Newtype confusion: VarId(u32)/ConstraintId(u32) share underlying type; mixing them in slices silently compiles. No From<u32> conversions in public API.
  • Infinity sentinel comparison: prefer bound.lower.is_infinite() && bound.lower < 0.0 over raw == NEG_INFINITY; document canonical form.
  • Empty matrix cols: a variable with zero nonzeros in A is valid; col_iter must return empty iterator, not panic.

1.7 Performance Concerns

  • LpBuilder pre-allocates Vec capacities via with_capacity when counts are known; avoids reallocation for large problems.
  • SparseMatrix is column-major by design; row-iteration is O(nnz), test-only; hot solver paths use col_iter only.
  • validate() is O(nnz + m + n); called once at build time, not in any inner loop.

1.8 Edge Cases

  • Single-variable, single-constraint LP (1×1) — minimal path; test pivot arithmetic later.
  • All-equality constraints (all Relation::Eq) — valid; Phase 2 adds only artificials, no slacks.
  • Variable with both bounds equal (fixed) — valid; Phase 12 presolve may substitute it out.
  • Zero objective coefficients — valid; problem is a feasibility LP.
  • Constraint with all-zero row in A — structurally valid; validate() accepts; Phase 12 presolve removes it.
  • rhs[i] = ±infinity — rejected in validate() as InvalidRhs.

1.9 Failure Analysis

  • Root cause: incorrect col_ptr construction in LpBuilder → iterator returns garbage rows → downstream pivot indexing corrupts basis.
  • Detection: SparseMatrix::check_invariants() asserts col_ptr monotone, all row_ind[k] < nrows, values.len() == nnz.
  • Containment: call check_invariants() in debug builds at end of build(); panic early rather than silently corrupt.
  • Recovery: fix builder accumulation logic; re-run fixture suite.
  • Operator visibility: ModelError carries structured fields; surfaced via thiserror formatting in CLI.

1.10 Operational Notes

  • LpProblem is immutable after build(); downstream phases receive a shared reference.
  • Logging: tracing::debug! emits n_vars, n_constraints, nnz at build completion.
  • No file I/O here; parsing lives in lp-parse (Phase 13).
  • Serialization: derive serde::Serialize/Deserialize on all lp-core types for fixture JSON round-trips and future checkpointing.
  • Compatibility: VarId/ConstraintId are u32 (not usize) to keep serialized fixtures stable across 32/64-bit targets.

Phase 2 — Standard-Form Conversion

2.1 Objectives

  • Transform any LpProblem into StandardForm suitable for simplex: minimize c·x, A_std x = b, x >= 0.
  • Handle all four cases: Le (add slack), Ge (add surplus + artificial), Eq (add artificial only).
  • Normalize Sense::Maximize by negating the objective.
  • Handle bounded variables via bounded-variable bookkeeping or free-variable splitting.
  • Record var_map so any StandardForm solution maps exactly back to original variable indices.
  • Invariant I10: optimal objective of standard form == optimal of original (up to sign flip).

2.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Anchors: I10 (standard-form equivalence — proven by matching original/standard objectives and asserted in to_standard_form/recover); P7 (presolve → standard-form → solve → postsolve pipeline positions this stage). lp-model crate additions (src/standard_form.rs, src/conversion.rs):

StandardForm struct:

A_std:         SparseMatrix      // m x n_total; extended with slack/artificial cols
b:             Vec<f64>          // rhs, all >= 0 after row sign-flip
c:             Vec<f64>          // objective (minimization); artificials get Big-M or 0
n_struct:      usize             // original structural variables (possibly split)
n_slack:       usize             // added slack / surplus variables
n_artificial:  usize             // added artificial variables
var_map:       Vec<VarMapEntry>  // len = n_struct; maps back to original VarId + type

VarMapEntry:

enum VarMapEntry {
    Original(VarId),                    // direct mapping
    SplitPos(VarId),                    // x = x+ - x-, this is x+
    SplitNeg(VarId),                    // this is x-
    Shifted(VarId, f64),                // x' = x - lb; lb stored
}

Conversion algorithm (to_standard_form(prob: &LpProblem) -> StandardForm):

  1. Sign normalization: if sense == Maximize, negate c; store negated.
  2. RHS sign normalization: for row i where b[i] < 0, negate row i in A and flip relations[i] (Le ↔ Ge, Eq unchanged).
  3. Free variables: for each VarId where bound.lower == -inf && bound.upper == +inf, split into x+ - x- both >= 0; two structural columns; var_map records SplitPos/SplitNeg.
  4. Bounded variables with finite lower bound: shift x' = x - lb; substitute into A and b; record Shifted(id, lb).
  5. Slack/surplus column insertion by relation:
    • Le: add slack col +e_i; column has single +1.0 in row i.
    • Ge: add surplus col -e_i (coefficient -1.0) + artificial col +e_i.
    • Eq: add artificial col +e_i.
  6. Objective for artificials: for two-phase, set artificial objective coefficients to 0.0 in the standard-form objective (Phase I will substitute); record artificial column indices.
  7. Assemble extended CSC A_std by appending slack/artificial columns to the original.
  8. Emit StandardForm.

VarMap::recover(sf: &StandardForm, x_std: &[f64]) -> Vec<f64>:

  • For Original(id): x[id] = x_std[col].
  • For SplitPos(id) + SplitNeg(id): x[id] = x_std[pos_col] - x_std[neg_col].
  • For Shifted(id, lb): x[id] = x_std[col] + lb.
  • Negative recovered values (within tolerance) clamped to bound; beyond tolerance is a postsolve failure.

2.3 Deliverable

  • to_standard_form() produces valid StandardForm for all Relation variants.
  • recover() inverts the transformation on any feasible standard-form solution.
  • Unit tests confirm A_std has correct column counts: n_struct + n_slack + n_artificial.
  • Maximize problem: negated objective produces correct minimum; recover() negates back.
  • cargo test -p lp-model passes.

2.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Pass condition
Column count A_std.ncols == n_struct + n_slack + n_artificial
Slack column Le row i: column n_struct+i has single +1.0 at row i
Artificial column Ge/Eq row i: artificial column has single +1.0 at row i
RHS non-negative all b[i] >= 0 after sign normalization
Maximize negation sf.c[j] == -prob.objective[j] for structural cols when sense=Maximize
Free-var split free var x=2.0 recovered from x+=2.0, x-=0.0
Shift recovery shifted var lb=3, x_std=1.5 → recovered x=4.5
Invariant I10 optimal standard-form objective == original objective (negated if Maximize)

2.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected
fixtures/phase2/le_only.json all Le constraints n_artificial=0, n_slack=m
fixtures/phase2/ge_only.json all Ge constraints n_artificial=m, n_slack=m
fixtures/phase2/eq_only.json all Eq constraints n_artificial=m, n_slack=0
fixtures/phase2/mixed.json Le+Ge+Eq mix counts match per-row logic
fixtures/phase2/free_var.json one free variable n_struct += 1 (split), recover correct
fixtures/phase2/maximize.json Maximize sense c negated; recovered obj matches
fixtures/phase2/neg_rhs.json b[0]<0 with Ge row flipped to Le; b[0]>0 after

2.6 Pitfalls

  • Double-negation on Maximize + negative RHS: negating the objective and independently negating a row that had b < 0 are independent operations; do not apply row-sign flip to the objective.
  • Free variable indexing drift: splitting a free variable increases n_struct; all subsequent column indices in var_map shift; compute var_map after all splits, not incrementally.
  • Artificial vs. surplus confusion: a Ge row needs both surplus (-1) and artificial (+1); omitting the surplus produces an infeasible initial basis for Phase I.
  • Bound-shifted b[i] not updated: shifting variable x' = x - lb substitutes lb into the RHS: b'[i] = b[i] - a_ij * lb for each row j containing variable i; missing this shifts Phase I artificials away from zero.
  • Equality rows and a natural basis: if A already contains an identity column matching an equality constraint (slack inserted for another row), do not insert a second artificial; check for an existing unit column before inserting.

2.7 Performance Concerns

  • Column insertion into CSC is O(nnz); build slack/artificial columns as independent small CSC blocks then concatenate column arrays once; avoid repeated Vec::insert in the middle.
  • var_map is allocated once at size n_total; no per-column heap allocation.
  • RHS sign-flip is O(m) scalar pass; done in place.

2.8 Edge Cases

  • LP with only equality constraints and no natural basis (needs all artificials).
  • Variable with lower == upper (fixed): shift reduces it to x'=0 with single allowed value; standard form has a trivial column.
  • Variable with lower = -inf, upper = 5.0 (upper-bounded only, not free): shift to x'' = upper - x; handled separately from fully-free case.
  • Objective with all-zero structural coefficients (feasibility problem): valid; standard form proceeds; optimal value is 0.
  • n_vars = 1, n_constraints = 1: minimal path; verify column counts are exactly 1+1+1 or 1+1+0 depending on relation.

2.9 Failure Analysis

  • Incorrect RHS after bound shift: recovered solution violates original constraint.
  • Detection: StandardForm::check_feasibility(x_std, tol) — recompute A_std * x_std and compare to b componentwise.
  • Blast radius: every subsequent phase uses b; a wrong shift corrupts all solutions.
  • Recovery: add debug_assert!(check_feasibility(...)) at end of to_standard_form against a known-feasible test point.
  • Postmortem signal: log n_struct, n_slack, n_artificial, nnz_A_std, b_min, b_max at conversion; negative b_min after normalization indicates a missed sign flip.

2.10 Operational Notes

  • tracing::debug! logs n_struct, n_slack, n_artificial, nnz at conversion exit.
  • StandardForm is immutable; solver phases hold a shared reference.
  • var_map serialization required for warm-start persistence (Phase 11); derive serde.
  • Phase 12 (presolve) may modify the LpProblem before this conversion; the conversion must be re-run after presolve.
  • FEASIBILITY_TOL = 1e-8, OPTIMALITY_TOL = 1e-8, PIVOT_TOL = 1e-7 defined as public constants in lp-core::tolerances; referenced here and used consistently in all downstream phases (P6).

Phase 3 — Dense Tableau + Pivot Operation

3.1 Objectives

  • Implement an explicit (m × n) simplex tableau in lp-tableau as the reference engine.
  • Provide a correct, readable pivot(row, col) operation: normalize pivot row, eliminate pivot column from all other rows and the objective row.
  • Maintain reduced costs and basic feasible solution (RHS column) in the tableau.
  • This is the clarity/oracle implementation; it is NOT the production path (that is lp-revised + lp-lu).
  • All subsequent solver phases in lp-simplex use lp-tableau for differential testing (P4).

3.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Anchors: P4 (lp-tableau is the reference oracle, never the production path); ADR-05 (dense tableau engine decision).

lp-tableau crate (src/tableau.rs, src/pivot.rs): Tableau struct:

data:       Vec<f64>     // row-major flat array; (m+1) rows x (n+1) cols
                         // rows 0..m = constraint rows; row m = objective row
                         // cols 0..n = variable columns; col n = RHS
m:          usize        // constraint count
n:          usize        // variable count (including slacks/artificials)
basis:      Vec<usize>   // basis[i] = column index of basic variable in row i; len = m

Layout (row-major data[i * (n+1) + j]):

           x0  x1 ... xn-1  | RHS
  row 0:  a00 a01 ... a0n-1 | b0       <- constraint rows
  ...
  row m-1:
  row m:   c0  c1 ...  cn-1 | -z       <- objective row (reduced costs; RHS = negative obj value)

Tableau::from_standard_form(sf: &StandardForm) -> Tableau:

  • Copy A_std into constraint rows dense; copy b into RHS column.
  • Copy negated c into objective row (convention: maximizing -z).
  • Set basis[i] to the slack/artificial column for row i (initial basis from Phase 2).
  • Set non-basic reduced costs from c (structural cols); basic reduced costs = 0.

pivot(row: usize, col: usize) -> Result<(), PivotError>:

  1. Read pivot element p = data[row][col].
  2. If |p| < PIVOT_TOL return Err(NumericallyZero).
  3. Scale entire pivot row by 1/p.
  4. For every other row r != row (including objective row): factor = data[r][col]; skip if |factor| < PIVOT_TOL; else data[r][*] -= factor * data[row][*].
  5. Set basis[row] = col.
  6. data[row][col] should now equal 1.0; data[r != row][col] should equal 0.0.

reduced_cost(col) returns data[m][col]; rhs(row) returns data[row][n]; objective_value() returns -data[m][n] (negate stored -z). PivotError: enum { NumericallyZero, RowOutOfRange, ColOutOfRange }. Tableau::check_basis_consistency(&self, tol: f64) -> bool: for each basis[i] = col, verify data[i][col] ≈ 1.0, data[r!=i][col] ≈ 0.0; asserts identity structure in basis columns.

3.3 Deliverable

  • lp-tableau compiles; from_standard_form initializes correctly for any StandardForm.
  • pivot transforms tableau correctly on hand-verified 2×3 and 3×5 examples.
  • check_basis_consistency passes after every pivot in tests.
  • cargo test -p lp-tableau passes; test suite includes at least 5 different LP instances.

3.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Pass condition
Pivot column becomes unit after pivot(r,c): data[r][c]==1.0, all others==0.0 (within PIVOT_TOL)
RHS non-negative after valid pivot basis solution is non-negative (feasibility maintained)
Objective row updated reduced_cost(c) == 0.0 after pivoting column c in
PivotError on near-zero pivot returns Err(NumericallyZero) when `
check_basis_consistency after n pivots passes for hand-traced examples (Chvatal §2 examples)
Objective value matches manual 2-var LP objective after 2 pivots == known optimal (e.g., Chvatal LP1)

3.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected
fixtures/phase3/chvatal_lp1.json Chvatal textbook LP1 objective = 5.0 after two pivots
fixtures/phase3/degenerate_2x3.json degenerate step (zero step size) pivot succeeds; rhs[i] unchanged
fixtures/phase3/near_zero_pivot.json pivot element 1e-9 Err(NumericallyZero)
fixtures/phase3/all_eq.json equality constraints only, artificials in basis initial basis set correctly
fixtures/phase3/single_var.json 1×1 system pivot produces trivial solution
fixtures/phase3/basis_consistency_sequence.json 10 sequential pivots check_basis_consistency passes after each

3.6 Pitfalls

  • Pivot element magnitude threshold: exact-zero check misses near-zero pivots that corrupt accuracy. Use |p| < PIVOT_TOL (1e-7); most common numerical failure in the tableau.
  • Row elimination includes objective row: common bug is iterating 0..m instead of 0..=m; row m (objective) must also be eliminated.
  • Sign convention for objective row: storing -z vs +z creates sign confusion in reduced_cost/objective_value; commit to one convention (store -z) and document it.
  • Basis tracking after pivot: forgetting basis[row] = col means later phases query the wrong column for basic variable identity.
  • Row-major access pattern: scanning a full row is cache-friendly; looping over all rows for one column causes cache misses on large tableaux — acceptable here (reference only) but document O(m·n) cost per pivot.
  • Integer overflow in flat index: i * (n+1) + j should use usize arithmetic; assert m * (n+1) <= usize::MAX at construction.

3.7 Performance Concerns

  • Dense tableau is O(m×n) per pivot; acceptable only for small problems (m, n ≤ 500) and testing.
  • Flat row-major Vec<f64> avoids indirection; row operations are a single contiguous memory range.
  • SIMD auto-vectorization of the inner data[r][*] -= factor * data[row][*] loop: no pointer aliasing; use split_at_mut correctly.
  • Memory: (m+1) * (n+1) * 8 bytes; m=n=500 is ~2 MB (fine); m=n=5000 is ~200 MB (dense tableau not used at that scale).
  • Allocation is a single vec![0.0; (m+1)*(n+1)] at construction; no per-pivot heap activity.

3.8 Edge Cases

  • Pivot element exactly equal to PIVOT_TOL: boundary case; use strict < (not <=) to allow marginal pivots through with a warning log.
  • All reduced costs non-negative before first pivot: tableau immediately optimal; pivot never called; check_basis_consistency still needed on initial basis.
  • m = 0: empty problem; no constraint rows; return immediately optimal with objective = 0.
  • n = m: square tableau (no slack variables), valid after Phase 2 adds columns; still a valid state.
  • Large coefficient magnitudes (e.g., 1e12): reduced-cost computation accumulates float error; check_basis_consistency tolerance should scale with ||c||.
  • RHS column exactly zero (degenerate BFS): pivot with zero step; basis changes without changing solution; test objective_value unchanged.

3.9 Failure Analysis

  • Accumulated round-off: after many pivots, basis columns drift from identity; check_basis_consistency fails.
  • Detection: run check_basis_consistency in debug builds after every pivot; in release, every 50 pivots.
  • Blast radius: corrupted basis rows contaminate the ratio test; incorrect leaving variable; invalid solution.
  • Recovery: call refactor_from_scratch() — re-extract basis matrix columns from A_std, re-form tableau by Gaussian elimination; O(m²n) but correctness-restoring (mirrors lp-lu refactorization).
  • Operator visibility: log WARN with n_pivots, max_basis_error when check_basis_consistency fails; surface in CLI as NumericalWarning.

3.10 Operational Notes

  • lp-tableau is gated by cfg(feature = "dense"); production binaries may exclude it to reduce binary size.
  • The differential test harness (Phase 14) runs lp-tableau and lp-revised on the same problem and asserts |obj_dense - obj_revised| < 1e-6.
  • tracing::trace! emits (row, col, pivot_val) per pivot call; enable with RUST_LOG=lp_tableau=trace for step-by-step debugging.
  • Tableau derives Clone to support snapshotting before a speculative pivot (used in testing, not production).
  • Benchmark target: lp-tableau pivot on m=n=200 dense tableau < 1 ms per pivot on reference hardware (sanity check, not a production SLA).

Phase 4 — Primal Simplex Phase II

4.1 Objectives

  • Implement the Phase II primal simplex loop in lp-simplex, consuming a feasible Basis and StandardForm. Entering variable: most-negative reduced cost (Dantzig rule) via lp-pricing::dantzig_entering; leaving variable: minimum-ratio test on the pivot column (positive entries only). Termination: all reduced costs >= -OPT_TOL (optimality) or no positive pivot-column entry (unboundedness).
  • Maintain I2 (primal feasibility) after every pivot; verify I4 (optimality) and I5 (strong duality c.x == b.y) at termination. Dense-tableau path via lp-tableau (revised path wired in Phase 9 — Phase 4 targets tableau path exclusively); Solution reconstruction extracts x/duals_y/reduced_costs from the final tableau.

4.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Anchors: I2 (primal feasibility maintained after every pivot); I4 (optimality condition checked at loop exit); I5 (strong duality verified on solution reconstruction); P1 (certified optimality — KKT-verified before returning Optimal).

Module: lp-simplex/src/phase2.rs. Entry signature:

pub fn phase2(tableau: &mut Tableau, basis: &mut Basis, opts: &SolverOpts) -> SolverResult;
// contract: tableau holds a primal-feasible basis on entry; returns Status + Certificate

Main loop structure:

Step Detail
Reduced-cost check call lp-pricing::dantzig_entering(tableau.obj_row(), opts.opt_tol) → Option<usize>
None → optimal reconstruct Solution, verify KKT, return Status::Optimal
Compute pivot column tableau.col(j) — the B^-1 a_j column in the dense tableau
Ratio test min { rhs[i] / col[i] : col[i] > opts.pivot_tol } over all basic rows i
No positive entries all col[i] <= pivot_tol → unbounded; build Certificate::PrimalRay
Ties in ratio test break by smallest basis.basic[i] index (Bland tiebreak) when opts.anti_cycle enabled
Execute pivot lp-tableau::pivot(tableau, entering, leaving_row) — updates all rows including objective
Basis update basis.basic[leaving_row] = entering; update basis.status for entering and leaving vars
Iteration cap if iter >= opts.max_iterations { return Status::IterationLimit }
Degeneracy detection if step.abs() < OBJ_STALL_TOL { stall_count += 1 } else { stall_count = 0 }
Anti-cycle trigger if stall_count >= opts.stall_window { active_rule = PivotRule::Bland }
Instrumentation emit Event::Pivot { iter, entering, leaving_row, step, obj_value } per pivot
SolverOpts fields consumed by Phase II:
Field Type
--- ---
max_iterations usize
opt_tol f64
pivot_tol f64
price_rule PivotRule
stall_window usize
obj_stall_tol f64
lp-tableau interface used in this phase: 
fn col(&self, j: usize) -> &[f64];             // pivot column view — B^-1 a_j
fn obj_row(&self) -> &[f64];                   // reduced-cost row
fn rhs(&self) -> &[f64];                       // current basic-variable values x_B
fn obj_value(&self) -> f64;                    // current objective value
fn pivot(&mut self, entering: usize, leaving_row: usize); // full O(m*n) update
fn recompute_obj_row(&mut self, basis: &Basis, c: &[f64]); // periodic drift correction

Solution reconstruction from final tableau:

// extract x: structural var values from rhs + basis mapping
// extract duals_y: final obj row coefficients for slack columns (Phase 3 convention)
// extract reduced_costs: final obj row for all columns
// verify: |c.x - b.y| < STRONG_DUAL_TOL

Primal ray construction (unbounded case): direction d: d[entering] = 1, d[basic[i]] = -col[i] for all rows i; verify A·d <= 0 and c·d < 0; return Certificate::PrimalRay(d). SolverResult type:

pub struct SolverResult {
    pub status: Status,
    pub solution: Option<Solution>,
    pub certificate: Option<Certificate>,
    pub iterations: usize,
    pub events: Vec<Event>,
}

4.3 Deliverable

  • phase2 compiles, links against lp-tableau and lp-pricing, passes all unit tests; solves LPs up to 500 variables / 200 constraints in the dense-tableau path. iter count logged and accessible via SolverResult.iterations.
  • Returns Solution { status: Optimal, x, duals_y, reduced_costs, objective } populated from the final tableau; unbounded case returns Certificate::PrimalRay(d) with c·d < -1e-6 and A·d <= 1e-8 (verified in test harness).

4.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
Trivial LP max c.x s.t. Ax<=b optimal x matches reference within 1e-9
Unbounded LP Status::Unbounded + valid PrimalRay certificate
Primal feasibility at optimum `
Reduced costs at optimum all >= -1e-8
KKT complementary slackness `
Strong duality `
Iteration count <= reference solver (Dantzig, same tolerance)
Iteration limit Status::IterationLimit, no panic, events non-empty
Degenerate pivot zero step handled without negative rhs entry
Objective row drift periodic recompute prevents optimality miss on 10k-iteration problems

4.5 Conformance Fixtures

fixtures/phase2/lp_equalities.lp equality constraints with presupplied basis obj matches reference

4.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root Cause Debug Signal Recovery
Pivot column all <= PIVOT_TOL but positive entries exist below threshold missed unboundedness; wrong optimal claimed PIVOT_TOL too large log max(col) before ratio test lower PIVOT_TOL; add explicit max_positive check separate from ratio-test guard
Reduced-cost row drift after many pivots optimality declared prematurely; negative reduced costs remain floating-point accumulation across O(m·n) pivot updates log min(obj_row) at termination; compare to recomputed value call recompute_obj_row every RECOMPUTE_INTERVAL (default 50) pivots
Degenerate pivot loop without anti-cycling nontermination on degenerate problems zero ratio-test steps; repeated basis iteration counter stagnant relative to objective enable Bland rule (Phase 6); stall detector switches automatically
RHS becoming slightly negative after pivot false infeasibility detection in next iteration near-zero pivot element causing cancellation negative rhs entries in event log skip pivots with col[i] < PIVOT_TOL; refactorize in revised path
leaving_row and basis index off-by-one corrupted basis; wrong pivot confusion between row index and variable index assert basis.basic[leaving_row] == entering_prev_nonbasic add debug_assert! in pivot path
Maximization not negated before Phase II objective improves in wrong direction Sense::Maximize passed without normalization c.x decreasing on "maximize" run assert sense == Minimize at Phase II entry; normalization in lp-model
Primal ray direction not verified incorrect certificate returned sign error in d[basic[i]] construction A·d positive in test verify A·d <= 0 and c·d < 0 in debug_assert! block

4.7 Performance Concerns

  • Dense tableau pivot: O(m·n) per iteration (m=200, n=500, 1000 pivots ≈ 1e8 flops — acceptable, replaced by revised simplex at scale, Phase 9). Objective row update: O(n) per pivot, separate cache-aligned Vec<f64>, not interleaved with tableau rows. Pivot column stored as a view in a column-major tableau; sequential read.
  • Ratio test: single-pass O(m) scan, no allocation, stack-local accumulator. Pre-allocated scratch buffer Vec<f64> (len max(m,n)) reused across pivots; recompute_obj_row every 50 pivots costs O(m·n), amortized O(m) per pivot — negligible.

4.8 Edge Cases

  • All variables at lower bound at entry: basis feasible, loop exits on first optimality check. m = 0 after presolve: no constraints; optimum unbounded (if c has negative component with free var) or 0; handle before entering Phase II.
  • All objective coefficients zero: trivially feasible-optimal at any BFS, exits immediately. Objective +Inf/NaN after pivot: detect via is_finite() on obj_value(); return Status::Numerical.
  • n_artificial > 0 at Phase II entry: programming error; debug_assert!(basis.basic.iter().all(|&v| v < n_struct + n_slack)). Entering variable index equals a basic variable: degenerate pivot, step = 0, stall counter increments. max_iterations = 0: immediately return Status::IterationLimit without entering loop.

4.9 Failure Analysis

  • Wrong optimum reported: reduced-cost row not updated after pivot; detect via KKT check in SolverResult verification (blast radius: downstream sensitivity analysis); recovery: recompute_obj_row. Silent unboundedness: returns Optimal for an unbounded LP, certificate check passes then primal-ray test c·d < 0 fails; postmortem: inspect PIVOT_TOL and max positive entry in pivot column log.
  • Panic on empty basis vector: rhs/basis.basic index OOB when m = 0; detect via zero-constraint integration test; fix: early-exit guard before loop. Numerical Status for solvable LP: pivot_tol too aggressive; detect via Netlib fixture regression; recovery: increase pivot_tol to 1e-7.

4.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: simplex.phase2.iterations (counter), simplex.phase2.obj_value (gauge), simplex.phase2.recompute_count (counter, objective-row recomputes).
  • DEBUG log per pivot: "iter={} entering={} leaving_row={} step={:.6e} obj={:.10}". INFO at termination: "Phase II: status={:?} iters={} obj={:.8}". WARN at stall_count >= stall_window / 2.
  • No filesystem I/O; solver is pure in-memory; Event stream is optional/zero-cost when disabled, serializable to JSONL for replay / differential testing against reference solvers.

Phase 5 — Phase I Feasibility (Two-Phase)

5.1 Objectives

  • Implement two-phase feasibility in lp-simplex for LPs that cannot start with an obvious feasible basis. Phase I: construct an auxiliary LP minimizing the sum of all artificial variables; solve via the Phase II loop.
  • Phase I optimum = 0 (within FEAS_TOL): all artificials driven to zero; extract a feasible basis. Phase I optimum > FEAS_TOL: original LP is infeasible; emit Status::Infeasible with Certificate::DualRay.
  • Remove artificial columns from the basis representation before handing off to Phase II. Invariant I10: standard-form equivalence — Phase I does not change the optimum of the original LP. Invariant I9: the feasible basis handed to Phase II produces the same optimal objective as a reference solve.
  • Document Big-M as an alternative in lp-simplex/src/bigm.rs behind #[cfg(feature = "bigm")]; it is never the default path.

5.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Anchors: P5 (every terminal status carries a certificate — Certificate::DualRay on infeasible); I8 (certificate validity — Farkas dual-ray conditions); I10 (standard-form equivalence preserved through Phase I).

Module: lp-simplex/src/phase1.rs. Entry signature:

pub fn phase1(sf: &StandardForm, opts: &SolverOpts) -> Phase1Result;
// contract: constructs aux problem, solves it, returns feasible Basis or Infeasible certificate

Phase1Result type:

pub struct Phase1Result {
    pub status: Status,                   // Optimal (feasible found) or Infeasible
    pub basis: Option<Basis>,             // populated iff status == Optimal
    pub certificate: Option<Certificate>, // DualRay iff Infeasible
    pub phase1_obj: f64,                  // final auxiliary objective value
    pub iters: usize,
}

AuxProblem construction:

Step Detail
Row sign normalization for each row i where b[i] < 0: multiply row i of A_std and b[i] by -1; ensures initial artificial is feasible
Artificial injection for each Ge and Eq row (and any Le row whose slack is ineligible): add column e_i (unit vector) as artificial; record index in artificial_cols: Vec<usize>
c_aux construction c_aux[j] = 1.0 if j in artificial_cols, else 0.0; sense = Minimize
Initial basis one artificial per row: basic[i] = artificial_cols[i]; all start at b[i] >= 0 (after sign flip)
Tableau construction Tableau::from_standard_form(&aux_sf, &c_aux, &initial_basis) — same as Phase 3 builder
Phase II call phase2(&mut aux_tableau, &mut aux_basis, &aux_opts) with own iteration cap
AuxProblem struct: 
struct AuxProblem {
    sf: StandardForm,            // extended with artificial columns
    artificial_cols: Vec<usize>, // indices into extended sf.A columns
    original_n: usize,           // column count before artificials
}

Post-Phase-I processing:

Condition Action
phase1_obj > FEAS_TOL infeasible; extract dual ray from aux final obj row; return
Artificial basic at zero (degenerate) attempt pivot: find structural column j with nonzero entry in that row; if found, pivot it in; else row is redundant — drop
All artificials nonbasic clean: basis.basic contains no artificial index
Index remapping map basis.basic indices from aux column space back to original sf column space via var_map
Phase II launch phase2_with_basis(original_tableau, clean_basis, opts)
Infeasibility certificate (Farkas witness): dual ray y = the final dual variables of the Phase I auxiliary problem; properties A^T y >= 0, b^T y < 0; extracted from Phase I final tableau objective row (y[i] = coefficient of slack for row i in the aux objective row); verified via debug_assert! in certificate validation module.
Big-M alternative (documented, not default): penalize artificials with a large coefficient M in the original objective — c_bigm[j] = c[j] + M for j in artificial_cols (Minimize sense). Risk: numerical instability when M is large relative to c; Bland/Devex pricing may select wrong entering variable. Implementation stub in lp-simplex/src/bigm.rs, activated only with feature = "bigm". Test: verify Big-M on infeasible_simple.lp produces same result as two-phase; document where it fails.
End-to-end dispatch in lp-simplex/src/solver.rs: 
pub fn solve(problem: &LpProblem, opts: &SolverOpts) -> SolverResult;
// dispatch: presolve → standard_form → phase1 → phase2 → postsolve → sensitivity

5.3 Deliverable

  • phase1 function compiles and links; Phase1Result type stable. Infeasible LPs return Status::Infeasible with a verifiable Certificate::DualRay.
  • Feasible LPs return a clean Basis (no artificials) that Phase II can accept directly. solve(problem, opts) wires Phase 1 → Phase 2 automatically without caller intervention.
  • End-to-end: LP read from file → solve → SolverResult with correct status and objective.

5.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
Feasible LP: Phase I auxiliary optimum phase1_obj <= FEAS_TOL = 1e-8
Feasible LP: no artificial in returned basis `assert!(basis.basic.iter().all(
Infeasible LP: status Status::Infeasible
Dual ray: Farkas condition A^T y >= -1e-8 elementwise; b^T y < -1e-8
Phase II after Phase I correct optimum within 1e-9 vs reference

b-negative row sign-normalized before artificial injection; initial aux basis feasible

5.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root Cause Debug Signal Recovery
Forgetting b-sign normalization when b[i] < 0 artificial starts with negative value; Phase I infeasible from iter 0 missing sign flip log initial rhs values of aux tableau; check any < 0 enforce b_aux[i] >= 0 invariant with debug_assert! after normalization
Artificial remains basic at nonzero at Phase I termination false feasibility; Phase II drifts Phase I hit iteration limit phase1_obj > FEAS_TOL not checked; check always, not only on normal exit separate termination-type check: if phase1_obj > FEAS_TOL → infeasible regardless of exit reason
Index confusion: aux vs original column numbering wrong pivot in Phase II; corrupted basis var_map not applied on basis extraction assertion: basis.basic max < original_n add remapping step; debug_assert! on all returned basis indices
Artificial column retained in Phase II tableau spurious entering variable from artificial column; wrong reduced cost cleanup step omitted watch for entering ∈ artificial_cols in Phase II event log assert no artificial column has negative reduced cost in Phase II
Dual ray extracted as zero vector invalid certificate degenerate Phase I final basis; all aux duals zero ` y

Redundant row removal changes row count row/column index mismatch in subsequent pivots row indices not renumbered after drop crash on out-of-bounds access rebuild tableau from scratch after redundant-row removal

5.7 Performance Concerns

  • Phase I adds n_artificial <= m extra columns: dense tableau grows to (m) × (n + m). Phase I pivot count is empirically comparable to Phase II pivot count; total solve is ~2× Phase-II-alone.
  • Artificial column deletion avoids shifting existing columns (no O(m·n) copy); uses a column-active bitmask, Phase II pricing skips inactive columns. Auxiliary objective row is sparse — nonzero only at artificial_cols; initial pricing is O(n_artificial) not O(n).
  • b-sign normalization: O(m·n) row operation, done once, unavoidable. Redundant-row pivot attempt: O(n) scan per stuck artificial; typically O(1) artificials stuck in degenerate cases.

5.8 Edge Cases

  • All Le constraints with b >= 0: slacks form an identity basis; n_artificial = 0; Phase I entirely skipped. LP feasible but highly degenerate: Phase I reaches zero with some artificials still basic at zero; cleanup pivots required.
  • Phase I and Phase II both hit iteration limits: return Status::IterationLimit; no false infeasibility. m = 0 after presolve: no rows, no artificials needed; skip to Phase II or return trivially feasible.
  • Mixed relation types on a single problem: each row independently determines whether a slack, surplus+artificial, or standalone artificial is needed. Phase I optimum exactly FEAS_TOL: boundary case; treat as feasible (use <= not <); log warning.
  • Infeasible and unbounded simultaneously: not possible for a valid LP; if Phase I is infeasible, stop; do not check unboundedness. All artificials at exactly zero by construction (b=0 rows): degenerate initial solution; still valid; no Phase I pivots needed.

5.9 Failure Analysis

  • False feasibility: phase1_obj tolerance too loose; detect via primal feasibility check after Phase I; recovery: tighten FEAS_TOL to 1e-10, rerun Phase I. False infeasibility: b-normalization skipped; detect via feasible_equality.lp fixture returning infeasible; postmortem: verify rhs signs in AuxProblem log, check normalization applied before artificial injection.
  • Missing certificate: Certificate::DualRay not constructed; detect via certificate field None on infeasible result in test; fix: extract dual row from Phase I final objective-row coefficients. Wrong Phase II objective after Phase I: artificial column not cleaned from basis; detect via Phase II optimal not matching reference; postmortem: log basis.basic at Phase I exit, confirm no index >= original_n.

5.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: simplex.phase1.n_artificials (gauge, artificial column count injected), simplex.phase1.iterations (counter), simplex.phase1.infeasible_count (counter), simplex.phase1.redundant_rows_dropped (counter, rows removed during artificial cleanup).
  • INFO log at Phase I termination: "Phase I: aux_obj={:.2e} iters={} status={:?}". DEBUG log: auxiliary tableau initial rhs values (first 10 entries) for row-sign-normalization audit.
  • Phase I events tagged phase=1 in the shared Event stream; Phase II events tagged phase=2.
  • Big-M feature flag: cargo build --features bigm; never enabled in release profile.

Phase 6 — Anti-Cycling

6.1 Objectives

  • Guarantee finite termination (invariant I7) of the primal simplex under all degeneracy patterns. Primary mechanism: Bland's rule in lp-pricing — smallest-column-index entering variable, smallest-basic-variable-index leaving variable. Secondary mechanism: lexicographic / perturbation method as a faster-in-practice alternative implemented in lp-pricing.
  • Auto-switch: after STALL_WINDOW consecutive degenerate pivots, switch from Dantzig to Bland automatically (PivotRule::AutoBland). Cycling is a correctness failure (not a performance issue): it must never occur in a production run. Both mechanisms pluggable via PivotRule enum; selection controlled by SolverOpts::price_rule. Stall detection must have zero overhead on non-degenerate problems.

6.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Anchors: P3 (guaranteed termination via automatic Bland fallback); I7 (finite termination enforced by stall detection plus Bland/lexicographic rules).

Module: lp-pricing/src/lib.rs — extends Phase 4 pricing. Additional modules: lp-pricing/src/bland.rs, lp-pricing/src/lexicographic.rs, lp-pricing/src/perturbation.rs. Bland's rule entering (in bland.rs):

pub fn bland_entering(obj_row: &[f64], opt_tol: f64) -> Option<usize>;
// contract: return Some(j) where j = min { j : obj_row[j] < -opt_tol }; None iff all >= -opt_tol

Bland's rule leaving (in bland.rs):

pub fn bland_leaving(col: &[f64], rhs: &[f64], basis: &[usize], pivot_tol: f64) -> RatioTestResult;
// contract: compute ratio test; among tied minimum-ratio rows, return argmin { basis.basic[i] : tied }
// RatioTestResult.leaving = Some(row_index) or None (unbounded)

Lexicographic ratio test (in lexicographic.rs):

Step Detail
Primary ratio r_0 = min { rhs[i] / col[i] : col[i] > pivot_tol }
Tie set T_0 = { i : col[i] > pivot_tol AND rhs[i]/col[i] <= r_0 + RATIO_TIE_TOL }
` T_0
Secondary ratio for k = 0, 1, ..., n_struct-1: compute r_k = min { tableau[i, basis_initial_col_k] / col[i] : i in T_{k-1} }
Reduce tie set T_k = { i in T_{k-1} : row_k_ratio[i] <= r_k + RATIO_TIE_TOL }
Termination first k where `
Theoretical guarantee the initial identity block of columns from the original basis yields unique tie-breaking in finite steps (Chvatal Theorem 4.2)
Signature: pub fn lex_ratio_test(col: &[f64], tableau: &Tableau, pivot_tol: f64) -> RatioTestResult;
Perturbation method (in perturbation.rs): 
pub fn perturb_rhs(rhs: &mut [f64], epsilon: f64);
// contract: rhs[i] += epsilon^(i+1); breaks all ties deterministically; epsilon default: 1e-7

Applied once before Phase II; removed from solution at termination by recovering original rhs from unperturbed StandardForm. Not applied by default; activated by SolverOpts::anti_cycle = AntiCycleMode::Perturbation. PivotRule enum (extended from Phase 4):

pub enum PivotRule {
    Dantzig,                          // Phase 4 default; fast, may cycle
    Bland,                            // guaranteed termination; slower
    Devex,                            // Phase 13 steepest-edge approximation
    AutoBland { window: usize },      // Dantzig with automatic Bland fallback
}

AntiCycleMode enum (in SolverOpts):

pub enum AntiCycleMode {
    Bland,                            // always Bland for leaving
    Lexicographic,                    // lex ratio test
    Perturbation { epsilon: f64 },    // perturb rhs
    Auto,                             // stall detection → Bland switch
    None,                             // disabled (benchmark/test only)
}

Stall detection and rule switching (in lp-simplex/src/phase2.rs):

Variable Type Initial Update
stall_count usize 0 += 1 if `
active_rule PivotRule opts.price_rule set to Bland when stall_count >= stall_window
progress_count usize 0 += 1 if improving; reset active_rule to original after PROGRESS_WINDOW improving pivots
Event emitted Event::RuleSwitch — logged when active_rule changes
Basis-hash dedup (debug assertions only): 
#[cfg(debug_assertions)]
let mut seen_bases: HashSet<Vec<usize>> = HashSet::new();
// each pivot: insert canonical(basis.basic); panic if already seen: "CYCLING DETECTED: basis repeated at iter {iter}"

Hash is over sorted basis.basic (canonical form); used only in test builds.

6.3 Deliverable

  • lp-pricing exports bland_entering, bland_leaving, lex_ratio_test, perturb_rhs; PivotRule::Bland and PivotRule::AutoBland functional in Phase II loop. Beale's 1955 cycling LP terminates correctly under Bland and AutoBland; Kuhn's 1956 degeneracy LP terminates correctly under Bland and Lexicographic.
  • Stall detection triggers at most once on standard Netlib test problems; Event::RuleSwitch emitted. Basis-hash dedup in debug mode; panics on any detected cycle in test suite.

6.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
Beale cycling LP: PivotRule::Bland terminates <= 30 pivots; correct objective
Beale cycling LP: PivotRule::AutoBland terminates; Event::RuleSwitch emitted; correct obj
Kuhn degenerate LP: PivotRule::Bland terminates; correct obj
Lex rule: Chvatal degenerate Example 4.3 terminates; `
Bland entering contract j = argmin { j : obj_row[j] < -opt_tol } — unit-tested with hand-crafted obj_row
Bland leaving contract leaving = argmin { basis.basic[i] : i in tied_ratio_rows } — unit-tested with hand-crafted tie
Non-degenerate LP Dantzig == AutoBland pivot count; no RuleSwitch event emitted
stall_window = 0 AutoBland behaves as pure Dantzig; no switch ever
Perturbation: feasible_equality.lp same optimum as unperturbed

6.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Purpose Expected

fixtures/anticycle/kuhn_degenerate.lp Kuhn 1956 degenerate LP terminates; obj matches reference; no cycle detected
