fixtures/anticycle/klee_minty_n5.lp Klee-Minty cube n=5 (32 vertices) Dantzig takes 2^5 pivots; Bland terminates in fewer (document count)

6.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root Cause Debug Signal Recovery
Bland leaving uses row index i instead of basis.basic[i] cycling still possible despite Bland confusing row index with variable index compare basis.basic[i] not i in tie-break fix: leaving = argmin_i { basis.basic[i] : i in tied }
AutoBland switches to Bland but obj_stall_tol too large stall never detected; Dantzig cycles threshold misconfigured simplex.stall_count gauge never approaches stall_window decrease obj_stall_tol to 1e-12
Perturbation epsilon too large perturbed solution violates primal feasibility tolerance epsilon not small enough post-solve ` Ax-b
Lexicographic tie-breaking uses wrong identity columns infinite tie-breaking loop incorrect column selection for secondary ratios ` T_k
Switching back from Bland to Dantzig re-triggers stall oscillating rule switches progress_count threshold too low Event::RuleSwitch appears repeatedly in log only switch back after PROGRESS_WINDOW = 50 consecutive improving pivots
Bland's entering scan scans deactivated columns wrong entering variable if inactive (artificial) columns still indexed inactive columns not excluded from obj_row artificial entering variable chosen mask inactive columns in obj_row before pricing scan
Basis-hash dedup causes false cycle detection panic in non-cycling degenerate problem different degenerate paths revisiting same basis via legitimate zero steps disable dedup; re-enable with debug_assertions dedup is only for test/debug; never panic in release

6.7 Performance Concerns

  • Bland's entering scan: O(n) — same as Dantzig but no magnitude comparison; marginally cheaper per call, similar cache footprint. Bland's leaving with tie-breaking: O(m log m) worst case when all rows tied (fully degenerate basis); typical case O(m) since few ties. Lex ratio test: O(m · k) per call where k = tie-breaking columns needed (empirically 1–3); O(m) amortized.
  • Stall detection: one subtraction + comparison per pivot; zero allocation, O(1). Rule switch overhead: one enum variant check per pricing call, branch predicted correctly after first switch. Perturbation: O(m) one-time cost; zero per-pivot overhead. Basis-hash in debug mode: HashSet insert per pivot, O(m log m); acceptable in test builds, zero in release. Pre-allocated tie-list buffer of length m stored in Tableau; reused across ratio-test calls; no per-pivot allocation.

6.8 Edge Cases

  • Single-variable LP: no ties possible; Bland and Dantzig identical, anti-cycling irrelevant. All reduced costs exactly at -OPT_TOL: both Bland and Dantzig return the same entering variable. stall_window = usize::MAX: effectively disables auto-switch; pure selected rule.
  • Beale cycling LP run with PivotRule::Dantzig and no anti-cycling: capped at max_iterations, returns Status::IterationLimit (documented expected behavior for the cycling demo). Perturbation creates rhs b + delta where delta[i] = epsilon^(i+1); epsilon = 1e-7, m = 200 → delta[199] = (1e-7)^200 ≈ 0, numerically identical to unperturbed for large i; still sufficient for low-dimension degeneracy.
  • Lexicographic rule on a degenerate LP with no identity block available (all artificial basis): fall back to Bland. Anti-cycle mode None: allowed only in #[cfg(test)] or benchmarks; must not be the default in SolverOpts::default(). obj_stall_tol = 0.0: any non-improving pivot triggers the stall counter; overly aggressive, not recommended.

6.9 Failure Analysis

  • Cycling detected at runtime: basis-hash dedup in debug mode panics with "CYCLING DETECTED: basis repeated at iter {}"; in release (no debug assertions) cycling manifests as Status::IterationLimit. Prevention: always use AntiCycleMode::Auto in production; recovery: rerun with PivotRule::Bland. Wrong Bland leaving implementation: off-by-one or using row index instead of basis.basic[i]; detection: beale_cycling.lp fixture returns wrong objective or fails to terminate; postmortem: trace each pivot's leaving_row and basis.basic[leaving_row], compare against Chvatal Table 4.1 pivot sequence.
  • Stall false positive on converging LP: Bland engages on a legitimately slow-converging problem, suboptimal performance; detection: simplex.price_rule_switch counter elevated on non-degenerate Netlib problems; recovery: increase stall_window to 50. Perturbation residual in final solution: post-perturbation rhs not restored, solution x slightly infeasible; detection: |Ax - b|_inf > 1e-6 in post-solve check; recovery: restore original b before solution extraction (perturbation is internal to Phase II).

6.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: simplex.price_rule_switch (counter, incremented each AutoBland rule change), simplex.stall_count (gauge, current consecutive degenerate pivot count, reset on progress), simplex.bland_entering_calls, simplex.lex_ratio_test_calls (counters per pricing rule).
  • INFO log on rule switch: "Anti-cycling: switched to Bland at iter={iter} stall_count={stall_count}"; on switch-back: "Anti-cycling: resumed Dantzig after {progress_count} improving pivots". DEBUG log when Bland leaving breaks a tie: "Bland leaving: tie broken, row={row} basis_var={var}".
  • Basis-hash dedup: #[cfg(debug_assertions)] only; HashSet<Vec<usize>>; panic on repeat. Recommended production setting: PivotRule::AutoBland { window: 20 }; PivotRule::Bland for conformance testing; PivotRule::Dantzig only for benchmarking non-degenerate problems. Devex/steepest-edge pricing (Phase 13) is registered in PivotRule but not implemented in this phase; calling it returns Err(PricingError::NotYetImplemented).

Phase 7 — Degeneracy, Unboundedness, Infeasibility Certificates

7.1 Objectives

  • Detect and handle degenerate pivots (zero step length) without stalling or looping.
  • Emit a primal ray Certificate::PrimalRay(Vec<f64>) for every Status::Unbounded result.
  • Emit a dual ray Certificate::DualRay(Vec<f64>) or Phase I residual for every Status::Infeasible result.
  • Ensure every terminal Status is paired with a Certificate; bare status with no certificate is a compile-time type error.
  • Enforce invariants I7 (finite termination), I8 (certificate validity), P5 (every terminal status carries a certificate).

7.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Anchors: I7 (finite termination); I8 (certificate validity — primal/dual ray verification); P5 (every terminal status carries a certificate).

Degeneracy detection — lp-simplex:

  • After each ratio test, compare RatioTestResult::step to PIVOT_TOL (named constant); log degenerate count per solve.
  • Track consecutive degenerate pivot count in solver state; after BLAND_TRIGGER (default 50) consecutive degenerate pivots, switch PivotRule to Bland for the remainder of the phase.
  • Perturbation fallback: lp-pricing supports an epsilon_perturb(b: &mut Vec<f64>, seed: u64) function that adds lexicographic perturbation to RHS entries, reversible by unperturb.

Unboundedness detection — lp-simplex::phase_ii_loop:

  • In ratio test: if column d = B^{-1} a_j has all components <= 0 for a valid entering variable j, the LP is unbounded.
  • Construct primal ray: x_ray[j] = 1.0; for each basic variable i, x_ray[basic[i]] = -d[i]; all other nonbasic = 0.
  • Return (Status::Unbounded, Certificate::PrimalRay(x_ray)).

Infeasibility detection — lp-simplex::phase_i_loop:

  • Phase I optimum > FEAS_TOL → infeasibility confirmed.
  • Extract dual ray from Phase I dual variables: y_feas = c_feas_B * B^{-1}; scale so y_feas * b < 0.
  • Return (Status::Infeasible, Certificate::DualRay(y_feas)).
  • For the dense-tableau path, compute y directly from the objective row of the tableau.

Certificate types — lp-core:

pub enum Certificate {
    KktOptimal,
    PrimalRay(Vec<f64>),   // direction: c·d < 0, A·d <= 0 (for maximization: c·d > 0)
    DualRay(Vec<f64>),     // y: y·b < 0, y·A >= 0 (Farkas witness)
}

Certificate verifier — lp-core::verify:

  • verify_primal_ray(problem, ray) -> bool: checks c·ray < -EPS and A·ray <= EPS * ||A|| row-wise.
  • verify_dual_ray(problem, ray) -> bool: checks Farkas conditions.
  • verify_kkt(problem, sol) -> bool: checks primal feasibility, dual feasibility, complementary slackness — all within KKT_TOL.
  • Verifier is called automatically before returning any Solution; failure triggers Status::Numerical.

Solve return type — lp-simplex:

  • fn solve(problem: &StandardForm, opts: &SolverOpts) -> (Solution, Certificate) — the pair is always returned together; the type system prevents returning a bare Status.

7.3 Deliverable

  • lp-simplex returns (Solution, Certificate) for all exit paths.
  • Certificate::KktOptimal verified against KKT conditions before returning.
  • PrimalRay / DualRay checked by the verifier; any verification failure surfaces as Status::Numerical.
  • Anti-cycling guard: Bland's rule activates automatically after stall threshold.
  • Degenerate pivot counter exposed via SolverStats.

7.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Threshold
Unbounded test problems (Klee-Minty variants, manual) PrimalRay verified, c·ray < -1e-8
Infeasible test problems (contradictory constraints) DualRay verified, Farkas holds
Degenerate pivot trigger (Bland activation) activates within BLAND_TRIGGER degenerate pivots
KKT verification on Netlib optimal instances residual < 1e-8 for primal+dual+CS
Phase I residual > 0 → infeasible flag 100 % of crafted infeasible instances
Zero certificate returned must not compile (type enforced)

File Purpose Expected Output

fixtures/infeasible_contradictory.lp x >= 1, x <= 0 Infeasible, Farkas ray
fixtures/degenerate_beale.lp Beale's cycling example Optimal (no infinite loop)
fixtures/degenerate_bland.lp Bland anti-cycling stress Optimal, pivot count finite
fixtures/kkt_verify_netlib_*.sol Pre-solved Netlib solutions KKT residual < 1e-8
fixtures/infeasible_phase1_residual.lp Phase I leaves positive residual Infeasible + dual ray

7.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root Cause Debug Signal Recovery
Degenerate loop without Bland Solver hangs, pivot count unbounded Cycling through degenerate bases SolverStats::degenerate_pivots grows without objective progress Force Bland after BLAND_TRIGGER
Ray not actually improving c·ray >= 0 despite Unbounded flag Sign error in ray construction (max vs. min normalization) Verifier triggers Numerical Re-check normalization of sense before ray extraction
Dual ray fails Farkas y·b >= 0 Phase I dual variables extracted before basis is dual-feasible Verifier log; check Phase I exit condition Ensure Phase I terminates with dual variables current
KKT CS violation at optimum x_j * rc_j != 0 Numerical drift in reduced costs near zero verify_kkt complementary slackness residual Refactorize and recompute reduced costs
Premature infeasibility Feasible instance classified infeasible FEAS_TOL too tight; artificial variable pinned by degeneracy Phase I objective trace; tighten artificial handling Loosen FEAS_TOL or run perturbation

7.7 Performance Concerns

  • Certificate construction is O(n) — allocates one Vec<f64> per terminal call; acceptable overhead.
  • KKT verifier scans the full constraint matrix: O(nnz); cache-friendly over CSC layout.
  • Bland rule fallback increases pivot count on degenerate instances; acceptable since it is finite.
  • Perturbation epsilon_perturb touches the entire b vector: O(m); cheap.

7.8 Edge Cases

  • Problem with m = 0 (no constraints): all variables are free, LP is unbounded unless bounded by variable bounds — requires special-case before ratio test.
  • All variables fixed by bounds: LP may be trivially optimal; Phase II should exit before first pivot.
  • Phase I introduces artificials that cannot be driven to zero due to rank deficiency — detected by checking rank of the artificial basis at Phase I exit.
  • Certificate::PrimalRay ray all-zeros: impossible by construction; assert ||ray||_inf > EPS before returning.
  • Sense::Maximize sign convention: negate c before ray construction so ray improves the original objective.

7.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Recovery
Verifier rejects certificate Status::Numerical returned Single solve; caller retries with different options Refactorize, tighten tolerances, re-solve
Cycling despite Bland Pivot count > MAX_ITER → IterationLimit Single solve Report IterationLimit; user inspects degenerate structure
KKT residual > KKT_TOL at reported optimum verify_kkt fails Status::Numerical Trigger refactorization + re-verification pass

7.10 Operational Notes

  • SolverStats fields: degenerate_pivots: u32, bland_activations: u32, phase1_objective_final: f64, kkt_residual: f64.
  • Log level DEBUG: emit certificate type and verifier residuals at solve completion.
  • Log level TRACE: emit each degenerate pivot detection event.
  • Metric: lp_solver_certificate_type{type="kkt"|"primal_ray"|"dual_ray"|"numerical"} — count per solve invocation.
  • No deployment/migration concern: certificate types are additive; old callers ignoring the Certificate field are unaffected.

Phase 8 — Bounded-Variable Simplex

8.1 Objectives

  • Implement lp-bounds: handle finite lower/upper bounds on structural variables natively in the simplex ratio test — no additional constraint rows.
  • Support VarStatus::NonbasicLower, VarStatus::NonbasicUpper, VarStatus::Basic, VarStatus::Free for each variable at each iteration.
  • Handle bound flips: an entering variable may hit its opposite bound before any basic variable hits a bound — execute a bound flip rather than a basis change.
  • Enforce invariant I6 (ratio-test correctness extended to bound-constrained variables) and P9 (bounded variables are native).
  • Compatible with both dense-tableau (lp-tableau) and revised (lp-revised) paths.

8.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Variable status book-keeping — lp-bounds:

  • BoundedBasis wraps Basis and extends VarStatus tracking: each non-basic var at either lower or upper.
  • fn shift_to_lower(var: VarId, basis: &mut BoundedBasis) / shift_to_upper: move nonbasic var to opposite bound.
  • Initial basis construction: nonbasic structural vars assigned to their lower bound (or upper if only upper exists).

Modified ratio test — lp-bounds::ratio_test_bounded:

  • Inputs: column d = B^{-1} a_j, entering var j moving from lower→upper (delta_max = u_j - l_j), basic variable bounds.
  • Compute minimum ratio among basic variables: theta_b = min_i { (x_i - l_i) / d_i for d_i > 0 } union { (u_i - x_i) / (-d_i) for d_i < 0 }.
  • Bound flip check: if delta_max < theta_b, the entering var hits its upper bound; no basis change — flip status to NonbasicUpper, add delta_max contribution to objective and solution.
  • Otherwise: standard pivot, leaving = argmin of ratio test; update solution x_B -= d * theta_b.
  • Return RatioTestResult { leaving: Option<usize>, step: f64, bound_flip: bool }.

Reduced cost sign convention — lp-bounds:

  • Var at lower bound: enters if rc_j < -OPT_TOL (improving direction: increase from lower).
  • Var at upper bound: enters if rc_j > OPT_TOL (improving direction: decrease from upper).
  • Free nonbasic: enters if |rc_j| > OPT_TOL (use sign to determine direction).
  • Optimality: all nonbasic vars satisfy sign condition; invariant I4 holds.

Integration with dense tableau — lp-tableau::pivot_bounded:

  • Extended to accept entering direction (+1 or -1) and bound_flip flag.
  • On flip: update RHS column without a full pivot; flip var's status; recompute objective row contribution.

Integration with revised simplex — Phase 9 uses BoundedBasis:

  • FTRAN / BTRAN unchanged; ratio_test_bounded replaces standard ratio test.
  • On bound flip: no LU update needed (basis unchanged); only solution vector x_B updated.

Free variables — lp-bounds:

  • Split-free handling: structural free var x split into x = x+ - x-, x+, x- >= 0, or handled via bounded-var with l = -INF, u = +INF.
  • Recommend the split approach for the initial implementation; bounded-inf as Phase 9 stretch.

Postsolve — lp-model::postsolve_bounds:

  • After solving, recover original variable values from shifted variables: x_orig[i] = x_shifted[i] + l_i.
  • Map NonbasicLower → original lower bound, NonbasicUpper → upper bound in Solution.

8.3 Deliverable

  • lp-bounds crate compiles with bounded ratio test; dense and revised paths both use it.
  • Solves LPs with general Bound { lower, upper } per variable without adding constraint rows.
  • Bound flips execute correctly; no basis LU update on a flip.
  • Solution::x values lie within variable bounds within FEAS_TOL.
  • SolverStats::bound_flips: u32 counter populated.

8.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Threshold
All structural var values within bounds at optimum max(0, l_i - x_i, x_i - u_i) < FEAS_TOL = 1e-8
Bound flip executes without LU update verified by SolverStats::lu_updates unchanged on flip iteration
Problems with only upper-bounded vars solve correctly 10 crafted instances, match dense-tableau reference
Infeasible due to l > u caught at model build validation error before Phase I
Bound-constrained Netlib instances (e.g., ADLITTLE, AFIRO) objective within 1e-6 of reference
Basis size = number of structural + slack, not inflated assert `

8.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Purpose Expected Output

fixtures/bounds_upper_only.lp Vars with only upper bounds Optimal, correct objective
fixtures/bounds_flip_trigger.lp Entering var hits upper before any basic var leaves bound_flip=true in stats
fixtures/bounds_infeasible_lb_gt_ub.lp l=5, u=3 Build-time Err(InvalidBound)

fixtures/netlib_adlittle_bounds.mps Netlib ADLITTLE Objective 2.254949631e+02 ± 1e-4

8.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root Cause Debug Signal Recovery
Incorrect reduced cost sign for upper-bounded var Solver moves variable in wrong direction; objective deteriorates Sign convention not flipped for NonbasicUpper rc_j > 0 but var is at lower — check direction logic Audit rc_sign per VarStatus in pricing
Bound flip skips LU update but corrupts x_B Solution values drift from feasibility Step delta_max applied with wrong sign FEAS_TOL violation detected at next iteration Re-derive x_B += direction * delta_max * d sign carefully
Free variable split creates near-duplicate columns Ill-conditioned basis; LU refactorization triggers frequently x+ and x- both basic simultaneously LuFactorization::n_updates grows rapidly Enforce complementarity: at most one of x+, x- is basic
Ratio test ignores basic variable upper bound Basic var driven past upper bound Only lower-bound constraint used in ratio test numerator FEAS_TOL violation on x_B > u check Add (u_i - x_i)/d_i for d_i > 0 case to ratio test
Postsolve shift error x_orig outside original bounds Forgot to add back l_i after solving shifted problem Check x_orig[i] - l_i vs x_shifted[i] Verify postsolve with round-trip test

8.7 Performance Concerns

  • Bound flip: no LU solve needed; O(m) update to x_B — faster than a pivot.
  • Ratio test extended to check both lower and upper directions: doubles comparisons per column; still O(m) per pivot.
  • VarStatus array: one byte per variable; fits in L1 cache for problems with n < 32768.
  • Avoid re-computing delta_max = u_j - l_j inside the loop; cache in BoundedBasis.
  • Free-variable split doubles column count; avoid for problems with many free vars by using the bounded-inf approach in Phase 9.

8.8 Edge Cases

  • Bound::lower == Bound::upper (fixed variable): substitute before solving; remove from basis.
  • Bound::lower == -INF and Bound::upper == +INF: free variable — handled by split or special status.
  • Entering variable direction flip when current status is NonbasicUpper: direction is -1, not +1.
  • delta_max = 0 (var already at both bounds due to fixed): should be removed by presolve (Phase 12).
  • step = 0 on bound flip: degenerate flip — count in SolverStats::degenerate_bound_flips.
  • Overflow: u - l where u = +INF — guard with is_finite() check; treat as unbounded direction.

8.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Recovery
Bound infeasibility undetected Solution has x_i > u_i; KKT verifier rejects Status::Numerical Fix ratio test upper-bound case; add post-pivot assertion
Infinite bound flip loop Pivot count > MAX_ITER IterationLimit Check if degenerate flips are cycling; apply Bland to bound-flip candidates
Free-variable negative value lost x_orig truncated to 0 Wrong optimal Audit postsolve split-merge: x = x+ - x-

8.10 Operational Notes

  • SolverStats additions: bound_flips: u32, degenerate_bound_flips: u32, fixed_vars_removed: u32.
  • Log DEBUG: each bound flip — entering var index, delta_max, objective delta.
  • Metric: lp_solver_bound_flips_total — counter incremented per bound flip.
  • Compatibility: lp-bounds is purely additive; existing lp-simplex tests remain valid (all bounds [0, +INF] default).

Phase 9 — Revised Simplex (FTRAN/BTRAN)

9.1 Objectives

  • Implement lp-revised: replace the dense m x n tableau with an implicit basis representation.
  • Compute B^{-1} a_j (FTRAN) and y = c_B^T B^{-1} (BTRAN) on demand using lp-lu's LuFactorization.
  • Price reduced costs as rc_j = c_j - y^T a_j without materializing the full tableau row.
  • Update the basis representation (eta vectors or LU update) on each pivot.
  • Achieve memory O(nnz(A) + nnz(B)) vs O(m*n) for the dense path.
  • Enforce P4 (revised over dense at scale), P2 (numerical stability via LU), I1 (basis nonsingularity).

9.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Anchors: P4 (revised path replaces the dense tableau at scale); P2 (LU-based numerical stability); I1 (basis nonsingularity maintained via refactorization triggers); P12 (basis factorization freshness — refactorize on n_updates/condition/residual thresholds).

lp-revised crate structure:

  • RevisedSimplex { basis: Basis, lu: LuFactorization, problem: &StandardForm, stats: SolverStats }.
  • fn solve_revised(problem: &StandardForm, opts: &SolverOpts, initial_basis: Option<Basis>) -> (Solution, Certificate) — top-level entry.
  • Reuses lp-pricing (Dantzig/Bland/Devex), lp-bounds (ratio test), lp-simplex Phase I/II loop structure. BTRAN — lp-revised::btran:
  • Signature: fn btran(lu: &LuFactorization, c_B: &[f64]) -> Vec<f64>.
  • Contract: solve y^T B = c_B^T; equivalently B^T y = c_B.
  • Implementation: forward substitution on U^T, backward on L^T (triangular solves in reverse order), applying row/col permutations.
  • Result: dual variables / pricing vector y; also the shadow prices at optimum. FTRAN — lp-revised::ftran:
  • Signature: fn ftran(lu: &LuFactorization, a_j: &SparseColumn) -> Vec<f64>.
  • Contract: compute d = B^{-1} a_j; used in ratio test and basis update.
  • Implementation: forward solve L d' = P_r a_j, then backward solve U d = d', apply P_c.
  • Sparse input a_j (CSC column slice) — exploit sparsity in forward solve for speed. Reduced-cost pricing — lp-revised::price_nonbasic:
  • For each nonbasic column j: rc_j = c_j - y · a_j where a_j is the j-th CSC column of A_std.
  • y recomputed via BTRAN once per iteration (not per column).
  • Dantzig: select j* = argmin rc_j (for min, entering if rc_j < -OPT_TOL).
  • Partial pricing variant: price sqrt(n) columns per iteration, cycling through all; reduces BTRAN cost. Basis update on pivot — lp-revised:
  • After pivot (entering j, leaving r): call lp-lu::update_basis(lu, d, r) to append an eta vector (Forrest-Tomlin or Bartels-Golub update — see Phase 10).
  • Increment LuFactorization::n_updates.
  • Trigger refactorization if n_updates >= REFACTOR_THRESHOLD (default 100) or lu condition estimate exceeds COND_TOL. Warm-start path — lp-revised::from_basis:
  • Accept Option<Basis> from Phase 11 (dual simplex warm start) or external save.
  • If provided and LU is fresh, skip Phase I entirely and enter Phase II (or dual simplex) directly.
  • Validate basis feasibility before proceeding: if primal infeasible, run Phase I from the warm basis. Dense-path differential test — lp-revised::test_support:
  • fn assert_equiv_tableau(revised: &RevisedSimplex, tableau: &Tableau) — compares d vectors and reduced costs at identical pivot sequences; used in integration tests. Solution extraction — lp-revised:
  • x_B = B^{-1} b via FTRAN with RHS b.
  • Nonbasic values from VarStatus (lower or upper bounds).
  • Dual values y from final BTRAN.
  • Reduced costs for sensitivity (Phase 13).

9.3 Deliverable

  • lp-revised crate compiles; solve_revised callable from lp-cli.
  • Correct (Solution, Certificate) on all Netlib instances tested in Phase 5.
  • Memory usage for revised path << dense for n > 500 (measurable by allocator instrumentation).
  • SolverStats::ftran_calls, btran_calls, refactorizations populated.
  • Differential test: assert_equiv_tableau passes on 10 small instances comparing revised vs. dense paths.

Check Threshold

Refactorization trigger fires at n_updates >= REFACTOR_THRESHOLD assert in integration test

9.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root Cause Debug Signal Recovery
BTRAN accumulates rounding error over iterations Reduced costs drift; pricing selects non-improving entering variable Eta-vector chain grows without refactorization SolverStats::n_updates large; objective non-monotone Reduce REFACTOR_THRESHOLD; trigger immediate refactorization
FTRAN column sign error under permutation Entering column direction wrong; basis becomes singular after pivot Row/column permutation applied in wrong order `
Partial pricing misses improving column Optimal not found; solver stalls at non-optimal Pricing cycle does not cover all columns before terminating Objective plateau with nonbasic rc < -OPT_TOL visible in full scan Complete one full-column scan every FULL_PRICE_FREQ iterations
Warm-start with stale LU Incorrect FTRAN/BTRAN; numerical errors from first pivot Saved basis LuFactorization is stale from a different problem instance `
x_B = B^{-1} b extraction triggers refactorization Excessive refactorizations inflate solve time FTRAN called on full b vector (dense); condition poor btran_calls / pivots >> 1 Cache x_B; update incrementally: x_B_new = x_B_old - d * theta
n_updates counter wraps u32 Silent modular overflow; refactorization suppressed Long solve on large instance n_updates == 0 when pivots > u32::MAX Use u64 for n_updates; saturating add

9.7 Performance Concerns

  • BTRAN: O(nnz(L) + nnz(U)) per iteration — dominant cost on large, sparse problems; avoid redundant BTRAN calls by caching y within the iteration.
  • FTRAN: sparse column a_j — exploit structural zeros via hyper-sparse solve (skip zero entries in forward solve).
  • Pricing inner product y · a_j: computed as a sparse dot product over nonzeros of a_j; O(nnz(col_j)) per candidate.
  • Partial pricing (price p = ceil(sqrt(n)) columns per iteration): reduces pricing cost from O(n * nnz_avg) to O(p * nnz_avg) at the cost of possibly more pivots.
  • refactorization is O(m^2) to O(m^3) depending on sparsity; schedule at REFACTOR_THRESHOLD rather than every pivot.
  • Vec<f64> allocations for d (FTRAN result) and y (BTRAN result): pre-allocate and reuse via scratch buffers in RevisedSimplex.

9.8 Edge Cases

  • m = 1 basis: FTRAN/BTRAN degenerate to scalar divide; special-case or let the general code handle without ill-conditioning checks.
  • Column a_j is all zeros (zero structural column): FTRAN yields zero vector; entering such a variable is degenerate/rank-reducing — reject.
  • Warm start with wrong basis dimension (saved from different problem): return Err(BasisDimensionMismatch) before any solve.
  • REFACTOR_THRESHOLD = 0 forced (testing mode): refactorize after every pivot — numerical accuracy test oracle.
  • Nonbasic variable at both lower and upper simultaneously (fixed): presolve must remove; assert in basis constructor.
  • y · a_j overflows for very large c or A entries: scale problem before computing (presolve scaling from Phase 12).

9.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Recovery
Basis becomes singular (pivot stability failure) LU refactorization returns rank-deficient error Status::Numerical Retry with tighter pivot threshold PIVOT_TOL; fall back to dense path
FTRAN residual too large ` B d - a_j
Revised and dense paths disagree on objective Differential test assertion failure Test failure only (not production) Bisect pivot sequence to find first disagreeing pivot; inspect FTRAN sign
Refactorization itself fails (e.g., near-zero pivot) lp-lu returns LuError::SingularPivot Status::Numerical Permute, add stabilization, return Numerical

9.10 Operational Notes

  • SolverStats fields: ftran_calls: u64, btran_calls: u64, refactorizations: u64, pivot_count: u64, partial_price_cycles: u64.
  • Log INFO: refactorization events with n_updates, condition estimate, and trigger reason.
  • Log DEBUG: per-iteration objective value, entering variable, reduced cost, step length.
  • Log TRACE: FTRAN/BTRAN residuals when compiled with #[cfg(debug_assertions)].
  • Metric: lp_solver_refactorizations_total, lp_solver_pivot_count, lp_solver_btran_duration_seconds_histogram.
  • No schema migration: lp-revised is a drop-in replacement for lp-tableau; same (Solution, Certificate) return type; selectable via SolverOpts::engine = Engine::Revised | Engine::Dense.

Phase 10 — LU Factorization of the Basis + Updates

10.1 Objectives

  • Implement lp-lu: sparse LU of the basis matrix B with partial (Markowitz-threshold) pivoting.
  • Provide FTRAN (B x = rhs) and BTRAN (B^T y = rhs) via forward/back triangular solves.
  • Support rank-1 pivot updates (Forrest-Tomlin eta-vector scheme; Bartels-Golub as fallback).
  • Trigger periodic refactorization when fill ratio or estimated condition number degrades.
  • This module is the numerical heart; stability here governs the entire solver's reliability.

10.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Anchors: P2 (numerical stability via LU with partial pivoting); I1 (basis nonsingularity enforced by refactorization triggers); P12 (basis factorization freshness — refactorize on fill-in/growth/residual thresholds); I11 (pivot rejected at selection time if below PIVOT_TOL).

Module: lp-lu/src/lib.rs, factor.rs, update.rs, solve.rs

Data structures (signatures only):

pub struct LuFactorization {
    pub l_eta: Vec<EtaVector>,          // L as product of eta vectors (column updates)
    pub u: CscMatrix,                   // upper triangular factor (sparse)
    pub row_perm: Vec<usize>,           // row permutation P
    pub col_perm: Vec<usize>,           // column permutation Q
    pub n_updates: usize,               // eta vectors appended since last full refactorize
}
pub struct EtaVector { pub col: usize, pub values: Vec<(usize, f64)> }

Factorization (factor.rs):

  • Markowitz cost = (r_i - 1)(c_j - 1) for candidate (i,j); choose pivot minimizing cost.
  • Threshold: accept pivot only if |a_ij| >= PIVOT_TOL * max-in-column (default 0.1).
  • On each elimination step, update remaining submatrix sparsity; store multipliers into L-eta list.
  • Build U as CSC; record row/col permutations P, Q such that P A Q = L U.

FTRAN (solve.rs):

  • Forward substitution through eta vectors left-to-right, then back-substitution through U.
  • Each eta application: apply column spike; O(nnz(eta)) per vector.

BTRAN (solve.rs):

  • Reverse eta traversal right-to-left (transpose order), then forward through U^T.

Forrest-Tomlin update (update.rs):

  • On a pivot (entering column e, leaving column l): compute spike vector via FTRAN on e, adjust U, append eta vector for the new column into l_eta.
  • Maintain a working upper Hessenberg structure; preserve U's triangularity by applying a sequence of Givens-style column operations.

Refactorization trigger:

  • n_updates >= REFACTOR_UPDATE_LIMIT (default 50) OR estimated condition > COND_THRESHOLD (default 1e10) OR fill ratio nnz(L+U)/nnz(B) > FILL_LIMIT (default 4.0).
  • Full re-factor re-runs Markowitz on the current basis columns extracted from the model.

Observability hooks:

  • LuStats { factorize_count, update_count, avg_fill_ratio, last_cond_estimate } exposed via LuFactorization::stats().

10.3 Deliverable

  • lp-lu crate compiles; factor, solve, update submodules pass unit tests.
  • lp-revised calls LuFactorization::ftran / btran; primal-simplex smoke test solves a 10-var LP via the revised path.
  • LuStats printed per-solve by the CLI --verbose flag.

10.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
Random dense 50×50 basis: residual ‖Bx - b‖_inf after FTRAN < 1e-10
Forrest-Tomlin: 200 sequential updates, residual < 1e-8
Refactorization triggered at n_updates == 50 verified by LuStats.factorize_count
BTRAN identity: y^T B = c_B^T within tolerance < 1e-10
100 random 100×100 bases: no singular panic, correct residual pass
Markowitz pivot selection: fill ratio for random sparse (5%) 200×200 <= 3× naive LU

10.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected
tests/lu/dense_5x5.json known LU; verify P, Q, L*U == A exact match
tests/lu/singular_near.json near-singular B; expect refactorization or Numerical status no panic, status returned
tests/lu/ftran_btran_roundtrip.json 50 random bases; FTRAN then BTRAN then compare residual < 1e-10
tests/lu/update_50.json 50 updates then residual check < 1e-8

10.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Debug Signal Recovery
Pivot below threshold accepted Massive fill, catastrophic cancellation avg_fill_ratio spike Tighten PIVOT_TOL; re-factor
Eta list grows unbounded Memory blowup, slow FTRAN/BTRAN (O(n_updates * m)) n_updates counter, solve time Enforce REFACTOR_UPDATE_LIMIT
Forrest-Tomlin drops triangularity Silently wrong FTRAN results; basis drift Residual monitor on FTRAN after update Fallback to full refactor
Permutation bookkeeping mismatch Scrambled FTRAN output; wrong pivots selected downstream Explicit PLU == B check in debug mode Unit-test every permutation path
Condition estimate too optimistic Refactorization deferred too long; numerical blow-up ‖Bx - b‖ growing per iteration Cheaper Oettli-Prager bound as secondary trigger
Integer overflow in row/col indices Wrong permutation applied silently on large m Saturating cast with assertion Use usize; add bounds-check assertions in debug

10.7 Performance Concerns

  • FTRAN/BTRAN cost: O(sum nnz(eta_i)); grows with n_updates — keep limit low.
  • Markowitz selection is O(nnz(B)^2) naive; cache eligible-pivot list per row/col to amortize.
  • Sparse U storage: CSC with sorted row indices; binary search on lookup — acceptable for m < 5000.
  • Avoid heap allocations inside the solve hot path; pre-allocate work vectors in LuFactorization.
  • FTRAN applied column-by-column in eta traversal — poor cache reuse for dense eta; threshold dense etas for compact storage.
  • Refactorization is expensive (O(m^2) avg); schedule only when triggered, not periodically at fixed intervals.

10.8 Edge Cases

  • Basis matrix B is exactly singular (degenerate problem): must return Err(Numerical) rather than divide-by-zero.
  • All-zero column in B (dropped variable): detect before factorization; reject with error.
  • Single-row basis (m=1): trivial LU; must not enter Markowitz loop.
  • Re-entry after Numerical status: calling code must re-initialize the basis, not retry the same factor.
  • n_updates wraps at usize::MAX if limit not enforced: guard with explicit check.
  • NaN/Inf propagation from a malformed input column: detect in FTRAN output; set Status::Numerical.

10.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Recovery
Numerical blow-up (condition > threshold) ‖Bx - b‖ > EPS_REFACTOR check Wrong primal solution; wrong duals Re-factor; if persists → Status::Numerical
Refactor produces singular U Diagonal zero in U All subsequent FTRAN/BTRAN invalid Return Err(Numerical) to simplex driver
Eta list corruption (bad update) BTRAN/FTRAN round-trip residual test Incorrect reduced costs; wrong pivots Discard updates; full re-factor
Memory OOM on large eta list Allocation failure Solve aborts REFACTOR_UPDATE_LIMIT cap prevents

10.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: lu_factorize_count, lu_update_count, lu_fill_ratio_avg, lu_cond_estimate_last — emit as structured log fields at TRACE level per solve.
  • CLI: --verbose prints LuStats summary at end of solve.
  • Reproducibility: PIVOT_TOL, REFACTOR_UPDATE_LIMIT, COND_THRESHOLD, FILL_LIMIT exposed as solver config fields (not compile-time constants) so conformance tests can pin them.
  • No external LAPACK dependency; all linear algebra is implemented within lp-lu to keep the crate self-contained.

Phase 11 — Dual Simplex + Warm Start

11.1 Objectives

  • Implement lp-dual: dual simplex that starts dual-feasible (reduced costs satisfy optimality) but primal-infeasible, and pivots to restore primal feasibility.
  • Support warm-start from a serialized Basis after a bound or RHS change without resolving from scratch.
  • Implement bounded-dual ratio test (handles finite variable bounds in the leaving-variable selection).
  • Leverage lp-lu FTRAN/BTRAN; reuse lp-pricing for entering variable selection in the dual context.
  • Enforce I3 (dual feasibility throughout) and I6 (ratio-test correctness) as runtime invariants.

11.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Anchors: I3 (dual feasibility maintained through the dual pivot loop); I6 (ratio-test correctness extended to the bounded-dual ratio test); P8 (warm-start friendliness via SavedBasis); P12 (factorization freshness re-checked after applying a ModelDelta, not assumed from n_updates alone).

Module: lp-dual/src/lib.rs, driver.rs, ratio.rs, warmstart.rs Core types (signatures):

pub fn dual_simplex(
    sf: &StandardForm,
    basis: &mut Basis,
    opts: &SolverOpts,
) -> Result<Solution, SolverError>;
pub fn warm_start(
    sf: &StandardForm,
    saved: SavedBasis,
    delta: &ModelDelta,
    opts: &SolverOpts,
) -> Result<Solution, SolverError>;
pub struct ModelDelta { pub rhs_changes: Vec<(usize, f64)>, pub bound_changes: Vec<(VarId, Bound)> }
pub struct SavedBasis { pub basic: Vec<VarId>, pub statuses: Vec<VarStatus>, pub factor_snapshot: Option<LuFactorization> }

Dual simplex loop (driver.rs):

  1. Verify dual feasibility: all reduced costs r_j >= -EPS_OPT for nonbasic-at-lower, <= EPS_OPT for nonbasic-at-upper. If violated, return Err(NotDualFeasible).
  2. Select leaving variable: basic variable with most infeasible value (most negative x_i - lb_i or most positive x_i - ub_i); if none, solution is primal-feasible → optimal.
  3. Compute pivot row via BTRAN: y = e_r B^-1; alpha_j = y^T a_j for each nonbasic j.
  4. Dual ratio test (ratio.rs): find entering j minimizing |r_j / alpha_j| subject to sign compatibility; maintain dual feasibility of the updated reduced costs.
  5. If no valid entering variable: problem is primal infeasible (dual ray exists) → emit Certificate::DualRay.
  6. Pivot: update Basis, apply Forrest-Tomlin update to LuFactorization, recompute RHS.
  7. Repeat until optimal or iteration limit. Bounded-dual ratio test (ratio.rs):
  • For a leaving basic variable at a bound, the entering variable may flip to its opposite bound (bound flip) if that maintains dual feasibility.
  • Track both lower-bound and upper-bound ratios; take the tighter constraint. Warm start (warmstart.rs):
  • Deserialize SavedBasis; apply ModelDelta (patch sf.b and sf.bounds in-place on a clone).
  • Recompute reduced costs from patched problem; check dual feasibility; if satisfied, run dual simplex.
  • If basis becomes primal-feasible immediately, emit Status::Optimal without iterations.
  • If delta invalidates dual feasibility, fall back to primal simplex with hot-started basis. Anti-cycling in dual simplex:
  • Use lexicographic rule on the leaving variable to prevent cycling (select smallest index on ties in the leaving ratio).

11.3 Deliverable

  • lp-dual crate; dual_simplex and warm_start functions callable from lp-simplex driver.
  • Demo: solve an LP, tighten a bound, warm-start dual simplex → correct re-optimized solution with fewer iterations than cold re-solve.
  • Certificate::DualRay emitted on proven-infeasible problems.

11.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
20 hand-crafted dual-feasible LPs solved correctly all optimal objectives match reference within 1e-8
Warm start after RHS perturbation: iteration count < 30% of cold re-solve iterations
Dual ray certificate on 5 infeasible problems verifiable: A^T y >= 0, b^T y < 0
Bounded-dual ratio test: 10 problems with general bounds correct, no bound violations
Dual feasibility invariant I3 maintained every iteration assert in debug builds
Cycling stress (Leen's dual-degenerate case): terminates within 500 pivots

11.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected
tests/dual/dual_feasible_start.json 5 small LPs, known dual-feasible basis optimal solution
tests/dual/warm_rhs_change.json Solve, perturb RHS by 5%, warm re-solve same optimal region, fewer iters
tests/dual/infeasible_dual_ray.json 3 infeasible problems DualRay certificate passes Farkas check
tests/dual/bounded_vars.json 10 problems with non-trivial bounds correct x, no bound violations

tests/dual/cold_fallback.json Delta invalidates dual feasibility falls back to primal, correct result

11.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Debug Signal Recovery
Not checking dual feasibility before entering loop Immediate incorrect pivot; wrong solution Pre-loop invariant assertion Add verify_dual_feasible guard at entry
Sign error in dual ratio test Wrong entering variable; dual infeasibility introduced I3 assertion fires Review sign conventions for Le/Ge/Eq constraints
Warm start with stale LU FTRAN/BTRAN yield wrong pivots after model change Residual check on first FTRAN Always re-factor after ModelDelta unless snapshot is fresh
Bound-flip omitted in bounded ratio test Basic variable driven past bound; primal infeasibility not resolved Bound violation log Implement bound-flip case in ratio.rs
Dual ray declared prematurely Missing entering candidates due to incorrect sign filter Check all alpha_j signs Expand eligibility check; add fixture for borderline case
Cycling in degenerate problem Infinite loop Iteration counter exceeds ITER_LIMIT Lexicographic anti-cycling on leaving selection

11.7 Performance Concerns

  • Dual ratio test scans all nonbasic columns: O(n - m) per iteration; cache reduced costs.
  • BTRAN per leaving variable: O(m) via eta traversal; dominant cost for large m.
  • Warm start saves re-factorization if SavedBasis.factor_snapshot is provided; avoids O(m^2) cost.
  • Bound-flip detection adds a branch per nonbasic variable in the ratio loop; branch predictor should handle well for sorted nonbasic lists.
  • Iteration count for dual simplex after small perturbation is typically O(perturbed constraints); warm start value is high.

11.8 Edge Cases

  • Delta adds a constraint that makes the basis invalid (column not in basis): rebuild basis with one dual Phase I step.
  • RHS change makes all constraints satisfied immediately: emit Status::Optimal without pivots.
  • Leaving variable has unbounded movement (all alpha_j wrong sign): infeasibility certificate.
  • ModelDelta is empty: skip re-solve, return original Solution unchanged.
  • Dual simplex on an unbounded problem: dual is infeasible before the solve starts; detect and return Status::Infeasible of the dual (= primal unbounded).
  • Very large RHS perturbation: dual simplex may require many pivots; fall back to cold primal if warm start exceeds WARM_ITER_BUDGET.

11.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Recovery
Dual feasibility lost mid-solve I3 assertion (debug) or residual check Incorrect optimal or wrong certificate Re-factor, re-check reduced costs; if persistent → Numerical
Warm start basis invalid after large delta FTRAN residual too large Wrong solution silently Trigger full cold re-solve with warning log
Dual ray certificate invalid Farkas check fails Incorrect infeasibility claim Re-run with stricter tolerance; return Numerical
Iteration limit hit before optimality Status::IterationLimit No solution Increase limit or hand off to primal simplex

11.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: dual_pivot_count, warm_start_count, warm_start_iter_saving_pct, dual_ray_count — structured log at DEBUG.
  • SavedBasis is serializable via serde (feature-gated); enables external warm-start workflows (e.g., sequential LP relaxations in branch-and-bound).
  • CLI: lp-cli solve --warm-basis basis.json loads a SavedBasis file; --save-basis writes one after solve.
  • Reproducibility: dual simplex pivot sequence is deterministic given the same PivotRule and sorted nonbasic list order (P10).
  • Shadow prices from dual simplex are the final dual values y; passed directly to lp-sensitivity.

Phase 12 — Presolve + Scaling

12.1 Objectives

  • Implement lp-presolve: problem reduction before the solve and solution mapping (postsolve) after. Presolve passes (applied in order): remove empty rows/cols, substitute singleton rows, tighten bounds via bound propagation, drop redundant constraints, fix variables at bounds.
  • Implement geometric mean scaling and Curtis-Reid equilibration scaling to improve conditioning of A. Postsolve: undo all presolve transformations in reverse order; produce a feasible equal-objective solution to the original problem.
  • Enforce I9 (presolve/postsolve fidelity) and I10 (standard-form equivalence) as correctness invariants.

12.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Anchors: I9 (presolve/postsolve fidelity enforced by reverse-order stack replay); I10 (standard-form equivalence preserved across presolve reduction); P7 (presolve → solve → postsolve pipeline).

Module: lp-presolve/src/lib.rs, passes.rs, scale.rs, postsolve.rs Core types (signatures):

pub struct PresolveProblem {
    pub reduced: LpProblem,
    pub stack: Vec<PresolveOp>,   // ordered undo stack
    pub col_map: Vec<Option<VarId>>,
    pub row_map: Vec<Option<ConstraintId>>,
}
pub enum PresolveOp {
    FixedVar { var: VarId, value: f64 },
    SingletonRow { row: ConstraintId, var: VarId, coeff: f64, rhs: f64 },
    RedundantRow { row: ConstraintId },
    EmptyCol { col: VarId },
    TightenedBound { var: VarId, old_bound: Bound },
    ScaleRow { row: ConstraintId, factor: f64 },
    ScaleCol { col: VarId, factor: f64 },
}
pub fn presolve(prob: &LpProblem, opts: &PresolveOpts) -> Result<PresolveProblem, SolverError>;
pub fn postsolve(presolved: &PresolveProblem, reduced_sol: &Solution) -> Solution;

Presolve passes (passes.rs):

Pass Condition Action Records
Empty row all coefficients zero if rhs feasible drop row; else infeasible RedundantRow
Empty col no constraints reference col if obj == 0 fix at bound; if obj < 0 and UB=∞ → unbounded EmptyCol or early exit
Singleton row exactly one nonzero in row substitute: fix x_j = (rhs - 0) / a_ij; update col bounds SingletonRow
Bound tightening row a_ij x_j <= b_i with all other terms bounded propagate implied bound on x_j TightenedBound
Fixed variable lb == ub substitute value into objective and constraints; remove col FixedVar
Redundant row row dominated by bound combination drop row RedundantRow
  • Passes iterate until no further reduction (fixed-point); limit to MAX_PRESOLVE_ROUNDS (default 10).
  • Infeasibility detected during presolve → return Err(SolverError::Infeasible) immediately (no solve needed). Scaling (scale.rs): Geometric mean scaling:
  • Row scale r_i = 1 / sqrt(max|a_ij| * min|a_ij|) for each row; column scale c_j analogously.
  • Apply: A' = R A C, b' = R b, c' = C c; scale back solution: x_orig = C x', y_orig = R y'. Curtis-Reid equilibration:
  • Iterative bipartite scaling minimizing log-ratio imbalance; converges in ~5 rounds.
  • Preferred over geometric for very ill-conditioned problems. Scaling control:
  • PresolveOpts { scaling: ScalingMode } — enum ScalingMode { None, Geometric, CurtisReid }.
  • Record row/col scale factors in PresolveOp::ScaleRow/ScaleCol; postsolve inverts them. Postsolve (postsolve.rs):
  • Pop stack in reverse order; for each PresolveOp reconstruct the variable value or constraint dual.
  • FixedVar: set x[var] = value; add value * obj_coeff to objective (already accounted — verify).
  • SingletonRow: recompute x[var] from the substitution equation; derive the dual for the dropped row.
  • ScaleRow/ScaleCol: invert scale on corresponding primal/dual values.
  • At end: verify ‖Ax - b‖ <= EPS_FEAS and obj_original == obj_reduced for assertion (debug only).

12.3 Deliverable

  • lp-presolve crate; presolve and postsolve callable from the lp-simplex orchestration loop.
  • Full pipeline: lp-parse → presolve → lp-simplex → postsolve → lp-sensitivity.
  • CLI lp-cli solve --presolve=none|geometric|curtisreid exercises all scaling modes.
  • Presolve stats printed: rows/cols removed, bounds tightened, rounds run.

12.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
10 small LPs: postsolve solution feasible in original ‖Ax - b‖ < EPS_FEAS = 1e-8
Presolve detects infeasibility before solve on 5 trivially infeasible LPs Err(Infeasible) returned, no simplex call
Geometric scaling: condition number of A after scaling vs. before < 0.1× original (order-of-magnitude improvement)
Curtis-Reid: convergence in <= 5 rounds assert in scale.rs
Presolve reduces size: test LP with 20% redundant rows >= 15% row reduction
Postsolve objective match `
Fixed-point termination at most MAX_PRESOLVE_ROUNDS iterations, no infinite loop

12.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected
tests/presolve/empty_row_col.lp Empty rows and cols; one infeasible empty row infeasible returned early
tests/presolve/singleton_chain.lp 5 chained singleton substitutions all variables fixed; trivial solve
tests/presolve/redundant_constraints.lp 30% redundant rows >= 25% row reduction

tests/presolve/scaling_ill_conditioned.lp A with condition ~ 1e8 after geometric scaling < 1e5
tests/presolve/postsolve_roundtrip.json 20 random LPs postsolve feasibility assertion passes

12.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Debug Signal Recovery
Postsolve reversal order wrong Incorrect variable values reconstructed Feasibility check ‖Ax-b‖ failure in postsolve Enforce strict stack pop order; unit-test each PresolveOp
Dual recovery omitted in postsolve Shadow prices wrong for removed rows Sensitivity results disagree with reference For each removed constraint derive dual from KKT conditions
Scaling applied but not inverted Solution in scaled space returned as-is Objective mismatch; variables out of bounds Assert scale-inversion in postsolve; add round-trip fixture
Bound tightening introduces numerical error False infeasibility declared Tighten only when gap > EPS_BOUND_TIGHTEN Use EPS_BOUND_TIGHTEN = 1e-9 guard
Singleton substitution with near-zero coefficient Division by near-zero; catastrophic values ` coeff
Empty-column unbounded case missed Solver runs, reports wrong result No EmptyCol op pushed; objective decreasing Check objective coefficient sign before marking empty col

12.7 Performance Concerns

  • Presolve passes scan the CSC matrix column-by-column; O(nnz) per pass; acceptable for m, n < 50000.
  • MAX_PRESOLVE_ROUNDS prevents quadratic pass accumulation on adversarial inputs.
  • stack (Vec<PresolveOp>) allocation: pre-allocate to m + n capacity to avoid repeated reallocs.
  • Curtis-Reid scaling: 5 rounds × O(nnz); dominates presolve time for large sparse problems — offer as opt-in.
  • Postsolve is always O(|stack|) = O(m + n); not on the critical path.
  • Geometric scaling: single pass O(nnz); default for speed.

12.8 Edge Cases

  • Problem entirely presolved to trivially optimal (all variables fixed): return Status::Optimal with objective computed from fixed values; no simplex call.
  • Presolve removes all constraints: all variables free within bounds; optimal is trivial (each free var at lb or ub per obj sign).
  • Scale factors of zero (zero row or column in A): detect before scaling; treat as empty row/col.
  • col_map / row_map with gaps: downstream code must handle None entries (removed rows/cols).
  • Free variables (lb = -inf, ub = +inf) in bound tightening: skip tightening for truly unbounded variables.
  • NaN/Inf in A detected during scaling: abort presolve with SolverError::Numerical.

12.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Recovery
False infeasibility from tightening Presolve returns Infeasible but problem is feasible Reproducible with tolerance tuning Increase EPS_BOUND_TIGHTEN; add bounds-check fixture
Postsolve produces infeasible solution ‖Ax-b‖ check fails Wrong solution returned to user Re-run without presolve; report Numerical
Scaling worsens condition Condition estimate increases after scaling LuStats.last_cond_estimate higher post-scale Fall back to ScalingMode::None
Stack corruption on partial presolve (panic mid-pass) Postsolve pops wrong ops Scrambled solution values Wrap each pass in error-boundary; roll back stack to checkpoint

12.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: presolve_rows_removed, presolve_cols_removed, presolve_bounds_tightened, presolve_rounds, scaling_mode, scaling_condition_before, scaling_condition_after — structured log at DEBUG.
  • CLI: --presolve-stats prints a summary table after presolve; --no-presolve disables all passes for debugging.
  • I9 enforced in integration tests: for every solved LP in the conformance suite, postsolve feasibility and objective match are asserted automatically.
  • Scaling factors are stored in PresolveProblem.stack and thus serializable alongside SavedBasis for warm-start correctness (scaling must be re-applied to a warm-started basis).
  • No dependency on external scaling libraries; all arithmetic within lp-presolve for portability and auditability.

Phase 13 — Sensitivity Analysis + Parsers + Driver

13.1 Objectives

  • Implement lp-sensitivity: cost-coefficient ranging, RHS ranging, shadow prices, reduced-cost ranges from the optimal basis.
  • Implement lp-parse: MPS (free/fixed-column) and LP-format readers; problem builder; solution writer.
  • Implement lp-cli: solve, sensitivity, convert, check subcommands; orchestrate presolve→solve→postsolve→report.
  • Constraints: sensitivity operates only on a provably optimal basis; ranging uses the optimal LuFactorization already in Basis; parser must handle all Netlib MPS files without panic.

13.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Anchors: P11 (numerical tolerance discipline — --tol-feas/--tol-opt map to named FEAS_TOL/OPT_TOL constants, never a literal comparison); P8 (warm-start basis file I/O via --warm-basis/--save-basis); P5 (check subcommand verifies certificates).

lp-sensitivity (lp-sensitivity/src/lib.rs)

  • Input: &Solution (status must be Optimal), &StandardForm, &Basis.
  • Cost-coefficient ranging for basic var j: compute allowable decrease/increase of c_j before a non-basic reduced cost changes sign; expressed as (delta_lo, delta_hi) per structural variable.
  • Cost-coefficient ranging for non-basic var j: reduced cost r_j = c_j − y^T a_j; allowable range = r_j itself (hits zero at one end, unbounded at the other unless another bound is binding).
  • RHS ranging for row i: use BTRAN to obtain the i-th row of B^-1; min-ratio on the basis values vs the direction vector gives the allowable delta b_i before basis changes; reported as (b_lo, b_hi).
  • Shadow prices: dual vector y = c_B B^-1 already in Solution.duals_y; reported directly.
  • Reduced-cost ranges: for non-basic vars, the interval within which c_j can vary and keep the variable non-basic.
  • Output struct: Sensitivity { obj_ranges: Vec<(f64,f64)>, rhs_ranges: Vec<(f64,f64)>, shadow_prices: Vec<f64>, reduced_cost_ranges: Vec<(f64,f64)> }.
  • Postsolve mapping: sensitivity computed on the presolved/scaled problem must be mapped back to original variable indices via StandardForm.var_map; scaling factors applied in reverse.
  • Guard: return Err(SensitivityError::NotOptimal) if solution.status != Status::Optimal.

lp-parse (lp-parse/src/mps.rs, lp-parse/src/lp_format.rs, lp-parse/src/builder.rs, lp-parse/src/writer.rs)

  • MPS fixed-column reader: 10-column field layout; sections NAME, ROWS, COLUMNS, RHS, BOUNDS, RANGES, OBJSENSE, ENDATA; integer markers.
  • MPS free-format reader: space-delimited variant; auto-detected by absence of strict column alignment.
  • LP-format reader: minimize/maximize, subject to, bounds, general, binary, end; infix expression a x1 + b x2 <= c; coefficient parsing with sign propagation.
  • LpBuilder: accumulate rows/columns into LpProblem incrementally; add_var, add_constraint, set_bound, set_objective; calls validate() on build().
  • Solution writer: emit primal/dual values, objective, status in MPS solution format and a simple text report.
  • Error handling: ParseError { line: usize, kind: ParseErrorKind } covering unknown section, malformed float, undeclared variable, dimension mismatch.

lp-cli (lp-cli/src/main.rs, lp-cli/src/commands/)

  • solve: parse→presolve→standard-form→Phase I/II simplex (or revised simplex if --revised)→postsolve→print solution + certificate.
  • sensitivity: same as solve, then compute and print Sensitivity; tabular output of ranges.
  • convert: parse input file (MPS or LP), write in the other format; no solving.
  • check: parse a problem + solution file; verify primal/dual feasibility and complementary slackness within --tol; exit code 0 = verified, 1 = violation found, 2 = parse error.
  • Flags: --pivot [dantzig|bland|devex], --revised, --presolve/--no-presolve, --scale/--no-scale, --max-iter N, --tol-feas F, --tol-opt F, --warm-basis FILE, --save-basis FILE.
  • Pipeline: lp_presolve::presolve → lp_model::to_standard_form → choose tableau (lp_tableau) or revised (lp_revised/lp_lu) → lp_simplex::solve → lp_presolve::postsolve → lp_sensitivity::analyze (if requested).
  • Output: structured JSON (--json) or human-readable table; both include status, objective, certificate type.

13.3 Deliverable

  • lp-sensitivity crate compiles and produces Sensitivity for any optimal Solution/Basis pair.
  • lp-parse reads all Netlib MPS files and the LP-format subset without panic; round-trips convert.
  • lp-cli binary: all four subcommands functional end-to-end; solve on a small LP prints correct answer.

13.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
Shadow prices match reference (scipy.optimize.linprog dual) relative error < 1e-7 on Netlib test set
Cost ranging lower/upper bounds verified by perturbing c_j and re-solving basis-change point matches within 1e-8
RHS ranging bounds verified by perturbing b_i and re-solving within 1e-8
MPS round-trip (parse→write→re-parse) objectives agree to 1e-10
check subcommand catches KKT violations injected at tolerance 1e-6 100% detection
LP-format parse of all bundled fixtures zero panics, correct variable counts

13.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected
fixtures/sensitivity/afiro.mps RHS and obj ranges against known reference ranges within 1e-8 of reference values
fixtures/sensitivity/blend.mps shadow price signs for <= constraints negative duals for binding Le rows
fixtures/parse/free_mps_sample.mps free-format MPS auto-detection identical LpProblem to fixed-format version
fixtures/parse/lp_format_sample.lp LP-format read same objective/constraints as MPS equivalent
fixtures/parse/malformed_undeclared_var.mps undeclared column in COLUMNS section ParseError::UndeclaredVariable
fixtures/parse/missing_endata.mps truncated file ParseError::UnexpectedEof
fixtures/cli/roundtrip.sh convert MPS→LP→MPS, solve both, compare objectives objectives agree < 1e-10

13.6 Pitfalls

  • Sensitivity on presolved problem maps to wrong original indices: var_map in StandardForm must cover artificials; postsolve must skip artificial variables when returning ranges. Debug via logging index translations.
  • Degenerate basis produces zero-width ranges: correct behavior — a degenerate optimal has zero allowable range in the degenerate direction; do not clamp or discard. Surface in output.
  • Infinite ranging silently stored as f64::INFINITY: downstream JSON serializers may emit null or error; serialize as a large sentinel or a null-aware schema; document the convention.
  • MPS RANGES section misread as RHS: RANGES modifies constraint type (Le→ranged), not just the RHS. Incorrect parsing produces wrong Relation and silently wrong dual values.
  • LP-format sign propagation across line breaks: a coefficient with sign separated from its variable by a newline is legal but commonly mishandled; tokenizer must treat newlines as whitespace in expressions.
  • --save-basis written before postsolve: a basis saved on the standard-form space cannot be directly warm-started on a re-parsed (re-presolved) problem; document that basis files are format-version stamped.

13.7 Performance Concerns

  • RHS ranging BTRAN per row: O(m) BTRAN solves for full ranging is O(m²); batch by reusing the factorization across rows; factorization already paid for.
  • Sensitivity memory: obj_ranges and rhs_ranges are O(n) + O(m) f64 pairs; negligible compared to LU factors.
  • MPS tokenizer allocation: avoid per-token String allocations; use a &str slice over a memory-mapped or pre-loaded String buffer.
  • CLI JSON output: avoid repeated small write! calls; buffer into String then single stdout().write_all.

13.8 Edge Cases

  • Problem with zero structural variables after presolve: sensitivity returns empty vecs, not panic.
  • All constraints non-binding at optimum: all shadow prices zero; ranging returns infinite upper bounds.
  • Variable at both lower and upper bound (fixed var): reduced cost undefined sign; emit NaN or a special tag in output with documentation.
  • MPS file with OBJSENSE MAX without explicit normalization: parse must negate objective before storing, then un-negate shadow prices in sensitivity output.
  • LP-format with no bounds section: all variables default to [0, +∞); must not inherit any previous problem's bounds in a CLI session that solves multiple files.

13.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Recovery
lp-sensitivity called on non-optimal basis assert status == Optimal at entry returns Err, no panic caller checks Solution.status
MPS parser panics on Netlib file integration test suite fails entire solve pipeline halted fix parser edge case; add fixture
CLI exits 0 on infeasible (bug) check fixture detects it wrong user decision from output audit exit-code mapping in solve
RHS range direction sign error shadow price sign check fixture incorrect ranging direction reported unit test with known LP

13.10 Operational Notes

  • Log level DEBUG in lp-parse: emit each section header encountered and variable count per section.
  • Log level INFO in lp-cli: emit final status, objective, iteration count, pivot rule used, presolve reduction stats.
  • --json output includes solver_version, pivot_rule, presolve_enabled, iterations, wall_time_ms.
  • sensitivity subcommand table columns: variable, obj_range_lo, obj_range_hi, reduced_cost; row table: constraint, shadow_price, rhs_range_lo, rhs_range_hi.
  • Basis files (.basis) are versioned with a header lp-solver-basis v1; a mismatch on load returns WarmStartError::VersionMismatch — does not abort, re-solves from scratch.

Phase 14 — Hardening + Conformance + Perf + Packing

14.1 Objectives

  • Conformance: solve all Netlib LP instances; match reference solver objective within tolerance; classify infeasible/unbounded correctly.
  • Correctness: KKT certificate verification on every reported-optimal solve; cycling stress tests (Beale, Kuhn-Quandt) pass without divergence.
  • Numerical: refactorization trigger tests; condition-number-driven stability tests; tolerance sensitivity sweeps.
  • Performance: benchmark suite on Netlib medium instances; solve-time ceiling per instance class.
  • Packing: cargo doc, README.md, CHANGELOG.md, crate Cargo.toml metadata, workspace Cargo.lock pinned; cargo publish --dry-run passes.

14.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Anchors: I7 (Beale/Kuhn-Quandt cycling tests verify finite termination); P11 (tolerance-sweep tests exercise named FEAS_TOL/OPT_TOL/PIVOT_TOL constants, clippy::float_cmp gate); P12 (refactorization-trigger tests verify basis factorization freshness); I1 (condition-number tracking keeps the basis nonsingular); I12 (KKT verifier checks the full certificate bundle on every Netlib instance).

Netlib conformance harness (tests/netlib_conformance.rs)

  • Download/vendor 90+ Netlib LP instances into tests/fixtures/netlib/ as MPS files.
  • For each instance: parse → presolve → solve (revised simplex, Dantzig pricing) → postsolve → verify status matches reference table; if optimal, verify objective within 1e-6 relative.
  • Reference table tests/fixtures/netlib_reference.csv: columns name,status,objective; populated from GLPK or HiGHS output offline.
  • KKT verifier (lp-core/src/kkt.rs): given LpProblem, Solution; check primal feasibility (Ax = b within tol, bounds), dual feasibility (reduced costs sign-correct), complementary slackness; return KktReport { primal_viol, dual_viol, cs_viol }.
  • Invoked automatically after every solve() call in #[cfg(test)] builds via a post-solve hook in lp-simplex. Cycling stress tests (tests/cycling.rs)
  • Beale's 6-variable cycling example: run with Dantzig (cycling expected without anti-cycling) → confirm Bland terminates; confirm lexicographic terminates; record iteration counts.
  • Kuhn-Quandt degenerate example: same structure.
  • Fixture: stored as inline LP strings; test asserts status == Optimal and cycle counter never exceeds 2 * n_vars * n_constraints. Numerical stability tests (tests/numerical.rs)
  • Ill-conditioned LP: Hilbert-matrix-derived constraints; verify refactorization triggered at least once (monitored via a refactorize_count metric in lp-lu).
  • Condition-number tracking: LuFactorization exposes estimated_condition() -> f64; test asserts it stays below 1e12 after refactorization.
  • Tolerance sweep: run a standard LP with tol_feas in [1e-4, 1e-12]; assert objective monotonically approaches reference as tolerance tightens.
  • Pivot stability: test that a near-zero pivot is rejected (pivot tolerance PIVOT_TOL = 1e-7); confirm alternative pivot selected. Benchmark suite (benches/solve.rs, using criterion)
    Benchmark Instance Metric
    bench_small_dense 20-var 15-constraint random LP median solve time
    bench_netlib_afiro AFIRO (27 rows, 51 cols) median iterations + time
    bench_netlib_blend BLEND (74 rows, 114 cols) median time
    bench_netlib_sc205 SC205 (205 rows) median time
    bench_tableau_vs_revised same LP, both backends speedup ratio recorded
  • Ceilings (CI gate): bench_small_dense < 1 ms; bench_netlib_afiro < 50 ms; no ceiling enforced as a hard failure on larger instances (record only). Packing (Cargo.toml workspace, README.md, CHANGELOG.md)
  • Each crate: description, license = "MIT OR Apache-2.0", keywords, categories, repository fields set.
  • cargo doc --no-deps --all-features produces zero warnings.
  • cargo clippy -- -D warnings clean on all crates.
  • cargo fmt --check passes.
  • cargo test --workspace passes including integration tests.
  • cargo publish --dry-run on lp-core and lp-cli succeeds.
  • README.md: quick-start (cargo install lp-cli), example invocations for all four subcommands, tolerance documentation, known limitations.

14.3 Deliverable

  • All Netlib conformance tests pass (status + objective) with zero regressions.
  • KKT verifier integrated; every optimal result in cargo test is KKT-checked.
  • Beale and Kuhn-Quandt cycling tests pass under Bland and lexicographic anti-cycling.
  • criterion benchmark report generated; bench_small_dense < 1 ms confirmed.
  • Workspace compiles clean with clippy -D warnings; docs build without warnings.

KKT dual violation at optimum <= tol_opt on all Netlib instances

14.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected
tests/fixtures/netlib/afiro.mps small optimal LP objective matches reference within 1e-6
tests/fixtures/netlib/infeas1.mps known infeasible Status::Infeasible + Certificate::DualRay
tests/fixtures/netlib/unbnd1.mps known unbounded Status::Unbounded + Certificate::PrimalRay
tests/fixtures/cycling/beale.lp Beale 6-var cycling terminates Status::Optimal under Bland
tests/fixtures/cycling/kuhn_quandt.lp degenerate cycling terminates Status::Optimal under Bland
tests/fixtures/numerical/hilbert_5.mps ill-conditioned basis refactorization triggered; optimal found
tests/fixtures/netlib_reference.csv reference objectives/statuses ground truth for conformance comparison

14.6 Pitfalls

  • Netlib MPS files with RANGES section: incorrect RANGES handling silently shifts the feasible region; affected instances report wrong objective. Debug by comparing parsed bounds to reference solver's model dump.
  • KKT verifier tolerance mismatch: using a stricter tolerance in the verifier than the solver's own tolerances causes false KKT failures on legitimately optimal solutions; always use the same tol_feas/tol_opt that the solver used.
  • Criterion benchmark noise on CI: wall-time measurements vary; use --sample-size 50 and record median; do not gate hard on wall time in CI — gate on iteration count or use #[bench] unit counters instead.
  • cargo publish ordering: crates with intra-workspace dependencies must be published in dependency order (lp-core first, then dependents); --dry-run does not enforce this but actual publish will fail.
  • Clippy false positives on numerical code: clippy::float_cmp, clippy::cast_precision_loss will trigger on intentional float comparisons; allow them at the crate level with a documented #[allow(...)] and a comment explaining the tolerance is handled explicitly (P6).
  • Postsolve KKT check on wrong problem: the KKT verifier must receive the original LpProblem (before presolve), not the StandardForm; verifying against StandardForm will fail on presolved problems where rows were dropped.

14.7 Performance Concerns

  • Netlib conformance test suite runtime: 90+ instances run sequentially; use cargo nextest with parallel test execution or split into feature-gated test groups to stay under 5 min CI limit.
  • Criterion warm-up: LU factorization JIT effects; use criterion's .warm_up_time(Duration::from_secs(2)) to stabilize.
  • cargo doc link checking: cross-crate intra-doc links resolved at doc-build time; broken links are warnings-as-errors under RUSTDOCFLAGS; audit before pack.
  • Workspace Cargo.lock drift: reproducible benchmark results require pinned dependencies; commit Cargo.lock even for libraries in this workspace.

14.8 Edge Cases

  • Netlib instance with no feasible solution but Status::IterationLimit returned (iteration cap too low): default max_iter = 10_000 * n_vars prevents this; conformance test asserts status is never IterationLimit on any Netlib instance.
  • KKT verifier called on Status::Numerical: return KktReport with skipped = true; do not assert violation thresholds.
  • Zero-row or zero-column LP after presolve: standard-form conversion produces empty matrix; solver should return Status::Optimal with objective equal to constant offset; KKT trivially passes.
  • Benchmark binary built without --release: silently slow; benches/ are only compiled with --release; add [[bench]] entries in Cargo.toml to enforce.
  • cargo doc with private items exposed via pub(crate) in lp-lu: intra-doc links to private types cause doc-build warnings; mark internal types explicitly.

14.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Recovery
Netlib regression (objective mismatch after refactor) conformance CI test fails solver correctness in doubt bisect commit history; compare pivot trace against known-good run
KKT verifier reports false positives verifier test on hand-solved LP fails blocks CI audit tolerance alignment between solver and verifier
Cycling test hangs CI timeout (set 60 s per test) blocks release confirm anti-cycling flag active; add pivot-count assert with early abort
cargo publish fails on dependency order publish CI step fails no release publish leaf crates first; automate order in release script
clippy -D warnings blocks CI on new Rust edition CI lint step fails blocks merge audit new lints per edition upgrade; add targeted allows with rationale

14.10 Operational Notes

  • lp-lu exposes metric counters: refactorize_count, eta_update_count, estimated_condition; accessible via LuFactorization::stats() -> LuStats; printed in lp-cli --verbose mode.
  • lp-simplex exposes SolveTrace { pivots: usize, phase1_pivots: usize, degeneracy_ratio: f64, wall_ns: u64 }; emitted to stderr as JSON under LPSOLVER_TRACE=1.
  • Conformance test output: on failure print name, expected_status, got_status, expected_obj, got_obj, abs_err, rel_err to aid triage.
  • README.md documents: crate graph, supported LP formats, tolerance parameters and defaults, known unsupported MPS features (SOS, semicontinuous), relationship to Phase 11 warm-start workflow.
  • CHANGELOG.md entries keyed by phase number: Phase 13 entry covers sensitivity + parsers + CLI; Phase 14 entry covers conformance + hardening + pack; future coding-phase entries TBD.
  • Release checklist (in RELEASING.md): cargo test --workspace, cargo clippy -D warnings, cargo fmt --check, cargo doc, conformance pass >= 95%, benchmark recorded, cargo publish in dependency order.

Threat Model

Trust Boundaries

Per the architecture diagram, the system has three trust boundaries and the following data-flow trust model:

Boundary Location in Architecture Data Crossing Validation Responsibility
File ingress lp-parse (sole parser crate) Raw MPS / LP bytes → LpProblem lp-parse; rejects before leaving crate
Configuration lp-cli startup CLI flags → SolverConfig lp-cli; validates at process start
Basis serialization lp-dual warm-start path Basis JSON file → Basis struct lp-dual; validates dimension + problem hash

The solver core (lp-model, lp-simplex, lp-revised, lp-lu, lp-presolve) runs in-process with full address-space access. No network listeners. No privileged ports. No setuid. No IPC sockets. Single-process, single-threaded solve loop; stateless between invocations.

Untrusted data does NOT enter any crate other than lp-parse. Internal crates receive validated LpProblem or StandardForm values; they assume these are structurally correct and focus on numerical correctness, not input validation.

Threat Table

# Threat Attack Vector Trust Boundary Impact Mitigation Detection Telemetry Recovery
T1 Malformed MPS/LP syntax Adversarial input file lp-parse ingress Panic / wrong parse / OOM Strict parser rejects unknown section keywords, validates all field counts, caps dimensions; fuzz lp-parse with libFuzzer Parse returns Err with byte offset + section lp_parse_errors_total{error_kind} counter Reject file; return Err; no partial internal state retained
T2 Integer overflow in dimension arithmetic File declaring size near usize::MAX lp-parse → lp-model Allocator panic; under-allocated buffer checked_mul / checked_add throughout all dimension computations; reject n_rows×n_cols overflow before any allocation Overflow triggers Err, not panic Structured error log with declared dimensions Err(Overflow) returned; zero allocation
T3 DoS via oversized dense matrix n_rows=10^7, n_cols=10^7 lp-parse OOM; process killed MAX_ROWS=500_000 (configurable), MAX_COLS=1_000_000, MAX_NNZ=50_000_000; checked at parse time Limit check fires before allocation log::warn with declared vs limit values Return Err(ProblemTooLarge)
T4 Algorithmic DoS via adversarial cycling Crafted LP with cycling basis sequence lp-simplex Infinite loop; CPU exhaustion I7: Bland's rule or lexicographic perturbation; hard MAX_ITERS ceiling (default 10^7, configurable); stall detector switches pricing to Bland after STALL_WINDOW pivots without improvement MAX_ITERS exceeded → Status::IterationLimit; stall counter in log lp_stall_count, lp_pivot_count Return IterationLimit; log pivot count and last objective

T11 Stale / incompatible warm-start basis Basis file from a different problem lp-dual warm-start Incorrect or divergent solve; I1 violation Basis validity check: compare n_rows, n_cols, VarId range, and problem hash (SHA-256 of A, b, c) before warm-start use Mismatch → Err(IncompatibleBasis) log::warn with stored hash vs current hash Reject basis; cold-start primal simplex
T12 Sensitivity ranging precision collapse Near-degenerate ranging interval → zero or sign flip lp-sensitivity Incorrect shadow prices or wrong ranging bounds Tolerance guards in ranging loops; clamp to (−∞, +∞) when step is genuinely unbounded; cross-check duals against Phase II reduced costs Sensitivity self-check: perturb c_j by ranging bound, re-solve, compare objective log::debug with ranging step magnitudes Report (−∞, +∞) for degenerate range; flag in output
T13 Path traversal in output file argument --output=../../etc/cron.d/evil lp-cli Overwrite arbitrary files Validate output path: must be absolute or relative to CWD; refuse paths containing .. components; require explicit --allow-outside-cwd flag for non-CWD paths Path validation error at startup log::error with rejected path Reject; exit with error
T14 Pivot tolerance too loose → basis singularity Default PIVOT_TOL set too large by operator lp-lu Singular pivot accepted; I1 violated Enforce PIVOT_TOL lower bound of 1e-12; warn if PIVOT_TOL > 1e-7; check pivot element ≥ PIVOT_TOL before accepting a pivot Singularity check in lp-lu triggers refactorize

Failure Modes and Recovery

Failure Classification

Failure classes are grouped into four families for triage priority:

Family Classes SLO Impact Default Severity
Correctness Wrong optimum, false infeasible, false unbounded, certificate mismatch Release blocker PAGE
Termination Cycling, IterationLimit on tractable problem SLO breach WARN
Numerical NaN in tableau, LU refactorization failure, Status::Numerical Release blocker if > 1% rate PAGE
Operational OOM, parse failure, warm-start divergence, postsolve mismatch, sensitivity error Functional regression WARN

Class Symptoms Root Causes Detection Automatic Recovery Manual Recovery Operator Actions Forensic Artifacts Long-term Mitigation
