OOM / allocation failure Process killed by OS; allocator panics Problem too large; runaway fill in LU; MAX_NNZ not enforced Allocation size log; MAX_PROBLEM_SIZE guards at parse Pre-rejected at parse if limits configured Reduce problem size; enable presolve to shrink --max-rows / --max-nnz CLI caps ulimit log; OS OOM log; problem file dimensions T3 limits; sparse LU fill-reduction via column ordering
NaN in tableau or solution NaN in objective or x vector Pivot on near-zero element; extreme coefficient magnitudes NaN sentinel check after each pivot (debug build); condition estimate alarm Refactorize immediately; reject pivot if pivot < PIVOT_TOL Re-scale coefficients; reduce problem Inspect input coefficient range; apply --scale=curtisreid
LU refactorization failure LuFactorization returns rank-deficient; zero diagonal in U Singular or near-singular basis after successive eta updates Rank check in lp-lu; zero diagonal detection Re-pivot with alternative entering variable; choose larger pivot element Restart from Phase I with full refactorization --refactor-freq=1 forces refactorization every pivot (slow, safe) Basis matrix dump at failure Partial pivoting (P2); column ordering heuristic for fill reduction
Presolve / postsolve mismatch Postsolve solution infeasible in original (I9 violated); objective differs Incorrect bound reconstruction; wrong substitution ordering; off-by-one Re-check A·x ≤/=/≥ b after postsolve within FEASIBILITY_TOL; objective equality within 1e-8 rel Disable presolve; re-solve original Trace postsolve stack step by step; isolate offending reduction --no-presolve bypass Presolve operation log; original + reduced problem file Unit test each reduction type; regression on Netlib presolved instances
Warm-start divergence Dual simplex fails to reach primal feasibility; degrades to many pivots Basis incompatible with modified problem; I8 violated in dual context Dimension / bound / hash check before warm start (T11) Fall back to cold-start primal simplex Verify basis file matches problem version --no-warm-start forces cold start Basis file + problem hash Version-stamp basis files; validate on load
Sensitivity analysis error Ranging interval wrong; shadow price deviates > 1e-6 from reference Off-by-one in ranging loop; wrong basis status; duals not extracted correctly --verify-sensitivity perturbation re-solve; comparison with reference solver on Netlib None automatic Trace ranging computation for affected row/col --verify-sensitivity flag; compare with GLPK output Solution + basis at reported optimum; reference output Differential test lp-sensitivity on Netlib; ranging unit tests at boundary conditions
Parse failure on valid input Err on a conforming MPS/LP file that GLPK accepts Parser too strict; CRLF/tab encoding; non-ASCII in comments Fuzz corpus coverage; conformance test vs GLPK-parsed versions None Normalize line endings; check non-ASCII dos2unix pre-processing; validate encoding Parser error with byte offset and line content Fuzz lp-parse with libFuzzer; add CRLF/tab normalization; expand conformance corpus
Status::Numerical (unresolved) Solver gives up; no certificate Extreme ill-conditioning; catastrophic cancellation; all retries exhausted Exhausted refactorization retries; condition estimate exceeds hard ceiling Return Status::Numerical with partial solution and condition log Apply Curtis-Reid scaling; reduce problem; try interior-point (stretch goal) --scale=curtisreid; inspect condition_estimate log Condition estimate log; final basis state Improve scaling pipeline (Phase 12); add interior-point fallback (Phase 14 stretch)
IterationLimit hit on tractable problem MAX_ITERS reached unexpectedly Default limit too tight for problem class; pricing rule inefficient Status::IterationLimit in output; pivot count log None automatic Increase MAX_ITERS; switch pricing rule --max-iters=N; --pivot-rule=devex Pivot count; objective progression log Calibrate default limits on Netlib benchmark; document per-class typical pivot counts
Certificate type mismatch Certificate::KktOptimal on Status::Infeasible or vice versa Status / Certificate produced by different code paths; out-of-sync Compile-time exhaustive match on (Status, Certificate) in verification function; runtime assertion None Trace phase transitions; verify certificate generation logic Debug build assertion fires with status + certificate values Phase log; status transition trace Enforce invariant at type level: Certificate is a discriminated union keyed on Status

Operational Readiness

Metrics Strategy

All metrics are emitted as structured JSON log events. A metrics sink is configured at startup via --metrics-output (file path, stderr, or no-op). No external metrics server is required in the base build. A thin adapter crate (lp-metrics-prom, optional) bridges to Prometheus text exposition format for integration with Prometheus + Grafana stacks.

Metric naming follows the pattern lp_<subsystem>_<noun>_<unit> using standard Prometheus conventions (underscores, lowercase, units as suffix). All metrics carry a problem_name label when emitted per-solve; aggregate metrics omit it.

Metric Type Labels Purpose
lp_solve_duration_seconds Histogram (buckets: 0.001, 0.01, 0.1, 1, 10, 60) problem_name, status, pricing_rule Solve time distribution
lp_pivot_count_total Counter problem_name, phase (phase1/phase2/dual) Simplex iteration cost per phase
lp_refactorization_count_total Counter problem_name, trigger (update_count/condition/periodic) LU stability signal
lp_condition_estimate Gauge problem_name Basis ill-conditioning; alarm > COND_THRESHOLD
lp_stall_count_total Counter problem_name Degeneracy frequency; trigger for Bland switch
lp_status_total Counter status (Optimal/Infeasible/Unbounded/IterationLimit/Numerical) Outcome distribution per status class
lp_kkt_primal_violation Gauge problem_name max
lp_kkt_dual_violation Gauge problem_name max reduced-cost sign violation at optimum
lp_kkt_complementary_slackness Gauge problem_name max
lp_presolve_row_reduction_pct Gauge problem_name Fraction of rows eliminated by presolve
lp_presolve_col_reduction_pct Gauge problem_name Fraction of columns eliminated by presolve
lp_parse_errors_total Counter error_kind (syntax/overflow/invalid_value/too_large) Input quality signal
lp_lu_fill_ratio Gauge problem_name LU fill relative to original nnz; triggers refactor threshold
lp_bound_flips_total Counter problem_name Bounded-variable simplex bound-flip frequency
lp_warm_start_accepted_total Counter — Warm-start basis accepted count
lp_warm_start_rejected_total Counter reason (incompatible/hash_mismatch/dimension) Warm-start rejection count

Tracing Strategy

All spans use the tracing crate (dev-dependency only; no-op subscriber by default). The caller or lp-cli enables a subscriber (e.g., tracing-subscriber with JSON layer) at the process boundary. The design ensures zero overhead when the no-op subscriber is active — no allocations in hot pivot loops.

Sampling policy for production: record one full span tree per solve; suppress DEBUG/TRACE spans unless the corresponding feature flag (debug-pivots / trace-linear-algebra) is compiled in and the subscriber's filter level is set accordingly. Per-pivot spans are disabled in release profile by default (cost: ~1 ns per pivot × 10^6 pivots = ~1 ms; acceptable for diagnostic builds, not for benchmarking).

Span Parent Key Attributes
lp.solve root n_rows, n_cols, n_nnz, pricing_rule, presolve_enabled, warm_start, problem_name
lp.parse lp.solve format (mps/lp), file_bytes, n_rows_declared, n_cols_declared
lp.presolve lp.solve rows_before, rows_after, cols_before, cols_after, n_reductions
lp.standard_form lp.solve n_slack, n_surplus, n_artificial, n_bound_splits
lp.phase1 lp.solve n_artificials, result (feasible/infeasible), pivot_count
lp.phase2 lp.solve pivot_count, pricing_rule_switches, stall_count
lp.dual_simplex lp.solve pivot_count, warm_start_accepted
lp.lu.refactor lp.phase2 or lp.dual_simplex trigger, fill_ratio, condition_estimate, nnz_before, nnz_after
lp.postsolve lp.solve n_steps, feasibility_check_ok, objective_delta_rel
lp.sensitivity lp.solve n_obj_ranges, n_rhs_ranges, verify_enabled
lp.kkt_verify lp.solve primal_violation, dual_violation, cs_violation, passed

Trace IDs are written into the solution JSON output field trace_id for log correlation.

Structured Logging

All log events are JSON lines with mandatory fields { timestamp, level, crate, span, event, ...context }.

Level Events
ERROR KKT certificate failure; NaN detected in solution; postsolve feasibility violation; Status::Numerical after all retries
WARN Stall detected and pricing switched; condition estimate > COND_THRESHOLD; tolerance override outside recommended range; basis rejected (warm-start); fill ratio > FILL_WARN_THRESHOLD
INFO Solve start (with config dump); solve end (status, objective, iteration count, duration); presolve summary; Phase I result; refactorization triggered
DEBUG Per-pivot detail (entering/leaving variable, step size, reduced cost) — gated behind feature flag debug-pivots
TRACE FTRAN/BTRAN vector values; eta-update details — gated behind feature flag trace-linear-algebra

Raw variable vectors are never logged at INFO or above; only summary statistics (max, norm, count of nonzeros) are logged.

Dashboard Design

Recommended panels for a Grafana-compatible dashboard consuming the JSON log sink:

Panel Visualization Source Metric Alert Threshold
Solve outcomes Pie chart lp_status_total —
Solve duration p50/p95/p99 Time-series lp_solve_duration_seconds p95 > 10× baseline
Pivot count per solve Scatter lp_pivot_count_total > 10^6 for n < 1000
Refactorization frequency Bar lp_refactorization_count_total > REFACTOR_LIMIT/2 per solve
Condition estimate Time-series + threshold line lp_condition_estimate > COND_THRESHOLD
KKT violations Gauge triple (primal/dual/cs) lp_kkt_* Any > OPTIMALITY_TOL
Stall rate Counter delta lp_stall_count_total Rising trend
Presolve reduction % Dual gauge row/col reduction —
LU fill ratio Gauge lp_lu_fill_ratio > 5× original nnz
Parse error rate Counter lp_parse_errors_total Any non-zero
Status::Numerical rate Counter lp_status_total{status=Numerical} > 1% of recent solves

Alerting Philosophy

Correctness alerts take precedence over performance alerts. When a correctness alert fires, silence performance alerts for the same problem class.

Severity Condition Response
PAGE KKT violation > OPTIMALITY_TOL on any reported Optimal Immediate investigation; run RB-01
PAGE NaN in any solution vector Run RB-04; disable that input class
PAGE Status::Numerical rate > 1% of solves in 5-minute window Run RB-04; check COND_THRESHOLD config
WARN condition_estimate > COND_THRESHOLD on > 5% of solves Review scaling; run RB-04
WARN Refactorization count per solve > REFACTOR_LIMIT / 2 Tighten REFACTOR_LIMIT; check coefficient magnitudes
WARN Stall rate increasing trend (3 consecutive 5-min windows) Review pricing rule; check for degenerate problem class
INFO p95 solve duration > 10× baseline for problem class Review pivot count; consider Devex pricing
INFO Warm-start rejection rate > 20% Verify basis file versioning; check problem mutation pattern

SLO Error Budget

SLOs are evaluated per release (full Netlib conformance run) and continuously (KKT check on every production solve). An error budget of zero is maintained for correctness SLOs: any breach blocks the release. Performance SLOs use a rolling 30-day window in production.

SLO Target Measurement Window Break Condition
Correct outcome on Netlib LP set (≥ 95 problems) 100% match within 1e-6 relative objective Per release Any objective deviation > 1e-6
KKT certificate valid on all reported optima 100% Per release + continuous Any KKT failure
No cycling on non-degenerate problems 100% (MAX_ITERS not hit) Per release Any IterationLimit on tractable instance
No cycling on Beale/Kuhn degenerate instances 100% with Bland enabled Per release Cycling detected
Solve time for Netlib medium (n ≤ 1000) < 5 s on reference hardware Per release benchmark p95 > 5 s
Zero panic on fuzz corpus (10^6 inputs) 0 panics Per release Any panic
Parse round-trip fidelity (MPS → internal → MPS) 100% objective / constraint preservation Per release Any round-trip failure
Presolve/postsolve fidelity 100% feasibility + objective equality Per release Any postsolve infeasibility
Warm-start regression: re-optimize after RHS change ≤ 2× cold-start pivot count Per release benchmark Ratio > 2×

Incident Response Procedure

Step Action Owner Artifact
1. Detect Alert fires (KKT, NaN, Numerical rate) or user report On-call Alert log; user bug report
2. Triage Retrieve problem file + solution JSON + trace_id; confirm Status, Certificate, KKT values On-call solution.json; trace log entry
3. Reproduce lp-cli solve --verbose --verify <problem.mps> on exact input; confirm failure On-call Reproduce command + output
4. Isolate Compare --dense-tableau vs revised; --no-presolve vs default; pricing rules; bisect phase log Engineer Phase log; comparison output
5. Fix Identify root crate + function; write minimal regression test; apply fix Engineer Patch; regression fixture
6. Verify Run full Netlib conformance suite; confirm SLOs pass Engineer CI run artifact
7. Postmortem Record root cause, affected class, SLO impact, fix, new test, CHANGELOG entry Team Postmortem doc

Severity escalation: if KKT failure is confirmed and cannot be reproduced with --dense-tableau (suggesting lp-revised bug) or reproduced on both paths (suggesting lp-tableau bug), escalate to P0 immediately.

Runbooks

Runbook Trigger Condition Key Steps
RB-01: Wrong optimal value KKT alert; user-reported incorrect objective Run --verify; run --dense-tableau; compare objectives; check condition_estimate log; file bug with problem + trace_id
RB-02: Unexpected Status::Infeasible Status::Infeasible on known-feasible problem Run --no-presolve; run --no-scale; increase FEASIBILITY_TOL 10×; check Farkas ray validity
RB-03: Unexpected Status::Unbounded Status::Unbounded on bounded problem Run --dense-tableau; compare primal-ray certificate; trace pivot log for bound-flip
RB-04: Status::Numerical / NaN NaN in solution; Numerical rate alert Run --scale=curtisreid; run --no-presolve; examine condition_estimate log; reduce problem if possible
RB-05: MAX_ITERS / cycling IterationLimit hit unexpectedly Run --pivot-rule=bland; check stall_count; examine pivot log for repeated basis; test with Bland forced
RB-06: OOM / allocation failure Process killed; allocation error in log Check n_rows/n_cols/n_nnz at parse; add --max-rows / --max-nnz limits; enable presolve
RB-07: Parse failure on valid file Parse Err on GLPK-accepted file Run dos2unix; check for non-ASCII; compare with GLPK parse; file parser conformance bug
RB-08: Warm-start divergence Dual simplex fails after basis load Confirm problem hash matches basis file hash; run --no-warm-start; verify basis file version
RB-09: Sensitivity mismatch Shadow prices / ranges deviate from reference Run --verify-sensitivity; trace ranging for affected row/col; compare with GLPK sensitivity output
RB-10: Presolve/postsolve infeasibility I9 violated; postsolve check fails Run --no-presolve; trace postsolve stack; identify offending reduction type; file presolve regression

Backup and State Management

Artifact Ownership Persistence Recovery
Problem file (MPS/LP) User-managed Permanent input Re-run with same file
Solution JSON Written by lp-cli Ephemeral output Re-run if lost
Basis JSON Written by --save-basis Warm-start cache Cold-start fallback if lost or stale
Metrics log Written to configured sink Ephemeral; rotate externally Metrics are advisory; loss is non-critical
Trace log Written to configured sink Ephemeral; rotate externally Correlation is advisory; loss is non-critical
  • Solver is stateless between invocations: no persistent database; no daemon; no shared memory.
  • Basis serialization: lp-cli solve --save-basis <file> writes JSON: { version, problem_hash, n_basic, basic_vars: [VarId], var_status: [VarStatus] }.
  • Basis restore: lp-cli solve --load-basis <file> validates version, dimension, and problem_hash before use; mismatch triggers cold-start fallback, not error exit.
  • Problem files: user-managed; solver never modifies input files.
  • Solution output: written atomically (write to <output>.tmp, then rename to <output>) to prevent partial files on interruption.
  • Log retention: JSON lines written to configured file or stderr; rotation via logrotate or equivalent is the operator's responsibility.
  • No database migrations: the solver has no persistent schema to migrate.
  • Problem hash: SHA-256 of the serialized (A, b, c, bounds, relations) tuple; computed once at problem construction in lp-model; stored in basis files and solution JSON for cross-artifact correlation.

Deployment Topology

Deployment Mode Description Notes
CLI / shell Direct invocation by human or script Default; no daemon required
Batch job Job scheduler (SLURM, GNU parallel) per problem Safe: stateless; no inter-process coordination
Container (Docker/OCI) Scratch or distroless image; problem files via volume mount No network ports; no privileged flags needed
Embedded library lp-simplex crate used as a Rust library Public API stable at 1.0; caller manages logging subscriber
CI / conformance runner Netlib test set run on each commit Output: solution JSON + metrics log per problem; diff vs reference

Resource sizing guidelines (per solve, revised simplex path):

Problem Class Rows Cols Typical Memory Typical Solve Time
Small ≤ 100 ≤ 500 < 1 MB < 10 ms
Medium (Netlib typical) ≤ 1000 ≤ 5000 < 50 MB < 5 s
Large ≤ 10,000 ≤ 50,000 < 2 GB minutes (estimated)
XL (pre-1.0 unsupported) > 10,000 > 50,000 operator-estimated not benchmarked
  • Single static binary (lp-cli); no runtime dependencies beyond libc.
  • No network listeners; no privileged ports; no setuid; no IPC sockets.
  • Run as unprivileged user with read access to input files and write access to output / log paths.
  • Parallelism: single-threaded solve loop; no shared mutable state across invocations; safe to run multiple instances concurrently on separate problems.
  • Container: scratch or distroless image; mount problem files via volume; configure output path to a mounted volume; stdout/stderr captured by container log driver.
  • Memory footprint: O(n_nnz) for sparse LU; O(m×n) for dense tableau path (disabled in production). Basis file is O(m) per problem.

Canary, Rolling Upgrade, Rollback

Operation Procedure
Canary Route subset of problems to new binary; compare Status and objective against old binary using lp-cli check --solution-a old.json --solution-b new.json --tol 1e-6
Rolling upgrade Replace binary in PATH; no schema migration needed (stateless); new binary reads all prior basis files within the same major version
Rollback Restore previous binary from artifact store; re-run affected problems; no state cleanup needed
Basis format change Old basis files rejected with Err(IncompatibleBasis) — automatic cold-start fallback; no crash; no silent corruption
Solution schema change Minor-version: additive only; old consumers read new output with unknown fields ignored; major-version: breaking changes documented in CHANGELOG with migration examples

Compatibility Guarantees

Overview

Compatibility is governed at four levels: (1) file-format compatibility (MPS/LP input, solution JSON, basis JSON), (2) public API stability (lp-core and lp-model types, lp-simplex and lp-sensitivity entry points), (3) CLI interface stability (subcommands, flags, exit codes), and (4) behavioral compatibility (same problem + config + pricing rule → same pivot sequence and result, per P10). Primary wire-format surfaces are Solution, Status, Certificate, Sensitivity, and Basis JSON schemas as defined in the domain model (section 8) and type registry (section 11).

Semantic Versioning Policy

Version Series Breaking Changes Guarantee
0.x.y pre-1.0 Permitted in minor versions No stability; CHANGELOG entry required
1.x.y post-1.0 MAJOR only Strict SemVer: MAJOR=breaking, MINOR=additive, PATCH=fix
  • Public API scope: lp-core types; lp-model builder; lp-parse format support; lp-cli subcommands and flags; solution JSON schema; basis JSON schema; lp-simplex and lp-sensitivity entry-point signatures.
  • Internal crate APIs (lp-lu internals, lp-tableau pivot mechanics, lp-revised basis representation) are not public API; may change in minor versions.
  • Tolerance default changes: MINOR if tightening (may cause new Numerical results on edge cases); PATCH if loosening within safe range.
  • Behavioral determinism (P10): same problem + config + pricing rule → same pivot sequence and objective in the same PATCH series; may differ across MINOR versions if algorithm improvements change the pivot sequence.

Wire and File Format Compatibility

Format Stability Class Guarantee
MPS fixed-format Stable from Phase 13 Conforms to standard MPS spec (ROWS, COLUMNS, RHS, BOUNDS, RANGES sections); extensions annotated with // extension: prefix in CHANGELOG
MPS free-row format Stable from Phase 13 Conforms to the MPS free-row extension used by GLPK and HiGHS
LP format (CPLEX-compatible) Stable from Phase 13 Supports the LP format subset accepted by GLPK; deviations documented
Solution JSON Stable at 1.0 Fields: status, objective, x, duals_y, reduced_costs, certificate, trace_id, config; additive-only in minor versions; field removal requires major bump
Basis JSON Best-effort across minor versions Fields: version, problem_hash, n_basic, basic_vars, var_status; incompatible basis triggers cold-start fallback, not error exit; breaking format changes bump version field
Metrics JSON log Stable at 1.0 Schema: {timestamp, level, crate, span, event, ...context}; new context fields additive in minor versions
CLI flag interface Stable at 1.0 Flag renames deprecated for one full major version before removal; deprecated flags log a warning

Type and Schema Evolution

Type / Schema Evolution Rule
Solution struct New fields added as Option<T> with #[serde(default)]; no field removal in minor versions
Status enum New variants (e.g., TimeLimit) additive in minor versions; consumers must handle unknown variants via _ arm
VarStatus enum Variants locked from Phase 8 onward; no removal; new variants only with major bump
Certificate enum New variants additive in minor versions; existing variant payloads do not change structure in minor versions
Sensitivity struct New range fields additive; shadow_prices ordering follows constraint order defined in LpProblem and does not change
LpProblem builder New optional builder methods additive; required constructor parameters do not change in minor versions
Bound struct Fields locked: lower: f64, upper: f64; semantics of ±inf do not change
Basis JSON schema version field bumped on incompatible structural change; loader validates and falls back gracefully
StandardForm Internal; no stability guarantee; do not expose across crate boundaries
LuFactorization Internal; no stability guarantee; do not serialize and store externally

API Stability Table

API Surface Crate Stability Notes
VarId, ConstraintId, Sense, Relation, Bound, VarStatus, Status, Certificate, Solution, Sensitivity lp-core Public / stable at 1.0 Foundation types used by all downstream consumers
LpProblem builder (add_var, add_constraint, set_objective, set_sense, set_bounds) lp-model Public / stable at 1.0 Primary problem construction surface
StandardForm (construction only) lp-model Public / stable at 1.0 Consumers may inspect n_struct, n_slack, n_artificial, var_map
parse_mps(reader) -> Result<LpProblem>, parse_lp(reader) -> Result<LpProblem> lp-parse Public / stable at 1.0 Format compatibility per table above
solve(problem: &LpProblem, config: &SolverConfig) -> Result<Solution, SolveError> lp-simplex Public / stable at 1.0 Entry point for all callers
analyze(solution: &Solution, problem: &LpProblem) -> Result<Sensitivity, SolveError> lp-sensitivity Public / stable at 1.0 Post-optimal analysis entry point
PivotRule enum (Dantzig, Bland, Devex) lp-pricing Public / stable at 1.0 New variants additive in minor versions
SolverConfig struct (tolerances, limits, flags) lp-simplex Public / stable at 1.0 New optional fields additive; defaults preserved
Dense tableau pivot internals lp-tableau Internal Testing / reference only; not re-exported from lp-simplex
LuFactorization field layout lp-lu Internal Consumed only by lp-revised
Basis struct fields lp-revised Internal Serialized form (Basis JSON) is public; in-memory layout is not
FTRAN/BTRAN function signatures lp-revised Internal May change with algorithm improvements

CLI Interface Compatibility

Subcommand Stable Flags at 1.0 Deprecation Policy
solve --input, --output, --sense, --pivot-rule, --max-iters, --feasibility-tol, --optimality-tol, --pivot-tol, --no-presolve, --no-scale, --scale, --save-basis, --load-basis, --no-warm-start, --dense-tableau, --verify, --verify-sensitivity, --max-rows, --max-cols, --max-nnz, --refactor-limit Deprecated flags warn for one major version; removed in next major; CHANGELOG entry required
sensitivity --input, --output, --basis, --verify Same policy
convert --from (mps/lp), --to (mps/lp), --input, --output Same policy
check --solution-a, --solution-b, --tol, --verbose Same policy

Exit codes: 0 = Optimal; 1 = Infeasible; 2 = Unbounded; 3 = IterationLimit; 4 = Numerical; 10 = parse/input error; 11 = configuration error; 12 = I/O error. Exit codes are stable at 1.0.

Migration Guarantees

Migration Scenario Guarantee
Pre-1.0 to 1.0 No guarantee; CHANGELOG provides migration guide
1.x to 1.(x+1) All public types, CLI flags, file formats backward-compatible; new optional features only
1.x to 2.0 Breaking changes documented in CHANGELOG with explicit before/after API examples; minimum one deprecation cycle for removed items
Presolve/postsolve across versions I9 maintained; the postsolve objective equality tolerance is POSTSOLVE_TOL (default 1e-8 relative) and does not tighten in patch versions
Standard-form conversion I10 maintained; var_map is stable — original VarId recovered from standard-form solution for all public solve results
Basis file version upgrade Incompatible → Err(IncompatibleBasis) with version info logged; automatic cold-start fallback; user notified to re-save basis
Tolerance default changes across releases Documented in CHANGELOG under [Changed]; if tightening causes regressions on user problems, treated as MINOR version, not PATCH
Netlib conformance All 1.x releases maintain 100% Netlib pass rate (≥ 95 problems) within 1e-6 relative objective; any regression is a release blocker

Deprecation Policy

Step Action Timing
1 Announce in CHANGELOG under [Deprecated] with target removal version Same release as deprecation
2 Add #[deprecated(since = "1.x.0", note = "use Y instead")] to Rust API symbols Same release
3 Emit runtime log::warn for deprecated CLI flags Same release
4 One full major version grace period Removal in next major
5 Remove in next major version bump with CHANGELOG entry under [Removed] Next major

No silent removal. No removal in patch versions. No deprecation without a documented replacement or migration path.

Rolling-Upgrade Guarantee

Upgrade Scenario Guarantee
Same major version, new minor Basis JSON forward-compatible; old basis files readable; Solution JSON additive
Major version bump Basis files rejected with Err(IncompatibleBasis) → cold-start fallback; Solution JSON may have breaking schema changes documented in CHANGELOG
Rollback to previous minor Binary replacement only; no state cleanup needed; any basis files saved by the newer version fall back gracefully
Parallel instances (different versions) Safe: stateless; no coordination; each instance reads its own input files
  • Solver is stateless: no coordination between instances needed during upgrade.
  • New binary reads all prior Basis JSON files within the same major version (additive schema only).
  • Basis files from a different major version are rejected with Err(IncompatibleBasis) and automatic cold-start fallback — no crash, no silent data corruption.
  • Solution JSON files produced by version N are fully readable by any version N+1 parser under the additive-only minor-version policy (new fields ignored by older consumers via #[serde(default)]).
  • Metrics log format: new context fields in minor versions do not break existing log parsers that ignore unknown fields.

Testing Strategy

Unit Testing

Target What How
lp-core types VarId/ConstraintId overflow, Bound normalization (lower>upper rejected), free-var detection #[test] exhaustive enum arms
lp-model builder dimension mismatch panics, maximize→minimize negation, slack/surplus/artificial counts property: roundtrip objective sign
Standard-form conversion n_struct + n_slack + n_artificial == n_cols_std; all original var bounds preserved in var_map assert on 10 hand-crafted LPs
lp-tableau pivot pivot row normalization (diagonal == 1.0); all other entries in pivot column driven to 0 within PIVOT_TOL deterministic 3×3 hand-traceable cases
Reduced-cost row maintenance after k pivots, reduced-cost row == hand-computed from scratch tolerance 1e-10
Ratio-test result leaving var index matches manual min-ratio; bound_flip == true iff step == range to opposite bound table-driven, 50 cases
lp-lu factorization B = L U within Frobenius-norm error 1e-12; forward/back triangular solves: U x = b solution correct; FTRAN/BTRAN: B x == rhs after solve random 20×20 dense square matrices
Rank-1 eta update LuFactorization after one Forrest-Tomlin update matches fresh refactorization on same matrix 500 random pivots on 50×50
lp-bounds ratio test bound-flip detection: entering var at lower, hits upper before any basic leaves → bound_flip=true, step == range 20 hand cases
Dual-feasibility check sign invariant on reduced costs during dual simplex iteration sampled every pivot
lp-sensitivity ranges cost-coefficient range at optimum: perturb c_j by (range+ε) → basis changes; perturb by (range−ε) → basis unchanged for each Netlib instance
Shadow prices dual value * RHS delta == objective change (to first order) for non-degenerate instances 20 hand LPs
lp-presolve singleton-row substitution leaves optimal value invariant; bound-tightening never widens a bound property: 500 random small LPs
lp-parse MPS reader round-trip: parse→serialize→parse produces identical LpProblem 100 Netlib .mps files
lp-parse LP-format objective sense, variable names, constraint relations, variable bounds hand-crafted edge cases

Integration Testing

Scenario Assertion
Full pipeline: parse MPS → presolve → standard form → dense simplex → postsolve Status::Optimal; objective matches reference within 1e-6
Full pipeline with revised simplex path Same status and objective as dense path
Infeasible LP (Farkas certificate) Status::Infeasible; certificate satisfies Farkas: y^T b < 0, y^T A >= 0
Unbounded LP (primal ray) Status::Unbounded; ray r: A r <= 0 (for Le constraints), c^T r < 0
Degenerate LP (multiple optimal vertices) Status::Optimal; objective matches; KKT verified
Sensitivity pipeline Sensitivity ranges non-empty; shadow prices == dual values from solution
lp-cli solve subcommand exit-code 0, JSON solution output, objective field correct
lp-cli sensitivity subcommand produces ranges table, valid JSON
lp-cli convert subcommand MPS↔LP round-trip: parsed back produces identical dimension and data
Warm-start reoptimize dual simplex after RHS perturbation reaches same optimum as cold-solve; fewer pivot iterations

Differential Testing (Reference Oracle)

  • Reference: glpk via subprocess (glpsol --mps) or highs binary; compare lp-solver output against reference for each Netlib instance.
  • Fields compared: Status, objective (absolute tolerance 1e-5 for well-conditioned, 1e-3 for ill-conditioned Netlib), primal x feasibility.
  • Script: cargo test --test differential invokes reference on 95 Netlib .mps files, compares JSON output.
  • Dense tableau vs revised simplex differential: same problem → identical Status and objective within 1e-10; same pivot sequence with Dantzig rule (P10 determinism).
  • Standard-form conversion differential: solve both original-bounds path and row-expanded path → same optimal objective.

Property-Based Testing (proptest / quickcheck)

Property Generator Shrink target
Any feasible LP (random A, b, c, bounds) → solver terminates random m in [2,30], n in [2,40], dense rational A smallest infeasible/cycling instance
KKT conditions hold at reported optimum same generator filtered to Status::Optimal smallest KKT violation
Strong duality: c^T x == b^T y at optimum same largest duality gap
Presolve fidelity: presolved objective == original objective random LP instance where postsolve maps incorrectly
Bound-flip legality: no basic variable exits its bounds after a bound-flip pivot random bounded LP smallest bound violation
Anti-cycling: Bland rule → finite termination on all generated LPs degenerate LP generator (zero-step pivots intentional) cycling trace
Forrest-Tomlin update stability: condition(B) after k updates <= threshold before refactorization triggers random basis sequences smallest k triggering drift

Fuzzing (cargo fuzz)

  • Fuzz targets: fuzz_mps_parse (arbitrary bytes → no panic, only Err), fuzz_lp_parse, fuzz_solve_small (structured LP bytes → termination within iteration limit).
  • Seed corpus: all Netlib .mps files + hand-crafted edge-case LPs (empty objective, single constraint, no constraints, duplicate variable names).
  • Invariant enforced under fuzzing: no panic!, no UB, no infinite loop (enforced by ITERATION_LIMIT).
  • Run in CI nightly with 30-minute budget per target; crash artifacts committed to fuzz/artifacts/.

Deterministic-Replay Testing

  • Record: for each Netlib instance, serialize pivot sequence (entering VarId, leaving VarId, step, iteration count) to a golden file.
  • Replay: run again with identical problem and tolerances; assert bit-identical pivot sequence (P10 determinism).
  • Golden files tracked in tests/golden/; a diff triggers CI failure.
  • Environment isolation: RUST_SEED not used; no HashMap with random state in hot paths; ordered structures only.

Soak / Stability Testing

  • 72-hour continuous-solve run over Netlib set in rotation; monitor for memory growth (/proc/self/status VmRSS delta), panic, or status drift.
  • Leak check: valgrind --leak-check=full or ASAN+LSAN on a representative 50-problem sequence.
  • Refactorization soak: force refactorization every 5 pivots on a 500-iteration LP; assert condition estimate stays bounded.

Fault Injection

Injected fault Expected behavior
Singular basis (hand-crafted pivot choice) solver detects via condition estimate, refactorizes or selects alternate pivot; no panic
NaN introduced in tableau row Status::Numerical returned; no silent wrong answer
RHS perturbation after optimal dual simplex warm-start re-establishes optimality; postsolve maps correctly
Truncated MPS file lp-parse returns Err(ParseError::UnexpectedEof); no panic
ITERATION_LIMIT exceeded Status::IterationLimit; partial solution struct returned with last feasible point if available

Conformance Validation (Netlib LP Test Set)

  • 95 instances from netlib.org/lp/data (afiro, adlittle, boeing1, boeing2, fit2d, maros-r7, …).
  • Pass criteria: Status matches reference; objective within max(1e-5, 1e-5 * |ref_obj|).
  • Classification: Optimal (≥ 85 instances), Infeasible (certificate valid), Unbounded (ray valid), NumericalDifficulty (logged, not failed).
  • CI gate: ≥ 90 of 95 must pass; any regression vs previous run blocks merge.

Performance-Regression Testing

  • Benchmark suite: cargo bench with criterion; golden baselines in benches/baseline/.
  • Gate: pivot rate must not regress > 5% relative to baseline on a fixed Netlib subset (afiro, adlittle, boeing2, sc205); CI compares JSON criterion output.
  • Tracked metrics: total pivot count (deterministic), wall time (median of 10 runs), peak RSS.

Performance Engineering

Metric Gate Summary (M1–M13)

ID Metric Target Phase Gate
M1 Dense pivot throughput (500×500 tableau, x86_64 single core) ≥ 50 000 pivots/s Phase 4
M2 Revised pivot throughput (1 000-constraint basis) ≥ 200 000 pivots/s Phase 9
M3 FTRAN/BTRAN solve latency (m=200 basis) ≤ 10 µs per solve Phase 9
M4 LU factorization latency (m=200 dense) ≤ 500 µs Phase 10
M5 Wall time — small LP (≤200 vars, parse+presolve+solve) p50 ≤ 5 ms, p99 ≤ 20 ms Phase 14
M6 Wall time — medium LP (1 000 vars) p50 ≤ 500 ms, p99 ≤ 2 s Phase 14
M7 Warm-start reoptimize time (±5% RHS perturbation) ≤ 10% of cold-start time Phase 11
M8 Memory — dense tableau (m=n=1 000) ≤ 8 × m × n bytes + 20% overhead (≤ 9.6 MB) Phase 3
M9 Memory — revised simplex basis storage ≤ 32 × nnz(B) bytes Phase 9
M10 Refactorization frequency every 100–200 pivots, or fill factor > 5×, or cond_est > 1e8 Phase 10
M11 Presolve reduction (typical structured LP) ≥ 20% variable/constraint reduction Phase 12
M12 KKT verification overhead ≤ 5% of solve time Phase 14
M13 Parse throughput (Netlib MPS) ≥ 50 MB/s Phase 13

Benchmarking Strategy

Benchmark Tool Metric Target (Phase 14)
Pivot throughput (dense tableau, afiro) criterion pivots/sec ≥ 50k pivots/sec on x86_64
Pivot throughput (revised simplex, boeing2) criterion pivots/sec ≥ 20k pivots/sec
FTRAN/BTRAN solve (m=200 basis) criterion µs/solve ≤ 10 µs per FTRAN
LU factorization (m=200 dense) criterion µs/factorize ≤ 500 µs
Full solve time (adlittle, afiro, sc205) criterion ms/solve ≤ 5 ms each
Full solve time (boeing1) criterion ms/solve ≤ 200 ms
Parse throughput (Netlib MPS) criterion MB/s ≥ 50 MB/s
Presolve reduction (fit2d) wall time % row/col reduction ≥ 30%
Memory peak (boeing2, revised path) /proc/self/status MB RSS ≤ 50 MB
  • All benchmarks run pinned to a single core (taskset 0x1), with CPU governor set to performance, on a reference machine (documented in benches/README.md).
  • criterion configuration: 100 samples, warm-up 3 s, significance level 0.05; JSON output compared against committed baseline.

Profiling Methodology

Step Tool Output
CPU hotspot perf record -g cargo bench --no-run; perf report annotated disassembly
Flamegraph cargo flamegraph --bench pivot_bench SVG in benches/flamegraphs/
Instruction-level perf annotate on lp_lu::ftran and lp_tableau::pivot identify branch mispredicts, cache misses
Branch prediction perf stat -e branch-misses,cache-misses per-benchmark
SIMD utilization llvm-mca on inner-loop LLVM IR throughput/latency model

Flamegraph Workflow

  1. Build with debug symbols preserved: CARGO_PROFILE_RELEASE_DEBUG=true cargo bench --no-run.
  2. Record: perf record --call-graph dwarf -F 999 ./target/release/deps/pivot_bench-*.
  3. Generate: perf script | stackcollapse-perf.pl | flamegraph.pl > pivot.svg.
  4. Target: lp_lu::ftran + lp_lu::btran + lp_tableau::pivot account for ≥ 80% of CPU; if alloc appears > 5%, investigate arena use.

Allocator Analysis

  • Hot paths (FTRAN/BTRAN inner loops, pivot row computation) must not allocate; use pre-allocated Vec scratch buffers in lp-revised (BasisWorkspace struct holds reusable buffers).
  • dhat (Rust dhat crate) used to count heap allocations per full solve; target: ≤ 100 allocations per solve for small LPs (m ≤ 50), zero allocations inside the pivot loop.
  • jemalloc or mimalloc as optional allocator feature for throughput experiments; default remains system allocator.
  • Allocation hotspot detection: heaptrack ./target/release/lp solve <mps> → heaptrack GUI.

Cache Analysis

Structure Access pattern Optimization
Dense tableau row sequential row-major access during pivot elimination row-major Vec<f64> layout; fit pivot row in L1 (≤ 16 KB)
Eta vectors (LU updates) sparse dot product store as (index, value) pairs; keep total eta fill ≤ L2 (256 KB) before refactorize
CSC sparse matrix A column-major traversal during pricing column-major CSC matches access pattern; no random row scatter
Basis status array sequential scan during pricing Vec<VarStatus> contiguous; fits in L1 for m ≤ 1000
  • perf stat -e L1-dcache-misses,LLC-load-misses benchmarked per phase; target: L1 miss rate < 5% in inner pivot loop.

Tracing Methodology

  • tracing crate with tracing-subscriber; spans on: presolve, phase1, phase2, dual_simplex, sensitivity.
  • RUST_LOG=lp_simplex=debug emits per-iteration reduced-cost summary and pivot choice.
  • RUST_LOG=lp_lu=trace emits condition estimate and update count before each refactorization.
  • Structured JSON log output for machine parsing in CI; human-readable ANSI output for interactive use.
  • No tracing overhead in profile.release builds: tracing macros compile out when max_level_release = "warn" set in Cargo.toml.

Syscall Analysis

  • Solver core (post-parse) makes zero syscalls; verified with strace -c on a benchmark run.
  • lp-parse I/O path: single read syscall via BufReader<File>; no mmap required.
  • CLI output: single write per result line via BufWriter<Stdout>.

Tail-Latency Analysis

  • For the CLI solve subcommand, measure P50/P95/P99 solve time over 1000 Netlib runs (automated).
  • Target: P99 ≤ 5× P50 (no severe outliers from refactorization storms).
  • Outlier diagnosis: log pivot count, refactorization count, and presolve reduction ratio per run; correlate with P99 outliers.

Memory Profiling

  • Instrument with DHAT_PROFILE=1 build feature; output dhat-heap.json after a full Netlib run.
  • Tracked allocations: LpProblem construction, StandardForm allocation, LuFactorization fill, eta vector growth.
  • Memory growth invariant: RSS must not grow across repeated solves of the same problem (no accumulation in eta vectors — refactorization resets l_eta).

Scalability Analysis

Dominant Scaling Pressures

Variable Growth impact
m (constraint count) LU factorization O(m²) dense, O(m·nnz_col) sparse; dominant cost
n (variable count) Pricing scan O(n) per iteration (Dantzig); Devex reduces via approximate norms
nnz (nonzeros in A) Sparse LU fill-in grows with nnz; refactorization frequency scales with fill
Iteration count Worst-case exponential (Klee-Minty); practical O(m) to O(m·n) with Dantzig
Eta fill accumulation Each pivot adds one eta vector; fill grows until refactorization; dominant memory pressure for large problems

Per-Component Scaling Ceilings

Component Ceiling Trigger
lp-tableau (dense) m × n ≈ 10k cells (m ≈ 100, n ≈ 100) O(m·n) memory; beyond this, revised path mandatory
lp-revised + lp-lu m ≈ 5000 with sparse LU; m > 10k requires advanced presolve Fill-in in LU; condition degradation
lp-lu eta accumulation ≤ 50–100 eta updates before refactorization (heuristic: fill ratio > 2× or update count > sqrt(m)) Memory and numerical stability
lp-presolve Linear in problem size (one pass over rows/cols) No ceiling; scales to full Netlib
lp-sensitivity O(m + n) per range computation Scales with problem; no fundamental ceiling
lp-parse MPS O(lines) I/O bound Scales to 100 MB+ files; bounded by disk I/O

Vertical Scaling

  • Single-threaded design: the simplex pivot is inherently sequential; no parallelism in the critical path.
  • BLAS-backed dense DGEMV (optional feature blas) accelerates pivot-row elimination for m > 500; controlled by feature flag to keep the no-dep default.
  • Larger L2/L3 cache directly benefits eta vector and sparse LU traversal; performance scales super-linearly with cache size up to the working-set boundary.
  • Increased clock speed: solver is compute-bound (LU factorization, FTRAN/BTRAN triangular solves) for large instances; 2× clock → approximately 2× throughput.

Horizontal Scaling

  • Not applicable to the simplex critical path: the pivot sequence is inherently serial.
  • Parallelism opportunity: solving multiple independent LPs in a batch (lp-cli solve --batch) — trivially parallel via rayon thread pool, one thread per LP.
  • Sensitivity analysis (independent range computations per variable/constraint) parallelizable via rayon; marked as a Phase 14 stretch goal.
  • Presolve passes (bound tightening, singleton detection) are read-mostly and can be parallelized per row/column block; current implementation single-threaded.

Coordination Overhead

  • No locking in the solver core: all data structures (StandardForm, Basis, LuFactorization) are owned by the single solving thread.
  • Batch solving: one Mutex<JobQueue> for work distribution; no coordination inside each solve.
  • Sensitivity output: immutable borrow of Solution; zero contention.

Metadata and Basis Scaling

Structure Size formula m=1000, n=2000
StandardForm CSC A O(nnz) ≈ 10 MB for 10% dense
Dense tableau O(m × n) 16 MB — exceeds L3; revised mandatory
Basis basic array O(m) 8 KB
Eta vectors (100 updates) O(m × 100) worst-case 800 KB
LU factors (sparse m=1000) O(m·fill_factor), fill ≈ 3–5× 4–8 MB
Solution + duals O(m + n) 24 KB

Scaling Recommendations by Problem Size

Size class m range Recommended path Expected solve time
Tiny ≤ 20 Dense tableau (lp-tableau) < 1 ms
Small 21–200 Revised simplex, dense LU < 50 ms
Medium 201–2000 Revised simplex, sparse LU, presolve < 5 s
Large 2001–10000 Revised + presolve + scaling + Devex pricing minutes; out of current scope
Netlib conformance suite varies (up to ≈3000) full pipeline all instances ≤ 60 s
  • Problems beyond m ≈ 5000 approach the ceiling for the current single-threaded sparse LU; the architecture is extensible to a supernodal factorization (documented as a post-Phase-14 stretch).
  • The ITERATION_LIMIT constant (default 10× m × n) is the hard ceiling on pivot count; beyond this, Status::IterationLimit is returned with the best known feasible point.

Mental Models & First Principles

  • MM1 — An LP optimum always sits at a vertex of the feasible polytope, so simplex walks vertices, never the interior. Predicts: the search space is the finite set of bases, not a continuum. Bug without it: trying to optimize over the interior (that's interior-point, a different method). Applied: §3, Phase 4, A1.
  • MM2 — A basis is the solution. Choosing m basic variables fixes a vertex; a pivot swaps one basic for one nonbasic, moving to an adjacent vertex. Predicts: the whole algorithm is bookkeeping over which variables are basic. Bug without it: recomputing from scratch each step instead of updating the basis. Applied: Phase 3/4, ADR-06.
  • MM3 — Reduced cost is the price signal. It gives the per-unit objective change of bringing a nonbasic variable in; optimality = no improving reduced cost. Predicts: pricing is choosing the best signal. Bug without it: a wrong entering-variable rule → stalls or wrong optimum. Applied: Phase 4, ADR-08, I4.
  • MM4 — The ratio test enforces feasibility. It picks the leaving variable that hits its bound first, bounding how far the entering variable can grow. Predicts: a tie in the ratio test = degeneracy = potential cycling. Bug without it: stepping too far → an infeasible basis. Applied: Phase 4/6, MM5.
  • MM5 — Degeneracy (a zero-length step) is what causes cycling; anti-cycling rules guarantee termination. Predicts: without Bland/lexicographic rules, simplex can loop forever on degenerate problems. Bug without it: relying on "it usually terminates" → an infinite loop on adversarial input. Applied: Phase 6, ADR-09, I7.
  • MM6 — Don't invert B; factor it. FTRAN/BTRAN solve Bx=b and Bᵀy=c via LU, and rank-1 updates (Forrest-Tomlin) keep the factorization cheap across pivots. Predicts: explicit B⁻¹ is dense and unstable; LU is sparse and stable. Bug without it: maintaining an explicit inverse → fill-in and numerical drift. Applied: Phase 9/10, ADR-06/07.
  • MM7 — Duality gives a certificate. Every optimal primal has a dual proving optimality; an infeasible/unbounded problem has a Farkas/ray certificate. Predicts: you can verify an answer independent of how you found it (KKT check). Bug without it: trusting the solver's "optimal" without a certificate → silent wrong answers. Applied: Phase 7/11, §4 (KKT verification).
  • MM8 — In floating point, never compare to exact zero — compare to a tolerance, and order the tolerances. Predicts: pivot_tol < feasibility_tol < optimality_tol is a hierarchy; mixing them causes wrong classifications. Bug without it: x == 0.0 on an f64 → spurious infeasibility or near-singular pivots. Applied: §4.2, P6, Phase 3.

Comparative Architecture

Dimension This design (lp) GLPK HiGHS COIN-OR CLP SoPlex
Primary method primal + dual revised simplex primal + dual simplex dual simplex + interior-point primal + dual simplex primal + dual simplex
Basis / factorization sparse LU, Forrest-Tomlin sparse LU sparse LU sparse LU (Clp factor) sparse LU
Pricing Dantzig + Bland (Devex optional) Dantzig / steepest-edge Devex / steepest-edge Devex / steepest-edge steepest-edge (default)
Presolve singleton / bound-tighten / redundancy basic extensive extensive moderate
MIP / IPM deferred (stretch) MIP branch-and-cut MIP + IPM MIP via Cbc exact-rational mode
Use reference/educational + library open-source solver open-source HP solver embeddable LP engine research / exact LP
  • GLPK is the canonical open-source reference simplex+MIP solver — it wins as a permissively replaceable baseline and for its GNU MathProg modeling language.
  • HiGHS chose a high-performance dual simplex plus an interior-point method and aggressive presolve — it wins for large sparse LPs where the dual simplex / IPM crossover beats a pure primal method.
  • COIN-OR CLP is the battle-tested open-source workhorse behind Cbc's MIP solving — it wins as an embeddable LP engine inside a larger branch-and-cut framework.
  • SoPlex offers an iterative-refinement exact-rational mode — it wins when bit-exact LP solutions (not just 1e-6) are required, e.g. inside exact MIP or certified computation.
  • Gurobi/CPLEX are the commercial state of the art with concurrent simplex+barrier and decades of numerical tuning — they win whenever raw solve performance and robustness on hard industrial models justify the license.

Back-of-Envelope Capacity Model

All inputs are taken from §4 (System Constraints); arithmetic is shown.

  1. Dense-tableau memory. 8 × m × n × 1.2. For m=n=1000 → 8e6 × 1.2 = 9.6 MB (matches §4).
  2. Revised-basis memory. 32 × nnz(B), nnz(B) ≈ 5–10× m. m=10K → nnz ≈ 50K–100K → 32 × 1e5 = 3.2 MB.
  3. LU fill ceiling. fill ≤ 5 × nnz(B) before refactor (§4); at nnz=1e5 → ≤ 5e5 nonzeros in the factors.
  4. Refactorization cadence. every 100–200 pivots (§4); a solve of P pivots → ≈ P/150 refactorizations.
  5. Dense pivot throughput. ≥ 50K pivots/s on 500×500 (§4); one pivot updates ≈ 500×500 = 250K f64 ops → 50K × 250K = 1.25e10 flops/s ≈ 12.5 GFLOP/s (plausible single-core f64).
  6. Revised pivot throughput. ≥ 200K/s on a 1000-constraint basis (§4); FTRAN+BTRAN each O(nnz(B)+nnz(eta)) ≈ few×1000 → 200K × ~5000 = 1e9 ops/s.
  7. Small LP wall time. ≤ 200 vars, pivots ≈ 2–3× m ≈ 400–600; at 50K/s dense ≈ ~10 ms → within p99 ≤ 20 ms (§4).
  8. Medium LP. 1000 vars, revised path ≈ 2000–3000 pivots at 200K/s ≈ 10–15 ms compute + LU refactor → fits p50 ≤ 500 ms (§4) with conditioning slack.
  9. Iteration ceiling. default 100K (§4) or 10 × m × n; for m=n=1000 → 1e7 hard ceiling — far above the ~2–3m expected, a safety net.
  10. Warm-start. ≤ 10% of cold for ±5% RHS perturbation (§4); dual simplex resumes from the old optimal basis → a few dual pivots vs a full Phase I.
  11. Netlib conformance. 95 instances, objective within 1e-6 relative (§4); up to ≈ 3000 constraints, all ≤ 60 s.
  12. Verification overhead. KKT ≤ 5%, sensitivity ≤ 10% of solve (§4); both reuse B⁻¹ already in lp-lu → no extra factorization.

Hard Questions (Self-Examination)

  1. Why does simplex search only vertices, never the interior (MM1, §3), and what does that imply about the feasible-set structure (Phase 4)?
  2. Why is "a basis" equivalent to "a vertex," and how does one pivot move between adjacent vertices (MM2, Phase 3/4)?
  3. Derive the entering-variable rule from reduced costs and state the optimality condition (MM3, Phase 4, I4).
  4. Why does the ratio test keep the new basis feasible (MM4, Phase 4), and what does a tie signify (MM5)?
  5. Why can simplex cycle on degenerate problems, and how do Bland's rule and lexicographic perturbation each prevent it (MM5, Phase 6, ADR-09)?
  6. Why factor B with LU rather than maintain B⁻¹ explicitly (MM6, ADR-06, Phase 9/10), and what does Forrest-Tomlin preserve?
  7. How does duality let you verify an optimum independently of how it was found (MM7, Phase 7/11, §4 KKT)?
  8. Why is comparing an f64 to exact 0.0 forbidden, and why must pivot_tol < feasibility_tol < optimality_tol (MM8, §4.2, P6)?
  9. Why is two-phase preferred over Big-M (ADR-10, Phase 5), and what numerical problem does Big-M introduce?
  10. Why does native bounded-variable simplex keep the basis smaller than adding explicit bound rows (ADR-11, Phase 8, §4 memory)?
  11. When does the dual simplex beat re-solving from scratch (ADR-12, Phase 11), and why is it essential for a branch-and-bound parent (Capacity Model warm-start)?
  12. Why refactorize every 100–200 pivots or when fill > 5× or cond > 1e8 (§4, Phase 10) rather than never or every pivot?
  13. Compute dense-tableau memory for m=n=1000 and revised-basis memory for m=10K, and explain why the revised path is mandatory above 1000 (Capacity Model, §4).
  14. Why keep the dense tableau as a reference oracle even though it's slower (ADR-05, Phase 3, §4 differential test) — what bug class does it catch?
  15. Why does presolve achieve ≥ 20% reduction (ADR-13, Phase 12, §4), and how does it interact with certificate validity (MM7)?
  16. How do primal-ray and dual-ray certificates classify unboundedness and infeasibility (Phase 7, §4 certificate verification, MM7)?
  17. Why is geometric-mean scaling O(nnz), and how does it lower the condition number without dense operations (ADR-14, Phase 12, Capacity Model)?
  18. Why is the solver core single-threaded with parallelism only at the batch-solve level (ADR-20, §4), and what about the inner loop resists parallelization?
  19. When would an interior-point method beat simplex (Comparative HiGHS, Deferred Register), and which problem structure flips the choice?
  20. When is SoPlex's exact-rational mode (Comparative) required over this engine's 1e-6 tolerance, and what §4 assumption makes floating point acceptable for v1?

Alternative Architectures (Roads Not Taken)

A1 — Interior-point / barrier method.

start interior → follow central path (Newton steps) → converge to optimum

Wins for very large, sparse LPs where the polynomial-time barrier method beats simplex's exponential worst case and warm-start isn't needed. Embodied by HiGHS IPM / Mosek. Rejected for v1 because simplex yields an exact vertex (basis) and trivial warm-start for branch-and-bound; IPM is a deferred stretch goal (Register).

A2 — Dense-tableau simplex only (no revised/LU).

[ full m×n tableau ] ── pivot in place ── repeat

Wins for tiny, dense problems (textbook size) where clarity beats scale and the tableau fits in cache. Embodied by classroom Bland's-rule implementations. Rejected as the core because it is O(m·n) memory and unusable above ~1000 vars (§4); kept only as the reference oracle (ADR-05).

A3 — Big-M single-phase method.

minimize  cᵀx + M·Σ(artificials)   in one solve

Wins when a single LP solve with a known-safe M is simpler than running two phases (quick prototypes). Embodied by textbook Big-M presentations. Rejected because a large M corrupts the numerics (MM8, ADR-10); two-phase is numerically clean.

A4 — Exact rational-arithmetic simplex.

pivots over exact rationals (no f64) → bit-exact optimum + certificate

Wins when bit-exact optimality is required (certified MIP, formal verification). Embodied by SoPlex iterative-refinement / exact-LP. Rejected for v1 because rational arithmetic is orders of magnitude slower and the 1e-6 tolerance (§4) suffices; certificate verification (MM7) covers the cases where exactness matters.

Deferred Features Register

Feature Anchor Building Phase Un-defer Trigger
Interior-point method (barrier) Deferred Features Register post-v1 stretch goal problems are large/sparse enough that IPM beats simplex, or warm-start is not needed
MIP branch-and-bound Deferred Features Register post-v1 stretch goal integer-constrained problems must be solved, not just LP relaxations

Glossary

Alphabetical. Every term maps to a type, invariant, module, or phase in this solver. Definitions are implementation-relevant and operationally precise.

Artificial variable

  • Added in Phase I (Phase 5) to rows with Ge or Eq relations to obtain an initial basis.
  • Counted in StandardForm.n_artificial; must be driven to zero for primal feasibility.
  • Removed before Phase II; if any remain positive at Phase I optimum: Status::Infeasible.

Bartels-Golub update

  • Rank-1 update to basis LU after a pivot; modifies U via row operations; O(m²) worst case.
  • Stored as an eta vector appended to LuFactorization.l_eta; increments n_updates.
  • Reference: Bartels & Golub, CACM 1969; implemented in lp-lu/src/update.rs (Phase 10).

Basic feasible solution (BFS)

  • A vertex of the feasible polytope corresponding to an invertible m × m basis submatrix B.
  • Stored as Basis + primal solution x: Vec<f64>; degenerate when any basic variable equals a bound (zero step in ratio test).
  • Simplex (lp-simplex) traverses adjacent BFSs; each pivot moves along one edge of the polytope.

Basic variable

  • A variable currently in the basis (VarStatus::Basic); one per constraint row.
  • Value determined by the triangular solve B x_B = b - N x_N; exposed by Basis::basic_solution.
  • Leaving the basis means it becomes NonbasicLower or NonbasicUpper.

Basis

  • The m × m invertible submatrix of A defined by Basis.basic: Vec<VarId>.
  • Stored with factorization: Basis { basic, status, factor: LuFactorization }.
  • Must satisfy I1 (nonsingularity) at all times; violation triggers refactorization or pivot rejection.

Big-M method

  • Alternative to two-phase: penalize artificials by M >> 0 in the objective.
  • Documented in lp-simplex/src/phase1.rs; not the primary path (two-phase is preferred).
  • Numerical issues arise when M is large relative to problem coefficients; use two-phase instead.

Bland's rule

  • Anti-cycling: enter the smallest-index variable with r_j < -OPTIMALITY_TOL; break ties on leaving by smallest index.
  • Guarantees finite termination (I7) by preventing basis repetition.
  • Enum PivotRule::Bland in lp-pricing; activated by stall detection in lp-simplex driver (Phase 6).

Bound flip

  • Bounded-variable simplex (Phase 8, lp-bounds): entering nonbasic variable hits its opposite bound before any basic variable hits a bound.
  • RatioTestResult { leaving: None, step: delta, bound_flip: true }; objective updates, basis unchanged.
  • Handled by apply_bound_flip; flips VarStatus from NonbasicLower to NonbasicUpper or vice versa.

BTRAN (backward transformation)

  • Solves B^T y = c_B via reverse triangular solves on the LU factors.
  • Produces dual variables / row prices y = c_B B^{-1} used to compute reduced costs.
  • Implemented in lp-lu/src/btran.rs; the pricing side of the revised simplex (Phase 9).

Certificate

  • Proof accompanying every terminal Status:
    • Certificate::KktOptimal — primal feasibility + dual feasibility + complementary slackness.
    • Certificate::PrimalRay(Vec<f64>) — improving direction proving Status::Unbounded.
    • Certificate::DualRay(Vec<f64>) — Farkas witness proving Status::Infeasible.
  • Required by P5; verified in kkt_verifier.rs and integration tests (Phase 14).

Complementary slackness

  • KKT condition: for each variable j, r_j * (x_j - bound_j) = 0 within OPTIMALITY_TOL.
  • Equivalent formulation: nonbasic variables at their active bound, basic variables with zero reduced cost.
  • Verified by kkt_verifier.rs (Phase 14) for every Status::Optimal result.

Constraint

  • A single row a_i . x {<=, =, >=} b_i; identified by ConstraintId(u32).
  • Relation enum encodes the comparison: Le, Eq, Ge.
  • Stored in LpProblem.matrix (CSC column-major) and LpProblem.rhs: Vec<f64>.

CSC (compressed sparse column)

  • Storage format for matrix A in SparseMatrix: values: Vec<f64>, row_indices: Vec<usize>, col_ptrs: Vec<usize>.
  • Column j spans row_indices[col_ptrs[j]..col_ptrs[j+1]]; enables O(nnz_j) column access for FTRAN.
  • Used throughout; avoids dense m × n allocation for large sparse problems.

Curtis-Reid scaling

  • Iterative row/column scaling in lp-presolve: minimizes 2-norm of log-scaled entries to convergence.
  • Superior to geometric scaling for highly unbalanced matrices; applied before LU factorization.
  • Scale vectors r: Vec<f64> (rows), c: Vec<f64> (columns) stored for postsolve unscaling.

Dantzig rule

  • Standard pricing: enter argmin_{j: r_j < -OPTIMALITY_TOL} r_j (most negative reduced cost).
  • Greedy; reduces objective most per pivot step but may cycle on degenerate problems.
  • Enum PivotRule::Dantzig; fastest per-pivot selection; default before stall detection.

Degeneracy

  • BFS where one or more basic variables equal a bound; produces RatioTestResult.step == 0.
  • Zero-step pivots make no primal progress but may still change basis; repeated degenerate pivots can cycle.
  • Handled by Phase 6 anti-cycling (Bland / perturbation); degenerate pivot count tracked in driver.

Devex / steepest-edge pricing

  • Approximate steepest-edge: weight entering selection by estimated w_j ≈ ||B^{-1} a_j||².
  • Score: r_j² / w_j; weights initialized to 1.0, updated per pivot, reset on refactorization.
  • Enum PivotRule::Devex in lp-pricing; typically reduces pivot count vs Dantzig on large problems.

Dual feasibility

  • Reduced costs have correct signs: r_j >= 0 for NonbasicLower, r_j <= 0 for NonbasicUpper.
  • Maintained throughout lp-dual (I3); checked within OPTIMALITY_TOL.
  • Necessary condition for KKT optimality (I4); verified before accepting warm-start basis.

Dual ray

  • Direction d with A^T d >= 0 (standard-form minimization) and b . d < 0.
  • Proves infeasibility via Farkas' lemma; emitted as Certificate::DualRay(Vec<f64>).
  • Validity checked in tests/integration/infeasible.rs (Phase 14).

Dual simplex

  • Maintains dual feasibility; restores primal feasibility by pivoting.
  • Leaving variable: most infeasible basic (x_B_r < lb_r or x_B_r > ub_r).
  • Entering variable: dual ratio test ensuring dual feasibility is preserved.
  • Implemented in lp-dual (Phase 11); ideal for warm-start reoptimization after bound/RHS changes (P8).

Duality

  • Every LP min c.x s.t. A x >= b has dual max b.y s.t. A^T y <= c, y >= 0.
  • Shadow prices are dual optimal values; feasible primal+dual with equal objectives proves optimality.
  • Strong duality (I5): at optimum, c.x* = b.y* within OPTIMALITY_TOL.

Eta vector

  • Sparse encoding of a single pivot update to L or U in the LU factorization.
  • LuFactorization.l_eta: Vec<EtaVector>; each entry records the column index and nonzero values.
  • Accumulation degrades FTRAN/BTRAN performance; triggers refactorization when n_updates > REFACTOR_LIMIT.

Farkas' lemma

  • Theorem: LP is infeasible iff b . d < 0 for some d with A^T d >= 0.
  • Constructive: dual simplex's dual ray satisfies this; forms Certificate::DualRay.
  • Reference: Bertsimas & Tsitsiklis §3.5; also Chvatal Chapter 7.

Feasibility tolerance

  • Named constant FEASIBILITY_TOL (~1e-7) in lp-core/src/tolerance.rs.
  • Threshold for |a_i . x - b_i| <= FEASIBILITY_TOL in primal-feasibility checks.
  • Used consistently in ratio test, postsolve verification, Phase I residual check (P6).

Feasible region

  • Polytope { x : A x = b, l <= x <= u } in standard form; convex.
  • Vertices are BFSs; edges connect adjacent vertices (bases differing by one column).
  • Empty feasible region → Status::Infeasible; unbounded recession direction → Status::Unbounded.

Fill-in

  • New nonzeros created in L or U during LU factorization or eta-vector updates.
  • Monitored by REFACTOR_FILL_RATIO; excessive fill triggers refactorization (I1, P2).
  • Markowitz ordering in lp-lu/src/factor.rs minimizes fill during initial factorization.

Forrest-Tomlin update

  • Rank-1 LU update preserving L factor, modifying only U; more stable than Bartels-Golub.
  • Primary update in lp-lu/src/update.rs (Phase 10).
  • Reference: Forrest & Tomlin, Mathematical Programming, 1972.

FTRAN (forward transformation)

  • Solves B d = a_j for the entering column's representation in the current basis.
  • Used in ratio test (compute step sizes) and basis update (eta vector construction).
  • Implemented in lp-lu/src/ftran.rs via forward triangular solve with permutations.

Geometric scaling

  • Scale row i by sqrt(max_j |a_ij| / min_j |a_ij|), column j similarly; O(nnz).
  • Good first-pass before Curtis-Reid; reduces extreme value ranges in A.
  • Applied in lp-presolve/src/scale.rs; vectors stored for postsolve unscaling.

Infeasible

  • Status::Infeasible: no point satisfies all constraints.
  • Proven by Phase I (lp-simplex/src/phase1.rs): sum-of-artificials objective > 0 at optimum.
  • Accompanied by Certificate::DualRay; I8 requires the ray satisfies Farkas' system.

KKT conditions

  • Necessary and sufficient for LP optimality: (1) primal feasibility, (2) dual feasibility, (3) complementary slackness.
  • Verified to emit Certificate::KktOptimal; all three checked within named tolerances (P1, P6).
  • Verified in kkt_verifier.rs for every Status::Optimal result in the conformance suite.

LpProblem

  • Root problem type: { sense: Sense, objective: Vec<f64>, matrix: SparseMatrix, rhs: Vec<f64>, relations: Vec<Relation>, bounds: Vec<Bound> }.
  • Built via ProblemBuilder; validated on build() (dimension agreement, bound sanity).
  • Passed to lp-presolve then converted to StandardForm by lp-model.

LU factorization

  • Decomposition P A Q = L U with row/column permutations for numerical stability.
  • Stored as LuFactorization { l_eta: Vec<EtaVector>, u: SparseTriangular, row_perm, col_perm, n_updates }.
  • Enables O(m²) FTRAN/BTRAN; updated by Forrest-Tomlin; refactorized when condition degrades (P2, I1).

Markowitz ordering

  • Pivot-selection heuristic during sparse LU factorization minimizing fill-in.
  • Score for entry (i, j): (nnz(row i) - 1) * (nnz(col j) - 1); choose minimum-score pivot.
  • Implemented in lp-lu/src/factor.rs; reduces eta-vector density and FTRAN/BTRAN cost.

MPS format

  • Standard LP serialization (fixed-column and free variants).
  • Sections: NAME, ROWS, COLUMNS, RHS, BOUNDS (UP/LO/FX/FR/MI), RANGES, OBJSENSE.
  • Parsed by lp-parse/src/mps.rs; Netlib test set uses this format.

Netlib LP test set

  • Public benchmark: ~80 LP instances at netlib.org/lp; sizes from tens to thousands of rows.
  • Reference optima in tests/conformance/netlib_expected.csv; objective must match within 1e-6.
  • Primary conformance criterion for Phase 14; includes infeasible and unbounded cases.

Nonbasic variable

  • Variable not in the basis; fixed at lb (NonbasicLower) or ub (NonbasicUpper).
  • Contributes c_j * x_j to the objective; reduced cost r_j governs whether it should enter.
  • Free nonbasic (VarStatus::Free): only in revised path; price r_j = 0 to enter if nonzero gradient.

Objective function

  • Linear form c . x; stored in LpProblem.objective: Vec<f64>.
  • Maximization normalized to minimization in StandardForm by negating c (Phase 2).
  • Optimal value reported in Solution.objective (in original sense after postsolve).

Optimality tolerance

  • Named constant OPTIMALITY_TOL (~1e-9) in lp-core/src/tolerance.rs.
  • Threshold for r_j >= -OPTIMALITY_TOL in Phase II termination check (I4).
  • Also used in KKT verification and dual feasibility checks; consistent across all modules (P6).

Partial pivoting

  • During LU factorization: swap rows to ensure |pivot| >= PIVOT_TOL * max(|col|).
  • Stabilizes U entries against catastrophic cancellation; row permutation stored in row_perm.
  • Implemented in lp-lu/src/factor.rs; prevents Status::Numerical from ill-conditioned pivots.

Perturbation method

  • Anti-cycling alternative to Bland: add small epsilon to RHS entries to remove degeneracy.
  • All BFSs become non-degenerate; no zero-step pivots possible; removed in postsolve.
  • Documented in lp-simplex/src/phase2.rs; reference: Bertsimas & Tsitsiklis §3.5.

Pivot

  • Exchange of one basic variable (leaving) and one nonbasic variable (entering).
  • Dense: Gaussian elimination on pivot row/column in lp-tableau; O(m·n).
  • Revised: FTRAN for entering column + LU update; O(m²) amortized; production path (P4).

Pivot tolerance

  • Named constant PIVOT_TOL in lp-core/src/tolerance.rs.
  • Minimum |d_r| before accepting the ratio-test leaving variable; prevents near-singular updates.
  • If no eligible leaving variable has |d_r| >= PIVOT_TOL: refactorize or skip this entering variable.

Postsolve

  • Reverse application of the presolve operation stack in lp-presolve/src/postsolve.rs.
  • Maps reduced Solution.x back to original variable space; unscales using stored scale vectors.
  • Must produce a feasible original solution with the same objective; verified by I9.

Primal feasibility

  • All constraints satisfied: A x = b (standard form) and l <= x <= u within FEASIBILITY_TOL.
  • Maintained throughout Phase II (I2); checked in postsolve and check subcommand.
  • kkt_verifier.rs verifies this as condition (1) of KKT.

Primal ray

  • Direction d with c . d < 0 (minimization) and A d = 0, d >= 0 (standard form).
  • Proves Status::Unbounded; emitted as Certificate::PrimalRay(Vec<f64>).
  • Constructed when ratio test finds no eligible leaving variable; validated in integration tests.

Primal simplex

  • Maintains primal feasibility; improves objective by entering variables with negative reduced cost.
  • Dense path: lp-tableau (Phases 3–7); revised path: lp-revised + lp-lu (Phases 9–10).
  • Two-phase structure: Phase I achieves feasibility, Phase II achieves optimality.

Ratio test

  • Determines the leaving variable: theta_i = (x_B_i - lb_i) / d_i for d_i > 0.
  • Take min theta_i; in bounded-variable case also consider theta = (ub_j - lb_j) for entering variable.
  • Result RatioTestResult { leaving: Option<usize>, step: f64, bound_flip: bool }; I6 guarantees correctness.

Reduced cost

  • r_j = c_j - y . a_j where y = c_B B^{-1} (dual prices from BTRAN).
  • Negative r_j (minimization): variable j can improve the objective; candidate for entering.
  • Zero for basic variables (complementary slackness); stored in Solution.reduced_costs: Vec<f64>.

Refactorization

  • Full recomputation of basis LU from scratch in lp-lu/src/refactor.rs.
  • Triggered when n_updates > REFACTOR_LIMIT or fill ratio > REFACTOR_FILL_RATIO.
  • Resets n_updates = 0; restores numerical accuracy; maintains I1 and P2.

Revised simplex

  • Avoids materializing the full m × n tableau; uses FTRAN/BTRAN on LU for all operations.
  • Memory: O(nnz(A) + m²) vs O(m·n) for dense; critical for large sparse problems.
  • Implemented in lp-revised (Phase 9); the production path per P4.

Scaling

  • Row/column diagonal rescaling of A, b, c to reduce condition number.
  • Methods: geometric (one pass) or Curtis-Reid (iterative); implemented in lp-presolve/src/scale.rs.
  • Applied before factorization; reversed in postsolve; improves pivot quality and reduces fill.

Sensitivity analysis

  • Post-optimal ranging: how far can c_j (cost) or b_i (RHS) change while keeping current basis optimal?
  • Cost range: [c_j - delta_lo, c_j + delta_hi] where basis optimality (dual feasibility) is maintained.
  • RHS range: [b_i - mu_lo, b_i + mu_hi] where basis primal feasibility is maintained.
  • Shadow prices (duals y_i) quantify objective change per unit RHS change; stored in Sensitivity.

Shadow price

  • Dual variable y_i for constraint i; y_i = dz*/db_i at optimum.
  • Stored in Solution.duals_y: Vec<f64> and Sensitivity.shadow_prices: Vec<f64>.
  • Zero when constraint is not tight (positive slack); sign depends on constraint direction (Le vs Ge).

Singleton row

  • Constraint row with exactly one nonzero entry; substituted directly in presolve.
  • Eliminates a variable and a constraint; operation saved in presolve stack for postsolve recovery.
  • Handled in lp-presolve/src/presolve.rs (Phase 12).

Slack variable

  • Added to Le constraint: a_i . x + s_i = b_i, s_i >= 0.
  • VarStatus::NonbasicLower (zero) when constraint is tight; counted in StandardForm.n_slack.
  • Provides an initial basic variable for Phase I; positive slack indicates the constraint is not binding.

Solution

  • Terminal result type: { status: Status, objective: f64, x: Vec<f64>, duals_y: Vec<f64>, reduced_costs: Vec<f64> }.
  • Accompanied by Certificate; valid iff status = Optimal satisfies I2, I3, I4, I5.
  • Written to output by lp-parse/src/writer.rs; verified by lp-cli check subcommand.

StandardForm

  • Canonical form min c x, A x = b, x >= 0 produced by lp-model/src/standard_form.rs.
  • Fields: { A_std, b, c, n_struct, n_slack, n_artificial, var_map }.
  • Equivalent to the original LP by I10; var_map enables postsolve recovery of original variables.

Strong duality

  • At optimum: c.x* = b.y* within OPTIMALITY_TOL (I5).
  • Primal and dual objectives must agree; checked as part of KKT verification.
  • Failure indicates numerical error in dual computation or postsolve unscaling.

Surplus variable

  • Added to Ge constraint: a_i . x - s_i = b_i, s_i >= 0.
  • Paired with an artificial variable for Phase I basis; counted in StandardForm.n_slack.
  • Positive surplus indicates the constraint is satisfied strictly (not binding).

Two-phase method

  • Phase I (Phase 5): minimize sum of artificials; drives to feasible BFS or proves infeasibility.
  • Phase II (Phase 4): optimize original objective from the feasible basis obtained by Phase I.
  • Primary feasibility strategy; Big-M is documented as an alternative in phase1.rs.

Unbounded

  • Status::Unbounded: objective can be decreased without bound (minimization).
  • Detected in Phase II when ratio test finds no eligible leaving variable (leaving = None).
  • Accompanied by Certificate::PrimalRay; I8 requires the ray to be a valid unbounded direction.

VarId / ConstraintId

  • Newtype wrappers VarId(u32) and ConstraintId(u32) providing type-safe dense indices.
  • Index into LpProblem.objective (VarId) and LpProblem.rhs (ConstraintId).
  • Prevent accidental mixing of row and column indices at compile time.

VarStatus

  • Enum encoding a variable's current role: Basic, NonbasicLower, NonbasicUpper, Free.
  • Stored in Basis.status: Vec<VarStatus>; governs reduced-cost sign check for I4.
  • Updated on every pivot and bound flip; must remain consistent with Basis.basic membership.

Warm start

  • Supply a saved Basis to lp-dual::warm_start to reoptimize without re-running Phase I + II.
  • Basis must be dual-feasible; warm.rs checks this before entering the dual loop (P8).
  • Enables fast reoptimization after small RHS or bound changes; tested in reopt.rs.

Appendix A — File Inventory

All leaf files consistent with the section-10 repository tree. Paths are workspace-relative.

Path Purpose

FINAL_BLUEPRINT.md This specification document.
final_ranking.md Workspace promotion ledger; project entry above Part II after blueprint written.

crates/lp-tableau/src/lib.rs Re-exports Tableau, pivot function.

crates/lp-tableau/src/pivot.rs Pivot: normalize pivot row by T[r][col]; Gaussian elimination on all other rows and objective row; O(m·n).
crates/lp-tableau/src/reduced_cost.rs Extract reduced costs from objective row; check optimality: all r_j >= -OPTIMALITY_TOL.
crates/lp-tableau/tests/pivot_tests.rs 2×2 and 3×3 pivot correctness; identity-basis initialization; regression vs known optimal values.
